\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]^{2}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{E}_{0}[\psi{}]=\sup_{v\in[1,\infty)}\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\sup_{u\in[1,\infty)}\int_{1}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\\

For
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}
, we define the following norms:

(2.29)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{}}\coloneqq{}\sum_{\beta{}\leq\alpha{}}\mathcal{E}[\Gamma{}^{\beta{}}\varphi{}],\qquad\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},p}\coloneqq{}\sum_{\beta{}\leq\alpha{}}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\beta{}}\varphi{}].

2.7.3.
Pointwise norm

For
p\geq 0
, define a pointwise norm
\mathcal{P}_{p}[\psi{}]
:

(2.30)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{p}[\psi{}]

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\lVert r\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert rU\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert r^{2}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert r^{1/2}\tau{}^{p/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\mathbf{1}_{p\geq 1+\eta{}_{0}}\lVert r\tau{}^{p/2-1/2-\eta{}_{0}/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}},

where we write
L^{\infty}=L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}})
.

2.4
.

By interpolation (splitting into regions
r\leq\tau{}
and
r\geq\tau{}
),

\mathcal{P}_{p}[\psi{}]
for
p>0
controls the quantity
\lVert r^{\rho{}}\tau^{p/2+1/2-\rho{}-\eta_{0}/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
for
\rho{}\in{}[1/2,1]
. In particular,

\mathcal{P}_{2-s}[\psi{}]^{2}
controls
\lVert r^{2-s-\eta{}_{0}}\tau{}\psi{}^{2}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
(take
\rho{}=1-s/2-\eta_{0}/2
).

For
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}
, we define the following norm:

(2.31)

\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},p}\coloneqq{}\sum_{\beta{}\leq\alpha{}}\mathcal{P}_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\beta{}}\varphi{}].

2.7.4.
Schematic geometric quantities

We first define a quantity
G
in terms of
which we can compare the double null coordinates
u
and
v
with the radius

r
and the time
\tau{}
(see
4.25
).

{lemma}
[Comparison of coordinates]
For each characteristic data set and each
\epsilon{}\in(0,1)
, there is
C_{\epsilon}{}>0

depending on
\epsilon{}
,
\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta_{0}}
,
\eta_{0}
and
R_{\bullet}
such that

(2.32)

C^{-1}_{\epsilon}{}\leq G_{\epsilon}{}\leq C_{\epsilon}{},

where
G_{\epsilon}{}
is the following quantity computed from the future development

\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
of the characteristic data:

(2.33)

G_{\epsilon}{}\coloneqq{}\sup_{\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}}[r/v+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u/v+(\mathbf{1}_{r\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}}+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v})v/u].

The proof will be given in
section

4
(see
4.17
).

With the notation introduced in
sections

2.6.4
and
2.6.5
in
mind, we define the following schematic geometric quantities:

(2.34)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\text{min}},R_{0},\eta{}_{0}}(1,\kappa{},\kappa{}^{-1},r^{-1}\varpi{},\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\{(-\gamma{}),(-\gamma{})^{-1},(1-\mu{})^{-1}\}),

(2.35)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u,v\}\{(1-\kappa{}),(1-\kappa{}^{-1}),\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\{(1-(-\gamma{})),(1-(-\gamma{})^{-1})\}\}),

(2.36)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{G_{\eta{}_{0}},G_{\eta{}_{0}}^{-1},R_{\bullet}}(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}(v-u-r)),

(2.37)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{b}_{0}\{1,\varpi{}\},

(2.38)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{0}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{g}_{0}.

For
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, define

(2.39)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{B}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{},\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\kappa{},\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{U}>0}r\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\kappa{},\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}(-\gamma{})),

(2.40)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{b}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u,v\}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\kappa{},r\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}+U}\kappa{},\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}ru,v\}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}(-\gamma{})),

(2.41)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\{1,\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{G}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}},\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{},r\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}(-\gamma{})\},

(2.42)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}\{1,\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{g}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}ru,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{U}>0}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\kappa{}\}.

Define

(2.43)

\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}{}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}}).

The quantities
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}{}
appear in the computation of the commutators

[U,S]
and
[V,S]
(see
2.60
). Observe that
\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
. Finally, define

(2.44)

\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{s}(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}).

The quantities
\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s}
appear in pointwise bounds for
\Box{}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}
.

2.5
Boundedness and growth of schematic geometric quantities.

We will show in our proof that the quantities
\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
and

\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}
are bounded, but we only show that the

\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
terms grow at a slow
rate (namely
r^{C_{\alpha}{}\eta_{0}}
for some sequence of constants

C_{\alpha}{}
that is increasing in
\alpha{}
). Observe that the quantities

\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}
are stronger than the quantities

\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
, and
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
is stronger than

\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}
.

2.6
Relation of schematic geometric quantities to the energy.

The weak geometric quantities
\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}
must be
controlled before the order-
\alpha{}
energy
\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}
is,
because these quantities appear in the commutator

\Box{}\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}
that arises as an error term in the energy
estimate. Once
\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}
is controlled, we control the strong
geometric quantities
\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}
and
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
.

2.7
Algebra of schematic geometric quantities.

We have arranged that
\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}

and
\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\equiv_{s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
. Moreover, the quantities become stronger as

\alpha{}
increases, in the sense that, for example,
b_{\beta}{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
whenever
\beta{}\leq\alpha{}
. Finally, the
quantities are compatible with differentiation by the vector fields

\Gamma{}
, in the sense that
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}
(and similarly for the other quantities).

2.8.
Vector field commutator calculations

In this section we collect useful calculations related to our vector field
commutators. We relegate the proofs to
appendix

A
.

2.8.1.
Comparing vector fields in different coordinate systems

{lemma}

[Coordinate change associated to
U
]

(2.45)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}U=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}\partial{}_{u}

{lemma}

[Coordinate change associated to
V
]
We have

(2.46)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\partial{}_{v}+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}U],

(2.47)

\displaystyle V-\overline{\partial}_{r}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\overline{\partial}_{r}+U],

(2.48)

\displaystyle\partial{}_{v}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[V+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}U].

{lemma}

[Coordinate change associated to
S
]
We have

(2.49)

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\{\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}U+\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\}V]

(2.50)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}(S-r\overline{\partial}_{r})

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\{1,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}v\}\overline{\partial}_{u}.

2.8.2.
Rearranging commutator vector fields

{lemma}

[Commuting with
\overline{\partial}_{r}
]
We have

(2.51)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}[\overline{\partial}_{r},U]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{0}[\overline{\partial}_{r}+U],

(2.52)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}[\overline{\partial}_{r},V]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{0}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{V}[\overline{\partial}_{r}+U],

(2.53)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}[\overline{\partial}_{r},S]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\overline{\partial}_{r}+\mathbf{1}_{R_{0}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{V}[\overline{\partial}_{r}+U].

{lemma}

[Commuting with one commutator vector field]
We have

(2.54)

\displaystyle[U,V]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{0}D,

(2.55)

\displaystyle[U,S]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}U+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{U}D,

(2.56)

\displaystyle[V,S]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}V+\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{V}D.

{lemma}

[Commuting with a product of commutator vector fields]
Let
\alpha{}\geq 0
and let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. We have

(2.57)

\displaystyle[U,L]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}[U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}],

(2.58)

\displaystyle[V,L]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}r^{-2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U},

(2.59)

\displaystyle[D,L]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}.

Moreover, we have

(2.60)

[V^{2},L]=_{\mathrm{s}}V^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}+V}r^{-2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U+V}.

{lemma}

[Bringing
U
to the front]
Fix
\alpha{}\geq 1
such that
\alpha_{U}>0
. Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. Then there is
L^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\alpha{}-U}
such that

(2.61)

\begin{split}L-UL^{\prime}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-U-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S})[U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-U-V}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-S}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}[U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-S}]\\
\end{split}

{lemma}

[Rearrangement formula]
Let
\alpha{}\geq 0
, and let
L,L^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. We have

(2.62)

L-L^{\prime}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{\leq\alpha{}-S}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}.

2.8.3.
Commuting with the wave operator

{lemma}

[Wave equations for commutator vector fields]
We have

(2.63)

UV,VU=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\Box{}+r^{-1}D+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}U^{2}]

and

(2.64)

\partial{}_{v}U=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\Box{}+r^{-1}D].

{lemma}

[Commutators of vector field with
\Box
]
The following commutation formulas hold for
R_{\bullet}
large enough depending on

\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}
:

(2.65)

\displaystyle[\Box{},U]+f_{U}U^{2}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{U}[\Box{}+r^{-2}V+r^{-2}U],

(2.66)

\displaystyle[\Box{},V]+f_{V}V^{2}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{V}[\Box{}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{V}V+r^{-2}U+r^{-2}U^{2}],

(2.67)

\displaystyle[\Box{},S]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{S}[\Box{}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{S}V^{2}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{S}V+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}r^{-2}UU^{\leq 1}].

where

(2.68)

0\leq f_{U}=\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{0}\qquad f_{V}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}\mathfrak{g}_{0}

{lemma}

[Main formula for commutation with
\Box
]
Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. For
R_{\bullet}
large enough depending on
\mathcal{G}_{0}^{\circ}
, we
have

(2.69)

\begin{split}[\Box{},L]+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}UL+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VL=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\Box{}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}V^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}].\end{split}

for
f_{U}
and
f_{V}
as in
2.68
.

In the following pointwise estimate
2.71
, we make crucial use of the good sign
f_{U}\geq 0
(see
2.68
).

{corollary}

Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 0
and let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. We have

(2.70)

\begin{split}r^{2-s}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert&\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})[\alpha{}_{V}\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert+\alpha{}_{U}\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})[\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert].\end{split}

Moreover, we have

(2.71)

r^{2-s}UL\varphi{}\Box{}L\varphi{}\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert)(\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert),

and

(2.72)

\begin{split}r^{2-s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert&\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert(\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert)+\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{wave-pointwise-2}\text{{)}}.\end{split}

3.
Reduction to a characteristic problem

For the rest of the paper, we study a characteristic problem.

3.1
Decay for characteristic problem.

Consider characteristic data on
C^{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{\mathrm{out}}
with
future
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
. Assume the following:

(1)

The initial data norm
\mathfrak{D}_{k}[\varphi{}]
defined on
C^{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{\mathrm{out}}
is finite.

(2)

The data vanishes on a neighbourhood of the outgoing null hypersurface

C^{\text{out}}
(in particular on a portion of
C^{\text{in}}
near the
vertex).

(3)

There exists a double null gauge
(u,v)
in which
C^{\mathrm{in}}=[1,\infty)_{u}\times\{1\}_{v}
and
C^{\mathrm{out}}=\{1\}_{u}\times[1,\infty)_{v}
(so
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
is given by

[1,\infty)\times[1,\infty)
) and is normalized such that

\mathchoice{\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu{}}}{\frac{\partial_{v}r}{1-\mu{}}}{\partial_{v}r/1-\mu{}}{\partial_{v}r/1-\mu{}}|_{\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}}=1
, where
r_{\mathcal{H}}=\sup_{\mathcal{H}}r
,
and
\lim_{v\to\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu{}}}{\frac{(-\partial_{u}r)}{1-\mu{}}}{(-\partial_{u}r)/1-\mu{}}{(-\partial_{u}r)/1-\mu{}}(u,v)=1
for each

u
.

(4)

We have
\partial_{u}r<0
and
\partial_{v}r\geq 0
in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
(note this is gauge independent).

(5)

We have
\varpi{}\leq\varpi{}_{i}
in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
.

(6)

There is
c_{\mathcal{H}}>0
such that
\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r\geq c_{\mathcal{H}}
in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
.

(7)

There is
r_{\mathrm{min}}>0
such that
r\geq r_{\mathrm{min}}
in
\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}
.

(8)

The value
R_{0}=r|_{C^{\mathrm{in}}\cap C^{\mathrm{out}}}
, is sufficiently large based on

\varpi_{i}
(as specified in
section

4
).

Then for every
\epsilon{}>0
and integer
k\geq 0
there is
C=C(\epsilon{},k,\varpi_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\mathrm{min}},\mathfrak{D}_{k}[\varphi{}])>0
such that

(3.1)

\lvert(v\underline{\partial{}}_{v})^{k}\varphi{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C\mathfrak{D}_{k}[\varphi{}]v^{-1+\epsilon{}}.

For
0\leq k\leq 4
, the same estimate holds with
v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}
replaced by

\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}}
.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
out
[formula]
in
[formula]
char
[formula]

Figure 2
.
The characteristic rectangle
\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
depicted on the Penrose diagram of a general solution to
1.1
. Note that the achronal singular set
\mathcal{S}
may be empty, as is the case in Reissner–Nordström. The statement that the diagram looks as depicted is due to
[
8
,
27
]
.

3.2
.

We have
r_{\mathcal{H}}=:\sup_{\mathcal{H}}r\leq C(\varpi{}_{i})
, since by

[
31
, Lem. A.2]
, we have
r_{\mathcal{H}}=\varpi_{\mathcal{H}}+\sqrt{\varpi_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{e}^{2}}\leq 2\varpi_{i}
, where

\varpi_{\mathcal{H}}\coloneqq{}\sup_{\mathcal{H}}\varpi{}
.

We note that it is enough to consider only one of the ends of the spacetime; the argument is the
same for the other end.
We now show that the future of compactly supported future-admissible Cauchy data
contains a characteristic rectangle satisfying the assumptions of

theorem

3.1
, thus reducing the proof of
theorem

1.1
to
establishing
theorem

3.1
.

Constructing a characteristic rectangle as in
theorem

3.1
in the setting of
theorem

1.1
.

By the global well-posedness of
1.1
(see
[
27
]

or
[
31
, Thm. 4.1]
), we can first choose a characteristic rectangle in
the exterior region—where assumption (4) is satisfied (see

[
27
]
, or
[
31
, Lem. A.1]
)—and then construct the
gauge in assumption (2) once
R_{0}
is chosen as in assumption (8). Since the
Cauchy data is compactly supported, we can ensure that the data vanishes on a
neighbourhood of
C^{\text{out}}
, which in particular implies assumptions (1)
and (2) (together with the global well-posedness of the system). By assumption
(4) and
2.12
and
2.13
, we can take
\varpi_{i}
in
assumption (4) to be the supremum of
\varpi{}
on the Cauchy data. The future
admissibility condition on the data implies that the event horizon is eventually
subextremal by
[
27
]
(see also
[
31
, Lem. A.2]
), so
if we write
\varpi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\coloneqq{}\lim_{u\to\infty}\varpi{}(u,v)
and
r_{\mathcal{H}}(v)=\lim_{u\to\infty}r(u,v)
, then
there is
c_{\mathcal{H}}>0
such that
\lim_{v\to\infty}(\varpi{}_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r_{\mathcal{H}})(v)=2c_{\mathcal{H}}>0
. In particular, there is
v_{\ast}{}\geq 1
such that

(\varpi_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r_{\mathcal{H}})(v_{\ast}{})\geq c_{\mathcal{H}}>0
. The monotonicity properties of (3) together with the
equations
2.12
and
2.13
imply that
(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)(u,v)\geq(\varpi_{\mathcal{H}}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r_{\mathcal{H}})(v_{\ast}{})
for
u\geq 1
and
v\geq v_{\ast}{}
.
After using the translation symmetry in the gauge, we can assume
v_{\ast}{}=1
. This settles assumption (6). Assumption (7) holds since

\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
is in the exterior region. Finally, since
r\to\infty
along
C^{\text{out}}
, we can translate
C^{\text{in}}
towards
the future to satisfy assumption (8).
∎

3.3
Compact support of data.

The assumption that the data is compactly supported in
theorem

1.1
is
only used to reduce to a characteristic problem (in particular to ensure that we
can choose a characteristic rectangle for which
\mathfrak{D}_{k}[\varphi{}]
is
finite).

From now on, we work in the region
\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
and pursue the
proof of
theorem

3.1
.

4.
Estimates for undifferentiated geometric quantities

From now on, fix the value of
R_{0}
large enough that the estimates of

section

4
hold and such that
R_{0}\geq C(\varpi_{i})\geq r_{\mathcal{H}}
(see
remark

3.2
).

{proposition}
[Estimates for undifferentiated geometric quantities]
Let
\eta>0
. Let
R\geq R_{0}\geq 1
. If
R_{0}
is sufficiently large (depending only on
\varpi{}_{i}
), then the following estimates hold (where we allow all constants to depend on
c_{\mathcal{H}}
,
r_{\mathrm{min}}
,
\varpi_{i}
, and
R
in addition to any named parameters):

(1)

Globally, we have

(4.1)

\displaystyle\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r\geq c_{\mathcal{H}}>0,

(4.2)

\displaystyle\varpi{}\leq\varpi{}_{i},

(4.3)

\displaystyle 0\leq 1-\mu{}\leq C,

(4.4)

\displaystyle\kappa>0,

(4.5)

\displaystyle\log(-\gamma)\leq 0,

(4.6)

\displaystyle\lvert\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{2},

(4.7)

\displaystyle\lvert\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta{}},\eta{})\mathcal{E}_{0,1}^{2}v^{-1},

(4.8)

\displaystyle 0\leq\lambda{}\leq C\exp(C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{2}),

(4.9)

\displaystyle 0\leq(-\nu)\leq C.

(2)

Away from the horizon, we have

(4.10)

\displaystyle\lvert\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq Cr^{-1}\tau^{-p}\mathcal{E}_{0,p}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R\},

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lvert\log(-\nu)\rvert\leq Cr^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R\},

(4.12)

\displaystyle 1-\mu{}\geq 1/2

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R\},

(4.13)

\displaystyle\lambda\geq C\exp(-C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{2})

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R\},

(4.14)

\displaystyle(-\nu)\geq 1/2

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R\}.

(3)

Near the horizon, there are constants
c_{\nu},c^{\prime}_{\nu},C>0
(where
c^{\prime}_{\nu}
depends on
\mathcal{E}_{0}
and is independent of
R
and the other constants depend on
\eta
and
\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta}
) such that

(4.15)

C^{-1}\exp(-c_{\nu}^{\prime}(u-v))\leq(-\nu{})\leq C\exp(-c_{\nu}(u-v))\quad\text{in }\{r\leq R\}.

(4)

We have

(4.16)

\lvert v-u-r\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta{}},\eta{})\log r\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R\}.

(5)

Let
\epsilon{}\in(0,1)
. For
G_{\epsilon}{}
as defined in
2.33
, there is
C>0
depending on
\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta{}}
,
\eta
,
\epsilon{}
, and
R_{\bullet}
such that

(4.17)

C^{-1}\leq G_{\epsilon}{}\leq C

Proof.

The estimates

4.1
,
4.2
,
4.3
,
4.4
,
4.6
,
4.7
,
4.5
,
4.8
,
4.9
,
4.10
,
4.12
,
4.13
,
4.14
and
4.15

follow as in the proof of
[
32
, Prop. 5.12]
. Although
[
32
]

uses the
(u,\tilde{v})
gauge, the relevant arguments go through in the

(u,v)
gauge. We now show the remaining estimates

4.11
,
4.16
and
4.17
.

First,
4.11
follows from the
\partial{}_{v}
-transport equation for

(-\nu{})
and
(-\nu{})|_{\mathcal{I}}=1
(which follows from
(-\nu{})=(1-\mu{})(-\gamma{})
, the gauge condition
(-\gamma{})|_{\mathcal{I}}=1
, and
1-\mu{}=1+O(r^{-1})
).

The estimate
4.16
follows from
4.11
(which implies

\lvert 1+\nu{}\rvert\leq Cr^{-1}
) and an argument as in the proof of

[
32
, Prop. 5.12]
.

Finally, we establish
4.17
. For the remainder of the proof, we
allow the symbol
\lesssim
to include dependence on

C(\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta{}},\eta{})
as in
4.16
, as well as

\epsilon{}
and
R_{\bullet}
. It is enough to show

(4.18)

\displaystyle r

\displaystyle\lesssim v,

(4.19)

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\lesssim v\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R_{0}\},

(4.20)

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\lesssim u\quad\text{in }\{r\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}\}\cup\{r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v\}.

Indeed,
4.18
,
4.19
and
4.20
imply the upper bound in
4.17
, and the
lower bound
G_{\epsilon}{}\geq 1
is clear. In
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
, we have

r-R_{0}\leq v/2
by
4.13
. Thus
r\leq(R_{0}+1/2)v
, which
establishes
4.18
. In
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
, by
4.16
, we have

u\lesssim v+r+\log r\lesssim v
, which establishes
4.19
. If
r\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}
, then
v\lesssim u+\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}+\log(\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet})
by
4.25
, so
v\lesssim u
in this region.
If
r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v
, then
v=u+r+O(\log r)\leq u+(1-\epsilon{})v+O(\log v)
, so for
v\geq v_{\ast}{}
and
v_{\ast}{}
large
enough we have
v\leq 2\epsilon{}^{-1}u
. For
v\leq v_{\ast}{}
we have
v\leq v_{\ast}{}u
since
u\geq 1
. Thus
v\leq\max(2\epsilon{}^{-1},v_{\ast}{})u
in this region.
∎

As an immediate corollary of
section

4
, we control the
zeroth order schematic geometric quantities.

{corollary}
[Estimates for zeroth order schematic geometric quantities]
We have

(4.21)

\begin{split}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})&\leq C(\mathcal{E}_{0},\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\mathrm{min}},R_{0},\eta{}_{0}),\\
C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ})&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0,1}),\\
C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})&\leq C(R_{\bullet},\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta{}_{0}}),\\
C(\mathfrak{g}_{0})&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0}),\\
C(\mathfrak{G}_{0})&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0}).\\
\end{split}

We now use
4.25
to compare the variables
r
,
u
,
v
, and

\tau{}
.

{lemma}

The following estimates hold for
\epsilon{}\in(0,1)
:

(4.22)

\displaystyle r+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u\leq G_{\epsilon}{}v,

(4.23)

\displaystyle v\leq G_{\epsilon}{}u,

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}\}\cup\{r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v\}

(4.24)

\displaystyle v\leq(G_{\epsilon}{}+\epsilon{}^{-1})(u+r).

Moreover, we have

(4.25)

\begin{split}G_{\epsilon}^{-1}v\leq\tau{}\leq G_{\epsilon}{}v&\quad\text{in }\{r\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}\}\cup\{r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v\}\\
\tau{}=u&\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R_{0}\}.\end{split}

Proof.

The definition of
G
implies
4.22
, as well as

4.23
in the region
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
. To extend

4.23
to the region
\{r\leq R_{0}\}
, note that if
r(u,v)\leq R_{0}
, then
u\geq u_{R_{0}}(v)
, and so
v\leq Gu_{R_{0}}(v)\leq Gu
. Next,

4.24
follows from
4.23
, because

(4.26)

v\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v}v+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq(1-\epsilon{})v}v\leq G_{\epsilon}{}u+(1-\epsilon{})^{-1}r.

By
4.22
and
4.23
, we have

(4.27)

G_{\epsilon}^{-1}v\leq u\leq G_{\epsilon}{}v\quad\text{in }(\{r\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{\bullet}\}\cup\{r\leq(1-\epsilon{})v\})\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}.

The estimates in
4.25
now follow from the definition of
\tau{}
(see

2.18
).
∎

5.
Boundedness of initial data

In this section we explain how the initial data
norm
\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}=\mathcal{D}[\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]
(which is not
gauge invariant) is controlled by the gauge invariant initial data norm

\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}
. See
section

2.7
for definitions of these
quantities.

{proposition}
[Boundedness of initial data]
If the data vanishes in a neighbourhood of
C^{\text{out}}
, then we have

(5.1)

\mathcal{D}_{0}=\mathfrak{D}_{0}.

and for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
we have

(5.2)

\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{<\alpha{}})\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}.

5.1
.

The restriction that the solution vanish in a neighbourhood of the outgoing null
part of the data hypersurface can be removed, but for the simplicity of the
argument we only give the proof in this case. Indeed, we have reduced to this
case in
section

3
using the compact support of the
Cauchy data.

Proof.

First,
5.1
is trivial. Define

(5.3)

\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{in}}[\psi{}]\coloneqq{}\sup_{C^{\mathrm{in}}}\lvert U^{\leq n+1}\psi{}\rvert\qquad\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{out}}[\psi{}]\coloneqq{}\sup_{C^{\mathrm{out}}}\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}(r\psi{})\rvert,

so that

(5.4)

\mathfrak{D}_{n}[\psi{}]=\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{in}}[\psi{}]+\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{out}}[\psi{}].

The vanishing assumption on the data implies by the domain of dependence
property that the solution vanishes in a neighbourhood of
C^{\text{out}}
,
and so
\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{out}}[\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]=0
. In
particular, it is enough to estimate

\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{in}}[\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]
. The desired estimate

5.2
follows from the
n=0
case of

5.5
:

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{in}}[\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}-1+nU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}-1+nU})\mathfrak{D}_{n+\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}^{\text{in}}[\varphi{}].

We now prove
5.5
.

Step 1: Reduction to
5.8
.
By induction,
5.5

follows from

(5.6)

\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\mathrm{in}}[\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}-1+nU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}-1+nU})\mathfrak{D}_{n+1}^{\text{in}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\varphi{}].

Since
v\equiv 1
on
C^{\text{in}}
and

Uv=0
, we have
S=V
, and so we can assume
\alpha_{S}=0
. Moreover,

U
commutes with
V
on
C^{\text{in}}\subset\{r\leq R_{\bullet}\}
. Clearly we have

(5.7)

\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{in}}[U\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]\leq\mathfrak{D}_{n+1}^{\text{in}}[\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}],

so it is enough to show that

(5.8)

\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{in}}[V\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+nU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+nU})\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{in}}[U\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}].

Step 2: Proof of
5.8
.
Use
2.63
and
2.8.3
and an
induction argument to compute

(5.9)

UV\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})[V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}+U^{\leq 2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}]

It follows from an induction argument that

(5.10)

\lvert U^{n}UV\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+nU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+nU})[\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert U^{\leq n+1}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+nU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+nU})[\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{in}}[\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]].

Integrate
5.10
for
n=0
to
C^{\text{in}}\cap C^{\text{out}}

(where the data vanishes) to get

(5.11)

\begin{split}\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}\rvert|_{v=1}(r)&\leq\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}\rvert|_{v=1}(r=R_{0})+\int_{r}^{R_{0}}\lvert UV\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})\mathfrak{D}_{0}^{\text{in}}[U\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}]+C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})\int_{r}^{R_{0}}\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}.\end{split}

Grönwall’s inequality and the finite
r
-range on
C^{\text{in}}
gives

(5.12)

\sup_{C^{\text{in}}}\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})\mathfrak{D}_{0}^{\text{in}}[U\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}].

Substitute
5.12
into
5.10
and use
5.7
to
obtain

(5.13)

\sup_{C^{\text{in}}}\lvert U^{n}UV\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+nU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+nU})\mathfrak{D}_{n}^{\text{in}}[U\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varphi{}].

Now
5.12
and
5.13
imply
5.8
.
∎

6.
Energy boundedness and decay

The goal of this section is to show the following proposition.

{proposition}
[Estimates for unweighted and weighted energy norms]
We have

(6.1)

\mathcal{E}_{0}\leq C(\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\mathrm{min}})\mathcal{D}_{0}.

and

(6.2)

\mathcal{E}_{0,2-\eta{}_{0}}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0})\mathcal{D}_{0}.

Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
. If
R_{\bullet}\geq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})
, then for

s\geq\eta{}_{0}
sufficiently small (depending on a numerical constant), we have

(6.3)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})[\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}+\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},4s}],

and when
s
is moreover small depending on
\alpha{}
, we have

(6.4)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},2-\eta{}_{0}-C_{\alpha}{}s}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}

for explicit constants
C_{\alpha}{}
depending only on
\alpha{}
.

Proof.

The zeroth order estimate
6.1
follows from

remark

6.1
,
6.12
, and
6.44
from

section

6.4
. The higher order estimate
6.3
is
established in
6.115
. The boundedness statements

6.2
and
6.4
for the weighted energy are
proved in
6.145
.
∎

6.1.
Hardy inequalities

Both of these lemmas and their proofs can be found in
[
31
, §8.8]
.

{lemma}

Let
a\in\mathbf{R}
,
1\leq u_{1}<u_{2}
, and
1\leq v_{1}<v_{2}
. For any
C^{1}
function
f

on
\{(u,v):v\in[v_{1},v_{2}]\}
, we have

(6.5)

\begin{split}&a\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}r^{a}\lambda{}f^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{a}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{a}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{a}/\lambda{}}{r^{a}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(rf))^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+r^{1+a}f^{2}(u,v_{1})\\
&=\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2+a}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{2+a}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{2+a}/\lambda{}}{r^{2+a}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}f)^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+r^{1+a}f^{2}(u,v_{2}).\end{split}

For any
C^{1}
function
f

on
\{(u,v):u\in[u_{1},u_{2}]\}
, we have

(6.6)

\begin{split}&a\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}r^{a}(-\nu{})f^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{a}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{a}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{a}/(-\nu{})}{r^{a}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}(rf))^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+r^{1+a}f^{2}(u_{2},v)\\
&=\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2+a}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2+a}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2+a}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2+a}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}f)^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+r^{1+a}f^{2}(u_{1},v).\end{split}

{lemma}

Let
a\in\mathbf{R}
,
1\leq u_{1}<u_{2}
, and
1\leq v_{1}<v_{2}
. For any
C^{1}
function
f

on
\{(u,v):v\in[v_{1},v_{2}]\}
, we have

(6.7)

\begin{split}&\mathchoice{\frac{(a+1)^{2}}{4}}{\frac{(a+1)^{2}}{4}}{(a+1)^{2}/4}{(a+1)^{2}/4}\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}r^{a}\lambda{}f^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{a}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{a}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{a}/\lambda{}}{r^{a}/\lambda{}}\Bigl(r\partial{}_{v}f+\mathchoice{\frac{a+1}{2}}{\frac{a+1}{2}}{a+1/2}{a+1/2}\lambda{}f\Bigr)^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\mathchoice{\frac{a+1}{2}}{\frac{a+1}{2}}{a+1/2}{a+1/2}r^{1+a}f^{2}(u,v_{1})\\
&=\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2+a}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{2+a}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{2+a}/\lambda{}}{r^{2+a}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}f)^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\mathchoice{\frac{a+1}{2}}{\frac{a+1}{2}}{a+1/2}{a+1/2}r^{1+a}f^{2}(u,v_{2}).\end{split}

For any
C^{1}
function
f

on
\{(u,v):u\in[u_{1},u_{2}]\}
, we have

(6.8)

\begin{split}&\mathchoice{\frac{(a+1)^{2}}{4}}{\frac{(a+1)^{2}}{4}}{(a+1)^{2}/4}{(a+1)^{2}/4}\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}r^{a}(-\nu{})f^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{a}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{a}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{a}/(-\nu{})}{r^{a}/(-\nu{})}\Bigl(r\partial{}_{u}f+\mathchoice{\frac{a+1}{2}}{\frac{a+1}{2}}{a+1/2}{a+1/2}(-\nu{})f\Bigr)^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\mathchoice{\frac{a+1}{2}}{\frac{a+1}{2}}{a+1/2}{a+1/2}r^{1+a}f^{2}(u_{2},v)\\
&=\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2+a}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2+a}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2+a}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2+a}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}f)^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\mathchoice{\frac{a+1}{2}}{\frac{a+1}{2}}{a+1/2}{a+1/2}r^{1+a}f^{2}(u_{1},v).\end{split}

6.2.
Energy quantities

In order to state the energy boundedness estimate, we introduce notation for
energy quantities along null curves and over spacetime regions.

6.2.1.
Energy along piecewise null curves

For
\Sigma{}
a null curve, we define

(6.9)

E[\psi{},\Sigma{}]\coloneqq{}\begin{cases}\vskip 4.30554pt\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}{}\dfrac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}(\partial_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v&\text{if }u\text{ is constant on }\Sigma,\\
\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma}{}\dfrac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u&\text{if }v\text{ is constant on }\Sigma.\\
\end{cases}

We extend the definition to piecewise null curves
\Sigma{}
in the natural way: if

\Sigma{}=\bigcup_{i}\Sigma_{i}
for null curves
\Sigma_{i}
such that

\Sigma_{i}\cap\Sigma_{j}
consists of at most one point when
i\neq{}j
, then
we set

(6.10)

E[\psi{},\Sigma{}]\coloneqq{}\sum_{i}E[\psi{},\Sigma_{i}].

6.1
.

The energy norm
\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]
defined in
section

2.7.2
is related to

E[\psi{},\Sigma{}]
as follows:

(6.11)

\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]^{2}=\sup_{\Sigma}{}E[\psi{},\Sigma{}],

where the supremum is taken over all null curves
\Sigma{}\subset\{u,v\geq 1\}
.

{lemma}

[Energy on initial data]
Let
p\in C^{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{\mathrm{out}}
. Then

(6.12)

E[\psi{},C^{\mathrm{in}}\cup C^{\mathrm{out}}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p)\leq C(r_{\mathrm{min}},\varpi{}_{i},R_{0})\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]^{2}.

Proof.

The proof is immediate by the definitions,
4.3
, and a change of
variables.
∎

6.2.2.
r^{p}
-weighted energy

For
\tau{}_{0}\geq 1
, define the level set
\Sigma_{\tau{}_{0}}\coloneqq{}\{p:\tau{}(p)=\tau{}_{0}\}
, where

\tau{}
was defined in
2.18
. Then
\Sigma_{\tau}{}
is piecewise null, with null pieces

(6.13)

\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\text{in}}\coloneqq{}\Sigma_{\tau}{}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}=\{(u,v_{R_{0}}(u)):u\geq\tau{}\}\qquad\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\text{out}}\coloneqq{}\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}=\{(\tau{},v):v\geq v_{R_{0}}(\tau{})\}.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 3
.
The foliation
\Sigma_{\tau}
.

We introduce the
r^{p}
-weighted energy flux associated to the foliation

\Sigma_{\tau}{}
:

(6.14)

E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{})\coloneqq{}\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\text{in}}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v_{R_{0}}(\tau{})).

We have the following preliminary estimate comparing
E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{})
and the
energy quantities
E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}]
(defined in
section

6.4
).

{lemma}

For any
p\geq 0
and
1\geq\tau{}
, we have

(6.15)

E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{}).

Proof.

It is enough to consider the case
p=0
,
which is an immediate consequence of a Hardy type inequality
(
6.6
with
a=0
and
v_{1}=v_{R_{0}}(\tau{})
in the
limit
v_{2}\to\infty
).
∎

{lemma}

For
p<3
, we have

(6.16)

E_{p}[\psi{}](1)\leq C(p,R_{0})\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]^{2}.

Proof.

This is immediate from the definitions and a change of variables:

(6.17)

E_{p}[\psi{}](1)=\int_{r_{\text{min}}}^{R_{0}}r^{2}(U\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r+\int_{R_{0}}^{\infty}r^{p}(\overline{\partial}_{r}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r+\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(1,1)\leq R_{0}^{2}\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]^{2}+\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]^{2}+\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]^{2}\int_{R_{0}}^{\infty}r^{p-4}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r.

The condition
p<3
is used to ensure that the final integral is finite.
∎

6.2.3.
Bulk energy over spacetime region

Let
\mathcal{R}
be a spacetime region. Define a bulk
energy quantity:

(6.18)

\begin{split}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]&\coloneqq{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})^{2}}}{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})^{2}}}{1/(-\nu{})^{2}}{1/(-\nu{})^{2}}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}^{2}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}^{2}}}{1/\kappa{}^{2}}{1/\kappa{}^{2}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{2}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{2}}\Bigr]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\qquad+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}}e^{-c_{\nu{}}^{\prime}(u-v)}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v,\end{split}

where
\eta_{0}
is our global small constant (see
section

2.6.2
) and

c_{\nu}^{\prime}
is as in
4.15
.

6.3.
Vector field multiplier identities

In this section we record the vector field multiplier identities used to derive
an energy estimate in
section

6
. We introduce the notation

(6.19)

D_{u}=U=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})}}{1/(-\nu{})}{1/(-\nu{})}\partial{}_{u}\qquad D_{v}=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}\partial{}_{v}.

The vector field multipliers we use are

(6.20)

\displaystyle T

\displaystyle=(1-\mu{})D_{u}+D_{v},

(Kodama vector field)

(6.21)

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle=f(r)((1-\mu{})D_{u}-D_{v}),

(Morawetz vector field)

(6.22)

\displaystyle Y

\displaystyle=\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)D_{u},

(Redshift vector field)

(6.23)

\displaystyle Z

\displaystyle=g(u,v)\partial{}_{v}.

(Irregular vector field)

{lemma}

[Vector field multiplier identity]
Let
W=W^{u}D_{u}+W^{v}D_{v}=(-\nu{})^{-1}W^{u}\partial_{u}+\kappa^{-1}W^{v}\partial_{v}
. Then

(6.24)

\partial{}_{u}(W^{v}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{})+\partial{}_{v}(W^{u}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{}))=[2W\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+K^{W}[\psi{}]]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}),

where

(6.25)

K^{W}[\psi{}]\coloneqq{}(-\nu{})D_{v}((-\nu{})^{-1}W^{u})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+\kappa{}D_{u}(\kappa{}^{-1}W^{v})(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r}}{\frac{2}{r}}{2/r}{2/r}(-W^{u}+(1-\mu{})W^{v})D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}.

Proof.

Note that
W=\widetilde{W}^{u}\partial_{u}+\widetilde{W}^{v}\partial_{v}
for

(\widetilde{W}^{u},\widetilde{W}^{v})=((-\nu{})^{-1}W^{u},\kappa{}^{-1}W^{v})
. Use
the wave equation in double null coordinates to compute

(6.26)

\begin{split}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}&=r^{2}\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\Bigl(\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}+\mathchoice{\frac{\partial{}_{v}r}{r}}{\frac{\partial{}_{v}r}{r}}{\partial{}_{v}r/r}{\partial{}_{v}r/r}\partial{}_{u}\psi{}+\mathchoice{\frac{\partial{}_{u}r}{r}}{\frac{\partial{}_{u}r}{r}}{\partial{}_{u}r/r}{\partial{}_{u}r/r}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}\Bigr)=r^{2}\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}\psi{}+r\partial{}_{v}r(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}+r\partial{}_{u}r\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}\\
&=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{v}(r^{2}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2})+r\partial{}_{u}r\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}.\\
\end{split}

Multiply by
\widetilde{W}^{u}
and commute
W^{u}
past
\partial_{v}
to arrive at

(6.27)

r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\widetilde{W}^{u}\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{v}(\widetilde{W}^{u}r^{2}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2})-\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{v}\widetilde{W}^{u}r^{2}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}+r\widetilde{W}^{u}\partial{}_{u}r\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}.

Add
6.27
to the result of exchanging
u
and
v
in

6.27
to obtain

(6.28)

\begin{split}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})W\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}&=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{v}(\widetilde{W}^{u}r^{2}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2})+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{u}(\widetilde{W}^{v}r^{2}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2})-\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{u}\widetilde{W}^{v}r^{2}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}-\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{v}\widetilde{W}^{u}r^{2}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+(r\widetilde{W}^{v}\partial{}_{v}r+r\widetilde{W}^{u}\partial{}_{u}r)\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}.\end{split}

Express
6.28
in terms of
D_{u}
and
D_{v}
, then rearrange to
obtain the desired result.
∎

{lemma}

[Kodama vector field identity]
Let
T=(1-\mu{})D_{u}+D_{v}
. We have

(6.29)

\partial{}_{u}((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{})+\partial{}_{v}((1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{}))=2T\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})+r[-(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}(D_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+(D_{u}\varphi{})^{2}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}),

Proof.

Use
6.25
with
(T^{u},T^{v})=(1-\mu{},1)
. In particular we use the
transport equations

(6.30)

D_{v}(-\gamma{})^{-1}=-(-\nu{})^{-1}r(D_{v}\varphi{})^{2}\qquad D_{u}\kappa{}^{-1}=\kappa{}^{-1}r(D_{u}\varphi{})^{2}

to compute

(6.31)

K^{T}[\psi{}]=(-\nu{})D_{v}((-\gamma{})^{-1})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+\kappa{}D_{u}(\kappa{}^{-1})(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}=r[-(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}(D_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+(D_{u}\varphi{})^{2}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}].

∎

{lemma}

[Morawetz vector field]
Let
f:(0,\infty)_{r}\to\mathbf{R}
be a
C^{2}
function. Let
X=(1-\mu{})f(r)D_{u}-f(r)D_{v}
. Then

(6.32)

\begin{split}&-f^{\prime}(r)[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})+4\mathchoice{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{f(r)/r}{f(r)/r}(1-\mu{})D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&\leq\partial{}_{u}(f(r)(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{})-\partial{}_{v}(f(r)(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{}))+2X\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}).\end{split}

Moreover,

(6.33)

\begin{split}&-f^{\prime}(r)[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})+4\mathchoice{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{f(r)/r}{f(r)/r}\mu{}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&\qquad+\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{2}{1-\mu{}}}{\frac{2}{1-\mu{}}}{2/1-\mu{}}{2/1-\mu{}}\mathchoice{\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(r)}{r}}{\frac{f^{\prime\prime}(r)}{r}}{f^{\prime\prime}(r)/r}{f^{\prime\prime}(r)/r}+(f^{\prime}(r)r-f(r))\mathchoice{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}\Bigr]\psi{}^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&\leq\partial{}_{u}(f(r)(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}-2f^{\prime}(r)r\lambda{}\psi{}^{2}+f(r)r\psi{}^{2})-\partial{}_{v}(f(r)(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{})+2f^{\prime}(r)r\nu{}\psi{}^{2}-f(r)r\psi{}^{2})\\
&\qquad+\Bigl(2X\psi{}+4f(r)\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}}{r}}{\frac{\psi{}}{r}}{\psi{}/r}{\psi{}/r}\Bigr)\Box{}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}).\end{split}

Proof.

Use
6.25
with
(X^{u},X^{v})=((1-\mu{})f(r),-f(r))
to obtain

(6.34)

K^{-X}[\psi{}]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})=\partial_{u}(f(r)(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{})-\partial_{v}(f(r)(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{}))+2X\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}.

From
6.30
we can compute

(6.35)

-(-\nu{})D_{v}(f(r)(-\gamma{})^{-1})=f(r)r(D_{v}\varphi{})^{2}-f^{\prime}(r)(1-\mu{})^{2}\qquad\kappa{}D_{u}(f(r)\kappa{}^{-1})=f(r)r(D_{u}\varphi{})^{2}-f^{\prime}(r),

and then obtain

(6.36)

K^{-X}[\psi{}]=[f(r)r(D_{v}\varphi{})^{2}-f^{\prime}(r)(1-\mu{})^{2}](D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+[f(r)r(D_{u}\varphi{})^{2}-f^{\prime}(r)](D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{4f(r)}{r}}{\frac{4f(r)}{r}}{4f(r)/r}{4f(r)/r}(1-\mu{})D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}.

Neglecting the terms with a positive sign, we arrive at

(6.37)

K^{-X}[\psi{}]\geq-f^{\prime}(r)[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]+4\mathchoice{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{f(r)/r}{f(r)/r}(1-\mu{})D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}.

To obtain
6.32
, use the wave equation for
r
to rewrite
the last term on the left of
6.32
:

(6.38)

\begin{split}4\mathchoice{\frac{f}{r}}{\frac{f}{r}}{f/r}{f/r}(1-\mu{})D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})&=4fr\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\partial{}_{v}\psi{}-4\mathchoice{\frac{f}{r}}{\frac{f}{r}}{f/r}{f/r}\mu{}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&=\partial{}_{u}(-2f^{\prime}r\lambda{}\psi{}^{2}+fr\psi{}^{2})+\partial{}_{v}(-2f^{\prime}r\nu{}\psi{}^{2}+fr\psi{}^{2})\\
&\qquad+\Bigl[-\mathchoice{\frac{4f}{r}}{\frac{4f}{r}}{4f/r}{4f/r}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+\mathchoice{\frac{2}{1-\mu{}}}{\frac{2}{1-\mu{}}}{2/1-\mu{}}{2/1-\mu{}}\mathchoice{\frac{f^{\prime\prime}}{r}}{\frac{f^{\prime\prime}}{r}}{f^{\prime\prime}/r}{f^{\prime\prime}/r}\psi{}^{2}\\
&\qquad+(f^{\prime}r-f)\mathchoice{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}\psi{}^{2}+4\mathchoice{\frac{f}{r}}{\frac{f}{r}}{f/r}{f/r}\mu{}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}\Bigr]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}).\end{split}

∎

{lemma}

[Morawetz vector field with
f(r)=-1
]
Let
\widetilde{X}=-(1-\mu{})D_{u}+D_{v}
. We have

(6.39)

\begin{split}&\partial{}_{u}((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}+r\psi{}^{2})-\partial{}_{v}((1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{})+r\psi{}^{2})+\mathchoice{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}\psi{}^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&\leq\Bigl(2\widetilde{X}\psi{}-4\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}}{r}}{\frac{\psi{}}{r}}{\psi{}/r}{\psi{}/r}\Bigr)\Box{}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})+\mathchoice{\frac{4(2\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{4(2\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{4(2\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{4(2\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}).\end{split}

Proof.

Set
f(r)=-1
in
6.33
and rearrange.
∎

{lemma}

[Redshift vector field identity]
Let
\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)
be a
C^{1}
function, and write
Y=\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)D_{u}
. We have

(6.40)

\begin{split}&\partial{}_{v}(\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}(-\nu{}))+\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&=\Bigl[2Y\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+(1-\mu{})\chi{}^{\prime}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}-\mathchoice{\frac{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r}}{\frac{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r}}{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r}{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}\Bigr]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}),\end{split}

Proof.

Set
(Y^{u},Y^{v})=(\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r),0)
. Compute

(6.41)

K^{Y}[\psi{}]=(-\nu{})D_{v}((-\nu{})^{-1}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r))(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}-\mathchoice{\frac{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r}}{\frac{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r}}{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r}{2\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}.

Use the equation for
\partial{}_{v}(-\nu{})
to compute

(6.42)

\begin{split}(-\nu{})D_{v}((-\nu{})^{-1}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r))&=-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)+(1-\mu{})\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}(r).\end{split}

Substitute
6.42
into
6.41
to get
∎

{lemma}

[Irregular vector field identity]
Let
g=(u,v)
be a
C^{1}
function. Then

(6.43)

(-\partial{}_{u}g)(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}=-\partial_{u}(g(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2})+\Bigl[2\kappa{}g\partial{}_{v}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r}}{\frac{2}{r}}{2/r}{2/r}\lambda{}gDu_{\psi}{}D_{v}\psi{}\Bigr]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{}).

Proof.

Apply
6.25
with
(W^{u},W^{v})=(0,\kappa{}g)
.
∎

6.4.
Energy boundedness estimate

We say
\mathcal{R}
is an
admissible spacetime
region
if the past and future boundaries of
\mathcal{R}
are each connected
and piecewise null.

6.2
.

We apply the energy estimate to characteristic rectangles and regions of
the form
\bigcup_{\tau{}\in[\tau_{1},\tau_{2}]}\Sigma_{\tau}{}
, which are both clearly admissible.

Recall the notations
D_{u}
and
D_{v}
introduced in
6.19
. See

section

6.3
for the vector field multipliers and
associated identities used in the proof of
section

6.4
.

{lemma}
[Energy estimate]
Let
\mathcal{R}
be an admissible spacetime region with past boundary
\Sigma_{1}

and future boundary
\Sigma_{2}
. We have

(6.44)

E[\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{2}]\leq C(\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\mathrm{min}})E[\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{1}].

Let
p_{\mathrm{fut}}
be the future endpoint of
\Sigma_{1}
. Then

(6.45)

E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{2}]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})[E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})]+\mathrm{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]

for an error term that admits a decomposition

(6.46)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq\mathrm{Err}_{U}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathrm{Err}_{v}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+\mathbf{1}_{\psi{}\neq{}\varphi{}}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{zo}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]),\end{split}

where

(6.47)

\mathrm{Err}_{U}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]=\iint_{\mathcal{R}}w_{U}D_{u}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}

for a non-negative weight
w_{U}
satisfying
0<C(\varpi{}_{i},r_{\mathrm{min}})\leq w_{U}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
, and

(6.48)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{v}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\lvert D_{v}\psi{}\rvert\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert,

(6.49)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}r\lvert D_{u}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\lvert D_{v}\psi{}\rvert^{2},

(6.50)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{zo}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{\lvert\psi{}\rvert}{r}}{\frac{\lvert\psi{}\rvert}{r}}{\lvert\psi{}\rvert/r}{\lvert\psi{}\rvert/r}\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert.

6.3
Structure of error terms.

Observe that the error terms composing
\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
are
non-negative, except for
\mathrm{Err}_{U}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
, which does
not have a sign. The positivity of the weight
w_{U}
is crucial for closing the
energy estimates. This is because commutation produces a
D_{u}\psi{}
term with
a negative coefficient (see
section

6.5
). In the absence of this good
sign, we would not be able to control this term, since there is no smallness to
exploit.

Proof.

We follow the proof of
[
32
, Lem. 8.35]
. We do not mention uses of

section

4
to estimate zeroth order geometric quantities
after Step 1. To simplify the notation, we omit the volume form

r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v
when integrating over spacetime regions.

Step 1: Energy estimate with a weaker bulk term and proof of
6.44
.

Define the energy quantity

(6.51)

E_{\mathrm{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]\coloneqq{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{1/r^{4}}{1/r^{4}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}].

Compared to the bulk term
E_{\mathrm{bulk}}
that we wish to control,

E_{\text{weak}}
lacks a zeroth order term, does not capture improved
integrated local energy decay near the horizon, and has weaker
r
-weights. We
will show that

(6.52)

\begin{split}E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{2}]+E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]&\lesssim E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}],\end{split}

where the constant implied by
\lesssim
is positive and depends only on and
\varpi_{i}
,

c_{\mathcal{H}}
, and
r_{\text{min}}
. Since
\text{Err}[\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]=0
, we obtain
6.44
as an immediate corollary of
6.52
.

Step 1a: Outline of proof.
The proof is a standard application of the vector
field multiplier method, using the Kodama vector field
T
, the Morawetz
vector field
X
, and the redshift vector field
Y
. We will give a careful
proof, in order to emphasize the structure of the right side, in particular the
parameters on which the constants depend and the positivity of the weight

w_{U}
.

We will show the following three estimates:

(6.53)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma_{2}^{v}}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{2}^{u}}(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{R}}T\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\mathbf{1}_{\psi{}\neq{}\varphi{}}\text{Err}_{\text{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}],

(6.54)

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}\Bigl[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}\Bigr]

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathcal{R}}X\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{energy:T-estimate}\text{{)}},

(6.55)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma{}_{2}^{u}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{1/r^{4}}{1/r^{4}}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{R}}Y\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{energy:X-estimate}\text{{)}},

where

(6.56)

T=(1-\mu{})U+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}\partial{}_{v},\qquad X=r^{-3}(1-\mu{})U-r^{-3}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}\partial{}_{v}\qquad Y=\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)U,

and the implicit constants depend only on
c_{\mathcal{H}}
,
\varpi{}_{i}
, and
r_{\text{min}}
.
Observe that the left sides of
6.53
and
6.54
bound the left side of

6.51
from above in a region
\{r\geq R\}
for
R\geq C(\varpi_{i},r_{\text{min}})
(in view of (
4.12
)), and
6.55
gives the control
in the remaining region
\{r\leq R\}
. Thus

6.53
,
6.54
and
6.55
together give

(6.57)

E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{2}]+E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}}W\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+CE[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\mathbf{1}_{\psi{}\neq{}\varphi{}}C\text{Err}_{\text{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}],

for a vector field

(6.58)

W=C_{T}T+C_{X}X+C_{Y}Y,

where the constants
C,C_{T},C_{X},C_{Y}
are positive and depend only on
\varpi_{i}
,

c_{\mathcal{H}}
, and
r_{\text{min}}
. In view of

6.56
, the coefficients of
W
are smooth and of size

C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
, and the
U
-coefficient is bounded below by a positive constant. In particular, we
have

(6.59)

W\psi{}=w_{U}U\psi{}+w_{v}\partial{}_{v}\psi{},\quad 1\lesssim w_{U},\quad\lvert w_{v}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0}),

and so

(6.60)

\iint_{\mathcal{R}}W\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}\leq\mathrm{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}],

which completes the proof of
6.51
when substituted into

6.57
.

Step 1b: Almost conservation law: proof of
6.53
.

Integrate the
T
-identity in
6.29
over
\mathcal{R}
to get

(6.61)

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma_{2}^{v}}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{2}^{u}}(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}&=2\iint_{\mathcal{R}}T\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+\int_{\Sigma_{1}^{v}}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\int_{\Sigma_{1}^{u}}(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+\iint_{\mathcal{R}}r[-(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}(D_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+(D_{u}\varphi{})^{2}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}].\end{split}

Since
1-\mu{}\leq C(\varpi_{i},r_{\text{min}})
, the boundary terms on
\Sigma_{1}
are
controlled by
E[\psi{},\Sigma_{1}]
. The final term vanishes if
\psi{}=\varphi{}
and is equal to
\text{Err}_{\text{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]

otherwise.

Step 1c: Weak integrated local energy decay estimate: proof of

6.54
.
Let
f(r)=r^{-3}
, so that

(6.62)

-f^{\prime}(r)-2f(r)/r=r^{-4}.

By Young’s inequality we have

(6.63)

r^{-4}[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\leq-f^{\prime}(r)[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]+4\mathchoice{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{f(r)/r}{f(r)/r}(1-\mu{})D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}=\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{X-estimate-equation}\text{{)}}.

The estimate follows from integrating
6.32
with
f(r)=r^{-3}
; of the four boundary terms generated, we can neglect two on account of
their sign, and control the other two using Step 1a.

Step 1d: Redshift estimate: proof of
6.55
.
Let
\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)
be a positive cutoff
function that is
1
in
\{r\leq R\}
and
0
in
\{r\geq 2R\}
.
Recall that
\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r\geq c_{\mathcal{H}}
and apply Young’s
inequality in the form

(6.64)

\Big\lvert\mathchoice{\frac{2\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r}}{\frac{2\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r}}{2\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r}{2\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}\Big\rvert\leq c_{\mathcal{H}}\mathchoice{\frac{\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)}{r^{2}}}{\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r^{2}}{\chi_{\mathcal{H}}(r)/r^{2}}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+c_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}.

after integrating the identity in
6.40
and noting the support properties of

\chi_{\mathcal{H}}
and
\chi_{\mathcal{H}}^{\prime}
to get

(6.65)

\begin{split}\int_{\Sigma{}_{2}^{u}}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+c_{\mathcal{H}}r_{\text{min}}^{2}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{1/r^{4}}{1/r^{4}}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}&\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{R}}Y\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{R\leq r\leq 2R\}}(1-\mu{})(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}\\
&\qquad+c_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq 2R\}}(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\int_{\Sigma{}_{1}^{u}}\chi{}_{\mathcal{H}}(r)(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}.\\
\end{split}

When
R\geq C(\varpi{}_{i},r_{\text{min}})
, we have
1-\mu{}\geq 1/2
by (
4.12
),
and so the second term can be controlled by Step 1b (up to a multiple of

R^{4}
), as can the third term. The final term is controlled by data.

Step 2: Control of zeroth order term away from the horizon.
The goal of this step is to show
that for
R_{1}\geq R_{0}
and
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
we have

(6.66)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq 2R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{4}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{4}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{4}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{4}}&\leq\epsilon{}^{1-\eta{}_{0}}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R_{1})\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R_{1},\epsilon{})(E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})).\end{split}

We multiply
6.39
by a non-decreasing cutoff function
\chi_{R_{1}}(r)

that is
0
in
\{r\leq R_{1}\}
and
1
in
\{r\geq 2R_{1}\}
and
integrate by parts. We illustrate how to handle the first term on the left side of

6.39
:

(6.67)

\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\chi{}_{R_{1}}(r)\partial{}_{u}((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}+r\psi{}^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\chi{}_{R_{1}}^{\prime}(r)(-\nu{})((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}+r\psi{}^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\Sigma{}_{1}^{\text{out}}}\chi{}_{R_{1}}(r)((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}+r\psi{}^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\qquad-\int_{\Sigma{}_{2}^{\text{out}}}\chi{}_{R_{1}}(r)((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}+r\psi{}^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\geq-\int_{\Sigma{}_{2}^{\text{out}}}((D_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\kappa{}+r\psi{}^{2})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\geq-C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})).\end{split}

The first terms on the second line have a good sign, and the term on the third line is
estimated by Step 1 and multiple uses of Hardy’s inequality in both the
u
-
and
v
-directions (it is here that we use the assumption that the past/future
boundary of
\mathcal{R}
is connected). One similarly obtains the analogous
estimate for the second term on the left of
6.39
. In
view of the above estimates and
4.1
, we obtain

(6.68)

\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq 2R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{4}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{4}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{4}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{4}}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}}))+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{X-tilde-identity-equation}\text{{)}}.

It is clear that

(6.69)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{X-tilde-identity-equation}\text{{)}}&\leq C\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\widetilde{X}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\iint_{\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\\
&\qquad+C(E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})).\end{split}

Although the first term on the right of (
6.69
) has a bad sign in front
of the
U\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}
term (recall that
\text{Err}_{U}
has a
positive weight), by Step 1 we can add a multiple of the non-negative quantity

E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
(where the constant depends on
R_{1}
)
to both sides of (
6.69
) and (in conjunction with
6.68
) obtain

(6.70)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq 2R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{4}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{4}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{4}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{4}}&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R_{1})\iint_{\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\\
&\qquad C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R_{1})(E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})).\end{split}

To complete the proof, note that the first term on the right of
6.70

is bounded by
C(R_{1},\epsilon{})E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
in the
region
\{R_{1}\leq r\leq\epsilon^{-1}R_{1}\}
, and in the remaining region we
have

(6.71)

\iint_{\{r\geq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\leq\epsilon{}^{1-\eta{}_{0}}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}].

Step 3: Improved integrated local energy decay away from the horizon.
In this
step we show that for
R_{1}=\max(2,2R_{0},\eta{}_{0}^{-1})
, we have

(6.72)

\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}\Bigl[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{2}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{2}}\Bigr]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]).

We will track the dependence of constants on
\eta_{0}
. Begin by writing

(6.73)

\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{X-estimate-equation-2}\text{{)}}=[\text{(I)}+\text{(II)}+\text{(III)}]r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})

for

(6.74)

\begin{split}\text{(I)}&=-f^{\prime}(r)[(1-\mu{})^{2}(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}],\\
\text{(II)}&=4\mathchoice{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{\frac{f(r)}{r}}{f(r)/r}{f(r)/r}\mu{}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{},\\
\text{(III)}&=\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{2}{1-\mu{}}}{\frac{2}{1-\mu{}}}{2/1-\mu{}}{2/1-\mu{}}f^{\prime\prime}(r)r+(f^{\prime}(r)r-f(r))\mathchoice{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{\frac{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{4}}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}{4(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{4}}\Bigr]\psi{}^{2}.\end{split}

Now
4.12
and the choice of
R_{1}
imply that for
f(r)=r^{-\eta_{0}}
and
r\geq R_{1}
, we have

(6.75)

\mathchoice{\frac{\eta{}_{0}}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{\eta{}_{0}}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\eta{}_{0}/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{\eta{}_{0}/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\lesssim\text{(I)},\quad\lvert\text{(II)}\rvert\lesssim\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{2+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{2+\eta{}_{0}}}D_{u}\psi{}D_{v}\psi{}\lesssim r^{-1}\text{(I)},\quad\mathchoice{\frac{\eta{}_{0}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{\eta{}_{0}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\eta{}_{0}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}{\eta{}_{0}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}\lesssim\text{(III)}.

We can therefore multiply (
6.73
) by a cutoff
\chi(r)
that is
0
in

\{r\leq R_{1}/2\}
and
1
in
\{r\geq R_{1}\}
, and integrate over
\mathcal{R}
to get

(6.76)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}\Bigl[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{2}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{2}}\Bigr]&\lesssim\eta{}_{0}^{-1}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{1}/2\}}\chi{}_{R_{1}}(r)\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{X-estimate-equation-2}\text{{)}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\end{split}

Now we integrate by parts on the right side. Using Hardy’s inequality in both
the
u
- and
v
-directions and Step 1, one can estimate the boundary terms
by
E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})
. The bulk term involving
\Box{}\psi{}
is bounded
by
\mathrm{Err}_{U}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
and

\mathrm{Err}_{v}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
and

\text{Err}_{\text{zo}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}\cap\{R_{1}/2\}]
. In view of Steps
1 and 2 (applied with
R_{1}/2
in place of
R_{1}
), the support properties of

\chi_{R_{1}}
, and
section

4
, the bulk terms arising
from integration by parts are bounded by

(6.77)

\begin{split}&C(R_{1})\eta{}_{0}^{-1}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{R_{1}/2\leq r\leq R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{\frac{1}{r^{4}}}{1/r^{4}}{1/r^{4}}\Bigl[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}+\psi{}^{2}\Bigr]\\
&\leq\epsilon{}^{1-\eta{}_{0}}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R_{1},\eta{}_{0})\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq\epsilon{}^{-1}R_{1}/2\}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{1/r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}[(D_{u}\psi{})^{2}+(D_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R_{1},\epsilon{},\eta{}_{0})(E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})).\end{split}

To conclude the proof, choose
\epsilon{}
small enough to absorb the first term on the
right of (
6.77
) to the left of (
6.76
).

Step 4: Control of zeroth order term near the horizon.
Here we upgrade the
control of the zeroth order term in
6.72
from Step 3 to

(6.78)

\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}\leq\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{energy-Step-3}\text{{)}}.

Let
R>0
. Use Hardy’s inequality in the
u
-direction to obtain

(6.79)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v&\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R\}}(-\nu{})\chi^{2}\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\Sigma{}_{1}^{\text{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}}r\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\qquad+\int_{v_{1}(R)}^{v_{2}(R)}r\psi{}^{2}(u_{R}(v),v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\end{split}

Integrate
6.79
over
R\in[R_{1},R_{1}+1]
, note the monotonicity in

R
of the first term on the left and first two terms on the right, and change
variables in the integration of the third term on the right to get

(6.80)

\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{1}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{\ast}{}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\frac{\psi{}^{2}}{r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}{\psi{}^{2}/r^{3+\eta{}_{0}}}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{1}+1\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\Sigma{}_{1}^{\text{out}}\cap\{r\leq R_{1}+1\}}r\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{R_{1}\leq r\leq R_{1}+1\}}(-\nu{})r\psi{}^{2}(u_{R}(v),v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\end{split}

We are done by a Hardy inequality argument as in Step 2 that controls the term
on
\Sigma_{1}^{\text{out}}
, together with the results of Steps 2–3 and

4.4
and
4.6
.

Step 5: Improved integrated local energy decay near the horizon.
We now control
the final term in the definition of
E_{\mathrm{bulk}}
by showing that

(6.81)

\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}}e^{-c_{\nu}^{\prime}(u-v)}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\lesssim E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+\text{Err}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}].

Let
g(u,v)=\chi{}(r(u,v))h(u,v)
for a cutoff function
\chi{}
satisfying

\chi{}(r)=1
in
\{r\leq R_{0}\}
and
\chi{}(r)=0
in
\{r\geq 2R_{0}\}
and
h(u,v)=e^{-c_{\nu}^{\prime}(u-v)}
, where
c_{\nu}^{\prime}>0
is as in

4.15
. Observe that
h\geq 0
and
-\partial_{u}h\geq 0
,
and
h\lesssim 1
in
\{r\leq 2R_{0}\}
by
4.15
.
Integrating
6.43
gives

(6.82)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}}(-\partial{}_{u}h)(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}r^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v&\leq\int_{\Sigma{}_{1}^{out}}g(D_{v}\psi{})r^{2}\kappa{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq 2R_{0}\}}2hD_{v}\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}\\
&\qquad+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq 2R_{0}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r}}{\frac{2}{r}}{2/r}{2/r}(1-\mu{})h\lvert D_{u}\psi{}\rvert\lvert D_{v}\psi{}\rvert\\
&\lesssim E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+\text{Err}_{v}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}].\end{split}

We are done after computing

(6.83)

-\partial_{u}h=-(-\nu{})\chi_{R}^{\prime}e^{-c_{\nu}^{\prime}(u-v)}+c_{\mathcal{H}}\chi_{R}e^{-c_{\nu}^{\prime}(u-v)}

and estimating the bulk term arising from the first term on the right of

6.83
by
E_{\text{weak}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
.
∎

6.5.
Boundedness of the unweighted energy
\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{}}

The goal of this section is to prove
6.115
.

{lemma}

Let
\alpha{}\geq 0
and let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. For
0<s<1/4
, we have

(6.84)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}r^{1+2s}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}&\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].\end{split}

Proof.

This follows from the pointwise estimate
2.70
and the
definitions of the energy quantities involved.
∎

{lemma}

[Estimate for zeroth order error term]
Let
\epsilon{}>0
and let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. For
s\in{}[\eta_{0},1/4]
, we have

(6.85)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{zo}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq\epsilon{}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]]\end{split}

Proof.

We omit the volume form
r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v
. Introduce a small
\delta{}>0
and
large
R>0
to be chosen in the course of the proof. Use an
r
-weighted
Young’s inequality to decompose

(6.86)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{zo}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&=\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert\\
&\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert+\delta{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{1+2s}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\delta{}^{-1}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R\}}r^{-s}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{1+s}}}{\frac{1}{r^{1+s}}}{1/r^{1+s}}{1/r^{1+s}}\mathchoice{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}/r^{2}}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}/r^{2}}\\
&\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}+\text{(II)}+\text{(III)}.\end{split}

To estimate term
(I)
, we use the structure of
\Box{}L\varphi{}
. In
particular,
\Box{}L\varphi{}
contains a top order term only when
L

contains a
U
or a
V
, in which case we can bring this vector field to the
front of
L
and treat the corresponding term as the derivative of a lower
order term. That is, we write
\alpha_{X}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},\alpha{})\lvert D\Gamma^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert
for

X\in\{U,V\}
. By
2.62
and
2.8.1
, we have

(6.87)

\begin{split}\alpha{}_{U}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert+\alpha{}_{V}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert&\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}})(\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert)(\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert).\end{split}

By
2.70
and
6.87
and Young’s inequality, we have

(6.88)

\begin{split}\mathchoice{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\frac{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert}{r}}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}{\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert/r}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert&\leq r^{-3+s}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})\Bigl[\alpha{}_{V}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert+\alpha{}_{U}\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert\Bigr]\\
&\qquad+r^{-3+s}C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert[\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert]\\
&\leq\delta{}r^{-3+s}[\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert^{2}]\\
&\qquad+r^{-3+s}C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\delta{})(\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert^{2}).\end{split}

Since
s<1
, integrating
6.88
with the volume form
r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v

gives

(6.89)

\text{(I)}\leq\delta{}E_{\text{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},R,\delta{})(E_{\text{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{s,\text{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]).

Next,
6.84
implies that

(6.90)

\text{(II)}\leq\delta{}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].

Finally, use
s\geq\eta_{0}
to estimate

(6.91)

\text{(III)}\leq\delta{}^{-1}R^{-\eta{}_{0}}E_{\text{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}].

Substitute
6.89
,
6.90
and
6.91
into
6.86
to obtain

(6.92)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{zo}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq(\delta{}+\delta{}^{-1}R^{-\eta{}_{0}})C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},R,\delta{})[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].\end{split}

To conclude, choose
\delta{}
small based on
\epsilon{}
and

C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})
, then choose
R
large depending
on
\eta_{0}
,
\delta{}
, and
\epsilon{}
.
∎

{lemma}

[Estimate for
U
- and
v
-error terms]
Let
\epsilon{}>0
. We have

(6.93)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{U}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq(\epsilon{}+R_{\bullet}^{-1+2s})C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{3s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].\end{split}

The same estimate holds with
\mathrm{Err}_{v}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]
on the left side.

Proof.

In what follows, we will freely use the bounds

(-\nu{}),\kappa{},\kappa{}^{-1},\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R}\lambda{}^{-1},r_{\mathrm{min}}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
. Use the positivity of

w_{U}
,
\kappa{}
, and
(-\nu{})
, the bound
\lvert w_{U}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
, the estimates for
UL\varphi{}\Box{}L\varphi{}
in

2.71
, and an
r
-weighted Young’s inequality to obtain

(6.94)

\begin{split}w_{U}UL\varphi{}\Box{}L\varphi{}r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})&\leq r^{s}C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert)(\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert)\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&\leq\epsilon{}r^{1-s}(\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert^{2})\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
