&\qquad+r^{-1+3s}C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})(\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert)\kappa{}(-\nu{})\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})r^{-1+3s}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert.\end{split}

Integrate (noting that
s\geq\eta_{0}
) to obtain

(6.95)

\text{Err}_{U}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq\epsilon{}E_{\text{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})[E_{\text{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{3s,\text{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].

Using the estimate for
\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\Box{}L\varphi{}
in

2.72
, one
obtains

(6.96)

\begin{split}\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert r^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})&\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})r^{s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert(\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert)+\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{err-wave-bound-prep}\text{{)}}\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})R_{\bullet}^{-1+2s}r^{1-s}(\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert^{2})+\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{err-wave-bound-prep}\text{{)}}\\
\end{split}

Integrate to obtain

(6.97)

\text{Err}_{v}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq R_{\bullet}^{-1+2s}C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})E_{\text{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{err-wave-bound-prep-2}\text{{)}}\\

Combine
6.95
and
6.97
to complete the proof.
∎

For the next two lemmas we introduce the following notation:

•

C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)
for the constant-
u
curve
\{(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}:u^{\prime}=u\}
,

•

v_{1}(u)
and
v_{2}(u)
for the past and future endpoints of

C^{\text{out}}(u)
i(the assumption that
\mathcal{R}
is an admissible
spacetime region ensures that
C^{\text{out}}(u)
is connected),

•

u_{1}
and
u_{2}
for the smallest and largest
u
-values attained in
\mathcal{R}
.

{lemma}

[Estimate for quartic error term]
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
and
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. There is
c_{\nu}>0
such that for
p\geq 1
and

\epsilon{}>0
, we have

(6.98)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq\epsilon{}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},p}^{2}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},p},\epsilon{})\Bigl[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}(u^{-p}+e^{-c_{\nu}u})E[L\varphi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\Bigr].\end{split}

Proof.

In Step 1 (see
6.99
), we prove the estimate for
\alpha{}=U
, and
Step 2 (see
6.102
) implies the estimate for
\alpha{}>U
.

Step 1: Estimate for
\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[U\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]
.
We start by showing that

(6.99)

\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[U\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{0,0}^{2}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\varphi{},\mathcal{R}].

By
2.64
, we have

(6.100)

(\partial{}_{v}U\varphi{})^{2}\leq r^{-2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})[(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+(U\varphi{})^{2}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{0,0}^{2}.

Thus

(6.101)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[U\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{r}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r}{\kappa{}}}{r/\kappa{}}{r/\kappa{}}(U\varphi{})^{2}(\partial{}_{v}U\varphi{})^{2}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{0,0}^{2}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{-1}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{-1}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{-1}/\kappa{}}{r^{-1}/\kappa{}}(U\varphi{})^{2}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{0,0}^{2}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\varphi{},\mathcal{R}],\end{split}

which proves
6.99
.

Step 2: Estimate for
\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]
in terms
of norms of
U\varphi{}
.
Let
\epsilon{}>0
and
p\geq 1
. We claim that

(6.102)

\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq\epsilon{}\mathcal{P}_{U,p}^{2}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]+\mathcal{P}_{U,p}^{2}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0},\epsilon{})\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}(u^{-p}+e^{-c_{\nu}u})E[\psi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}_{v_{1},v_{2}}(u)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u.

Since
r_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1}\leq C
, we immediately obtain

(6.103)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}_{\mathrm{quartic}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq C\mathcal{P}_{U,p}^{2}\iint_{\mathcal{R}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}\tau{}^{-p}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\end{split}

Split the integral in
6.103
as

(6.104)

\iint_{\mathcal{R}}=\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}\cap\{\tau{}\geq\epsilon{}^{-1}\}}+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\leq R_{0}\}\cap\{\tau{}\leq\epsilon{}^{-1}\}}+\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}+\text{(II)}+\text{(III)}.

To handle term
(I)
, let
c_{\nu}>0
be as in
4.15

and control
(-\nu{})
with
4.15
, use the smallness of

\tau{}^{-1}
and the near-horizon term in
E_{\text{bulk}}
(see
section

6.2.3
):

(6.105)

\text{(I)}\leq C\epsilon{}\iint_{\mathcal{R}\cap\{r\geq R_{0}\}}e^{-c_{\nu}(u-v)}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\leq C\epsilon{}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}].

Now we treat term
(II)
. Recall the notation introduced before the statement of this lemma. By

4.25
, in the region
\{r\leq R_{0}\}\cap\{\tau{}\leq\epsilon^{-1}\}
we have
v\leq v_{\ast}{}
for some
v_{\ast}{}\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0},\epsilon{})
. Since
\tau{}\geq 1
, the bound on
(-\nu{})
in

\{r\leq R_{0}\}
from
4.15
implies

(6.106)

\begin{split}\text{(II)}&\leq C\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}\int_{v_{1}(u)}^{\min(v_{2}(u),v_{R_{0}}(u),v_{\ast}{})}e^{-c_{\nu}(u-v)}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\\
&\leq Ce^{c_{\nu}v_{\ast}{}}\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}e^{-c_{\nu}u}\int_{v_{1}}^{v_{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u=C(\mathcal{B}_{0},\epsilon{})\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}e^{-c_{\nu}u}E[\psi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u.\end{split}

Finally, we handle term
(III)
. We have
(-\nu{})\leq C
by
4.9
,
and
\tau{}=u
in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
by definition, so

(6.107)

\begin{split}\text{(III)}&=\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}\int_{\max(v_{1}(u),v_{R_{0}}(u))}^{v_{2}(u)}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}\tau{}^{-p}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(-\nu{})(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\\
&\leq C\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}u^{-p}\int_{\max(v_{1}(u),v_{R_{0}}(u))}^{v_{2}(u)}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(u,v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\leq C\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}u^{-p}E[\psi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u.\end{split}

We conclude
6.102
by substituting

6.105
,
6.106
and
6.107
into

6.104
and
6.103
.
∎

{lemma}

Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. For
R_{\bullet}
sufficiently large depending on

C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})
and
s\geq\eta_{0}
sufficiently
small, we have

(6.108)

E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{2}]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})[E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].

Proof.

We induct on
\alpha{}
. The base case
\alpha{}=0
follows from
6.45
,
since
\mathrm{Err}[\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]
vanishes. It is therefore enough
to show that

(6.109)

E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{2}]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},p})[E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u_{1},v_{2})+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].

Let
\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2},\epsilon_{3}>0
. Combining

6.85
,
6.5
and
6.98
(and using
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
so that
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})
) gives

(6.110)

\begin{split}\text{Err}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq(\epsilon{}_{1}+\epsilon{}_{2}+\epsilon{}_{3}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}}^{2}+R_{\bullet}^{-1+2s})C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}},\epsilon{}_{1},\epsilon{}_{2},\epsilon{}_{3})\Bigl[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}(u^{-1-\eta{}_{0}}+e^{-c_{\nu}u})E[L\varphi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\Bigr].\end{split}

One can choose
\epsilon_{1}
,
\epsilon_{2}
, and
\epsilon_{3}
small based on
C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta_{0}},\alpha{})
and
R_{\bullet}
large based on

C(\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})
, to obtain

(6.111)

\begin{split}\mathrm{Err}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]&\leq\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})\Bigl[E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{2}}(u^{-p}+e^{-c_{\nu}u})E[L\varphi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)\Bigr].\\
\end{split}

Now return to
6.45
and absorb the

E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]
term on the right of

6.111
to the left, and then apply the same analysis to the
subregion
\mathcal{R}(u_{\ast}{})\coloneqq{}\mathcal{R}\cap\{u\leq u_{\ast}{}\}
for
u_{\ast}{}\in[u_{1},u_{2}]
to obtain

(6.112)

\begin{split}E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{2}(u_{\ast}{})]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(u_{\ast}{})]&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})\Bigl[E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u_{1},v_{2})+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]\\
&\qquad+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+\int_{u_{1}}^{u_{\ast}{}}(u^{-1-\eta{}_{0}}+e^{-c_{\nu}u})E[L\varphi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\Bigr],\end{split}

where we have written
\Sigma_{2}(u_{\ast}{})
for the future boundary of

\mathcal{R}(u_{\ast}{})
. Since
E[L\varphi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u_{\ast}{})]\leq E[L\varphi{},\Sigma_{2}(u_{\ast}{})]
, we can use Grönwall’s
inequality in
6.112
to obtain

(6.113)

\begin{split}\max_{u\in[u_{1},u_{\ast}{}]}E[L\varphi{},C^{\mathrm{out}}(u)]&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},p})[E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\lvert L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u_{1},v_{2})\\
&\qquad+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].\end{split}

Combine
6.113
and
6.112
and take
u_{\ast}{}=u_{2}

to conclude
6.109
.
∎

From now on, we fix
R_{\bullet}
large enough that
6.108
holds.

{lemma}
[Energy boundedness estimate on characteristic rectangles]
Let
\mathcal{R}
be a characteristic rectangle with past
\Sigma_{1}
. For any
null curve
\Sigma{}\subset\mathcal{R}
, when
s\geq\eta_{0}
is sufficiently small we have

(6.114)

E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}]\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})[E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{1}]+r\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}]].

Proof.

A null curve
\Sigma{}\subset\mathcal{R}
can be extended to a piecewise null curve

\Sigma_{2}
so that
\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma{}_{2}
are the past and future
boundaries of a rectangular region
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{R}
. Apply

6.108
for the region
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
, and take a supremum over
null curves
\Sigma{}\subset\mathcal{R}
(and hence
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{R}
).
∎

{corollary}

[Estimate for unweighted energy norm]
When
s\geq\eta_{0}
is sufficiently small, we have

(6.115)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}^{2}+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}_{\mathrm{char}}]\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})[\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},4s}^{2}].

Proof.

Apply
6.114
to a characteristic rectangle

\mathcal{R}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
whose past
\Sigma_{1}
is
contained in the past of
\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
. Since
\Sigma_{1}
and

p_{\mathrm{fut}}
are contained in the data surface
C^{\text{in}}\cup C^{\text{out}}
, the energy
E[\psi{},\Sigma_{1}]
and the value

r\psi{}^{2}(p_{\mathrm{fut}})
are controlled by data (see
6.12
). Take
a supremum over
\mathcal{R}\subset\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
.
∎

6.6.
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate

We now adapt the work of Dafermos–Rodnianski
[
12
]
to derive an

r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate. Recall the weighted energy quantity
E_{p}

defined in
section

6.2.2
.

{lemma}
[Preliminary estimate for the quantity
E_{p}
]
Suppose that
\lim_{v\to\infty}r\psi^{2}(u,v)=0
for each
u\geq 1
. Then for any
p\geq 0
and
R\geq R_{0}+1
and
\tau{}\geq 1
, we have

(6.116)

E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{})\leq\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}],

and for
1\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
we have

(6.117)

\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{})\leq\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\Bigl(\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\Bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})],

Proof.

Introduce the following energy quantity that is localized to the region
\{r\leq R\}
:

(6.118)

\begin{split}E_{\leq R}^{\circ}[\psi{}](\tau{})&\coloneqq{}\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\text{in}}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{R-1\leq r\leq R\}}\lambda{}r\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&=E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}\cap\{r\geq R\}]+\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{R-1\leq r\leq R\}}\lambda{}r\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\end{split}

Observe that the desired
6.116
and
6.117
are a consequence of the
following three estimates:

(6.119)

\displaystyle E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)E_{\leq R}^{\circ}(\tau{})

(6.120)

\displaystyle E_{\leq R}^{\circ}(\tau{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})[E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}\cap\{r\geq R\}]+\limsup_{v\to\infty}r\psi^{2}|_{u=\tau{}}(v))]

(6.121)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}\cap\{r\leq R\}]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R)E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})].

We now prove
6.119
,
6.120
and
6.121
one by one.

Step 1: Proof of
6.119
.
By the definitions of
E_{p}
and
E_{\leq R}^{\circ}
, it is enough to show that

(6.122)

\displaystyle\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v_{R_{0}}(\tau{}))

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)E_{\leq R}^{\circ}[\psi{}](\tau{}).

To prove
6.122
, we start with the following pointwise estimate on

\Sigma_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}\subset\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R\}
:

(6.123)

\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\leq R^{p}[\lambda^{2}\psi^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{2}/\lambda{}}{r^{2}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}]\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)\bigl[\lambda{}r\psi{}^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\bigr].

Integrate
6.123
over
v\in[v_{R_{0}}(\tau{}),v_{R}(\tau{})]
and add

R_{0}\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v_{R_{0}}(\tau{}))
to both sides to get

(6.124)

\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{rp-in-bound-dv}\text{{)}}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)\Bigl[\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\lambda{}r\psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+R_{0}\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v_{R_{0}}(\tau{}))+\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\Bigr].

For any
R^{\prime}\in[R-1,R]
, we can control the first two terms on the right side of

6.124
using Hardy’s inequality (
6.8
with

a=0
) and arrive at

(6.125)

\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{rp-in-bound-dv}\text{{)}}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},R,p)\Bigl[R^{\prime}\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v_{R^{\prime}}(\tau{}))+\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\Bigr].

Averaging over
R^{\prime}\in[R-1,R]
yields
6.122
.

Step 2: Proof of
6.120
.
It is enough to prove
6.120

with the left side replaced by
\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{R-1\leq r\leq R\}}\lambda{}r\psi^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v
; this estimate is a consequence of Hardy’s
inequality (
6.8
with
a=0
in the limit
v_{2}\to\infty
).

Step 3: Proof of
6.121
.
Recall from
2.18
that
\tau{}
is a
function of
v
in
\{r\leq R_{0}\}
(with
r(\tau{},v)=R_{0}
) and a
function of
u
in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
(with
\tau{}=u
). Thus

6.121
follows from changing variables with the following
computation,

(6.126)

\mathchoice{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}}{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}/\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}/\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}(u,v)=\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}(u_{R_{0}}(v),v)\quad\text{in }\{r\leq R_{0}\},\qquad\mathchoice{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u}}{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}/\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\tau{}/\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}u}(u,v)=1\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R_{0}\}.

∎

{lemma}

Let
p\in\mathbf{R}
. We have

(6.127)

\begin{split}&\partial{}_{u}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\Bigr)+\partial{}_{v}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}(r\psi{})^{2}\Bigr)\\
&\qquad+\Bigl(p(-\nu{})-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r}(-\gamma{})\Bigr)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}+(3-p)r^{p-4}r^{2}(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)(r\psi{})^{2}\\
&=2\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p+1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p+1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p+1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p+1}/\lambda{}}\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})\kappa{}(-\nu{})\Box{}\psi{}+r^{p-3}\partial{}_{v}\Bigl(r^{2}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{\lambda{}}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/\lambda{}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/\lambda{}}\Bigr)\psi{}^{2}.\end{split}

Proof.

We refer to
[
32
, Lem. 8.52]
for details of the computation.
∎

{lemma}

[
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate]
Suppose that
\lim_{v\to\infty}r\psi^{2}(u,v)=0
for each
u\geq 1
. Then for
1\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
and
p\in(0,3)
, we have

(6.128)

\begin{split}&E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{}_{2})+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p-1}[\psi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0},p)\Bigl[E_{p}[\psi{}](\tau{}_{1})+\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})}r^{p+3}\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]+E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{2}}]\Bigr].\end{split}

Proof.

Introduce the notation
\Psi{}\coloneqq{}r\psi{}
. By
6.117
, it is enough to show
that for
R=C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0},p)
, we have

(6.129)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Gamma_{\tau_{2}}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}\Psi)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\Bigl(\int_{\Gamma_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}\cap\{r\geq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi)^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\Bigr)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\leq\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{rp-estimate-equation}\text{{)}}.\end{split}

To show
6.129
, let
\chi{}_{R}(r)
be a smooth non-decreasing function
such that
\chi_{R}\equiv 1
on
\{r\geq R\}
and
\chi_{R}\equiv 0
on

\{r\leq R/2\}
. To begin, let
R\geq 3R_{0}
. Define

(6.130)

\begin{split}\text{(I)}&\coloneqq{}\chi{}_{R}(r)\partial{}_{u}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\Bigr)+\chi{}_{R}(r)\partial{}_{v}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}\Psi{}^{2}\Bigr),\\
\text{(II)}&\coloneqq{}\chi{}_{R}(r)\Bigl[p(-\nu{})-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r}(-\gamma{})\Bigr]\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}+\chi{}_{R}(r)2(3-p)r^{p-4}(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)\lambda{}(-\gamma{})\Psi{}^{2},\\
\text{(III)}&\coloneqq{}\chi{}_{R}(r)\Bigl[2\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p+1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p+1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p+1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p+1}/\lambda{}}\partial{}_{v}\Psi{}r\Box{}\psi{}+r^{p-3}\partial{}_{v}\Bigl(r^{2}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{\lambda{}}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/\lambda{}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/\lambda{}}\Bigr)\psi{}^{2}\Bigr],\end{split}

so that the result of multiplying the statement of
6.127
by
\chi_{R}(r)
reads
\text{(I)}+\text{(II)}=\text{(III)}
.
By
4.13
,
4.12
and
4.14
, for
R\geq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},p,\varpi{}_{i},r_{\mathrm{min}})
and
c=c(c_{\mathcal{H}})
, the
square bracketed term in
(I)
is positive, and we have

(6.131)

\text{(II)}\geq\chi_{R}(r)cp\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}+\chi_{R}(r)c(3-p)r^{p-4}\Psi{}^{2}.

Since
p\in(0,3)
, the right side of
6.131
is positive. Since

(6.132)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\Big\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Bigl(r^{2}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{\lambda{}}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/\lambda{}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/\lambda{}}\Bigr)\Big\rvert=\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\Big\lvert\partial{}_{v}(2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)(-\gamma{}))\Big\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0})r^{-2},

we have

(6.133)

\begin{split}\lvert(\text{III})\rvert&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{rp-lower-bound-II}\text{{)}}^{1/2}\cdot\bigl(r^{p+3}\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}+r^{-1}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0})\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{rp-lower-bound-II}\text{{)}}\ \\
&\leq\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\text{(II)}+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0})r^{p+3}\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert^{2}.\end{split}

Integrate by parts, using
R\geq 3R_{0}
to note that
\Supp\chi{}_{R}
is disjoint
from
\Gamma_{\tau}^{\text{in}}
:

(6.134)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\text{(I)}&=\int_{\Gamma_{\tau_{2}}^{\mathrm{out}}}\chi_{R}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v-\int_{\Gamma_{\tau_{1}}^{\mathrm{out}}}\chi_{R}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\qquad-\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\chi_{R}^{\prime}(r)\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}\Psi{}^{2}\Bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v.\end{split}

Integrating the identity
\text{(I)}+\text{(II)}=\text{(III)}
and using

6.131
and
6.133
gives

(6.135)

\begin{split}&\int_{\Gamma_{\tau_{2}}^{\mathrm{out}}}\chi_{R}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Gamma_{\tau}^{\mathrm{out}}}\chi_{R}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\Gamma_{\tau_{1}}^{\mathrm{out}}}\chi_{R}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})}\chi_{R}^{\prime}(r)\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}\Psi{}^{2}\Bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\qquad+\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})}\chi{}_{R}(r)r^{p+3}\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\lesssim\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{rp-estimate-equation}\text{{)}}+\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})}\chi{}_{R}^{\prime}(r)\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}\mathchoice{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{\frac{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)}{r}}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}{(-\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)/r}\Psi{}^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v,\end{split}

where the implied constant is
C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0},p)
. Since

\chi{}^{\prime}_{R}
is supported in a finite-
r
region, the last term on the right is
controlled by
E_{\text{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]
. For the remaining
parts of
E_{p}[\psi{}]
on the left of
6.128
, we have

(6.136)

\int_{\Gamma_{\tau_{2}}^{\text{in}}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}\int_{\Gamma_{\tau}^{\text{in}}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\varphi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{R_{0}}E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{2}}]+E_{\text{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})].

An argument using Hardy’s inequality allows us to estimate

(6.137)

\int_{\Gamma{}_{\tau{}}^{\text{out}}\cap\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}{r^{p}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\int_{\Gamma{}_{\tau{}}^{\text{out}}\cap\{R_{0}\leq r\leq R\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{p-1}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}{r^{p-1}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}\Psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\lesssim_{R,\mathfrak{b}_{0}}E[\psi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}]+E_{\text{bulk}}[\psi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})].

Add
6.135
,
6.136
and
6.137
to complete the proof.
∎

6.7.
Boundedness of the weighted energy
\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},p}

In this section, we complete the proof of

section

6
by controlling the
p
-weighted energy (for
p
close to

2
) by initial data, as well as geometric quantities, unweighted pointwise
norms, and lower order weighted pointwise norms. To this end, we first formulate
the pigeonhole argument of
[
12
]
by which one obtains pointwise decay
from
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimates.

{lemma}
[Pigeonhole argument]
Suppose
F_{p}:[1,\infty)\to\mathbf{R}_{\geq 0}
is a family of non-negative functions
defined for
p\in\mathbf{R}
such that

(1)

F_{p}(\tau{})\lesssim_{p,p^{\prime}}F_{p^{\prime}}(\tau{})
whenever
p\leq p^{\prime}
,

(2)

and there are
q\geq 0
,
\epsilon{}>0
, and
A\geq 0
such that for any
p\in(0,2-\epsilon{}]
and
1\leq\tau_{1}\leq\tau_{2}
, we have

(6.138)

F_{p}(\tau{}_{2})+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}F_{p-1}(\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\lesssim_{p}F_{p}(\tau{}_{1})+A\tau{}_{1}^{p-q}.

Then

(6.139)

\displaystyle F_{2-\epsilon{}}(\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim F_{2-\epsilon{}}(1)+A,

(6.140)

\displaystyle F_{1-\epsilon{}}(\tau{})

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau{}^{-1}(F_{2-\epsilon{}}(1)+A\max(1,\tau{}^{2-\epsilon{}-q}),

(6.141)

\displaystyle\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau}{}F_{-\epsilon{}}(\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau{}^{-1}((F_{2-\epsilon{}}(1)+A\max(1,\tau{}^{2-\epsilon{}-q}))

for
\tau{}\geq 1
, with implicit constants depending on
\epsilon{}
.

Proof.

We do not track the dependence of implicit constants on
\epsilon{}
(that arises from
the use of
6.138
for fixed values of
p
that depend on
\epsilon{}
).

Step 1: Proof of
6.139
and proof of

6.140
and
6.141
for
\tau{}\in[1,2]
.
First,

6.139
follows from
6.138
for
p=2-\epsilon
and

\tau_{1}=1
(since the
F_{p}
are non-negative). Next, suppose
1\leq\tau{}\leq 2
. Start with
6.138
for
p=1-\epsilon{}
over the
interval
[1,\tau{}]
, and then use the consequence
F_{1-\epsilon{}}(1)\lesssim F_{2-\epsilon{}}(1)
of the monotonicity assumption (1) to get

(6.142)

F_{1-\epsilon{}}(\tau{})+\int_{1}^{\tau{}}F_{-\epsilon{}}(\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}\lesssim F_{1-\epsilon{}}(1)+A\lesssim F_{2-\epsilon{}}(1)+A.

This implies
6.140
and
6.141
when
\tau{}\in[1,2]
. We can
therefore suppose for the rest of the proof that
\tau{}\geq 2
.

Step 2: Proof of
6.140
for
\tau{}\geq 2
.
The estimate

6.138
for
p=2-\epsilon{}
and the non-negativity of the
F_{p}
give

(6.143)

\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau{}}F_{1-\epsilon{}}(\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}\lesssim F_{2-\epsilon{}}(\tau{}/2)+A\tau{}^{2-\epsilon{}-q}.

There is
\overline{\tau{}}\in[\tau{}/2,\tau{}]
at which
F_{1-\epsilon{}}
is bounded by its
average value over the interval (“the pigeonhole principle”). Combine this
observation with
6.143
and
6.139
to
arrive at

(6.144)

F_{1-\epsilon{}}(\overline{\tau{}})\leq 2\tau{}^{-1}\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau}{}F_{1-\epsilon{}}(\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}\lesssim\tau{}^{-1}(F_{2-\epsilon{}}(1)+A\max(1,\tau{}^{2-\epsilon{}-q})).

Conclude
6.140
by substituting
6.144
into

6.138
with
p=1-\epsilon{}
over the interval
[\overline{\tau{}},\tau{}]
.

Step 3: Proof of
6.141
for
\tau{}\geq 2
.
Start with

6.138
for
p=1-\epsilon
over the interval

[\tau{}/2,\tau{}]
, and then use the just established
6.140

and the non-negativity of
F_{p}
.
∎

{lemma}

[Estimate for weighted energy norm]
There are explicit constants
C_{\alpha}{}
depending only on
\alpha{}
such that for
s\geq\eta_{0}
sufficiently small (depending on a numerical constant), we have

(6.145)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},2-\eta{}_{0}-C_{\alpha}{}s}\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}},s)\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}.

Proof.

We use the notation
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})\coloneqq{}\bigcup_{\tau_{1}\leq\tau{}\leq\tau_{2}}\Sigma_{\tau}{}
. Write
A_{\alpha{},s}\coloneqq{}C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}},s)
.

Step 1:
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate and energy boundedness for the
foliation
\Sigma_{\tau}{}
.
Let
1\leq\tau_{1}<\tau_{2}
. In this step we show that for
p\in(0,2-4B_{\alpha}{}s-\eta_{0}]
, we have

(6.146)

\displaystyle E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{2}}]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]

\displaystyle\leq C(A_{\alpha,s},s)[E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{1}}]+E_{4B_{\alpha}{}s}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})],

(6.147)

\displaystyle E_{p}[L\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2})+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p-1}[L\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}

\displaystyle\leq C(A_{\alpha,s},p,s)[E_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})+E_{p+4B_{\alpha}{}s}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})],

where
B_{\alpha}{}=\#{}\{\beta{}:0\leq\beta{}<\alpha{}\}
is the number of multi-indices less than

\alpha{}
. These follow from Steps 2abc below (see

6.148
,
6.149
and
6.152
).

Step 1a: Energy boundedness.
We begin by showing that

(6.148)

\begin{split}E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{2}}]+E_{\mathrm{bulk}}[L\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]&\leq C(A_{\alpha,s})[E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{1}}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]].\end{split}

In fact, this is a consequence of
6.108
applied to the region

\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})
. The limiting boundary terms on

\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{I}
can be controlled by another application of

6.108
to the region
\mathcal{R}(\tau_{1},\tau_{2})
. We can
neglect the
p_{\mathrm{fut}}
term because
\Sigma_{\tau{}_{1}}
extends to

v=\infty
and we can assume
\limsup_{v\to\infty}r\lvert\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau_{1},v)=0
by including
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0}

in the constant on the right (since the limit supremum vanishes if

\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0}
is finite).

Step 1b: Controlling the error terms in the
r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate.

Let
p\in(0,2-4s-\eta_{0}]
. We show that

(6.149)

E_{p}[L\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2})+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p-1}[L\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\leq C(A_{\alpha,s},p)[E_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})+E_{p+4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]].

Since
p\leq 2-2s-\eta_{0}
,
2.70
implies

(6.150)

\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})}r^{p+3}\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})\iint_{\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})}r^{p-1+2s}[\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert^{2}]\kappa{}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})[E_{\text{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]+E_{p+2s,\text{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]].\end{split}

Substitute
6.148
and
6.150
into the

r^{p}
-weighted energy estimate
6.128
(noting as in Step 1a
that we can assume
\limsup_{v\to\infty}r\lvert\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v)=0
for all
\tau{}\geq 1
by including

\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0}
in the constant on the right) to obtain

(6.151)

\begin{split}E_{p}[L\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2})+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p-1}[L\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}&\leq C(A_{\alpha,s},p)\bigl[E_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})+E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau{}_{1}}]\\
&\qquad+E_{\max(p+2s,4s),\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]\bigr].\end{split}

To complete the proof, use
\max(p+2s,4s)\leq p+4s
and control
E[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma_{\tau_{1}}]
by
E_{p}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})
using

6.15
.

Step 1c: Estimate for
r^{p}
-weighted bulk term.
We claim that for
\tau{}\geq 1

and
p\in(0,2-4B_{\alpha}{}s-\eta{}_{0}]
, we have

(6.152)

E_{p,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{},\infty)]\leq C(A_{\alpha,s},p)[E_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})+E_{p+4B_{\alpha}{}s}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})],

where
B_{\alpha}{}=\#{}\{\beta{}:0\leq\beta{}<\alpha{}\}
is the number of multi-indices smaller than
\alpha{}
.
Indeed, Step 1b (see
6.149
) implies

(6.153)

\begin{split}E_{p,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p-1}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\\
&\leq C(A_{\alpha,s})[E_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})+E_{p+4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{}_{1},\tau{}_{2})]].\end{split}

An induction argument establishes
6.152
.

Step 2: Interpolation.
Observe that for any
\tau{}\geq 1
and
\epsilon{}\geq 0
and
q\geq-\epsilon{}
, we have

(6.154)

\begin{split}E_{q}[\psi{}](\tau{})&\leq\int_{\Sigma_{\tau}^{\text{in}}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial_{u}\psi{})^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u+r\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}(\tau{},v_{R_{0}}(\tau{}))\\
&\qquad+\tau{}^{q+\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\text{out}}\cap\{r\leq\tau{}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{-\epsilon}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{-\epsilon}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{-\epsilon}/\lambda{}}{r^{-\epsilon}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v+\tau^{q-1+\epsilon}\int_{\Sigma{}_{\tau}^{\text{out}}\cap\{r\geq\tau{}\}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{1-\epsilon}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{1-\epsilon}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{1-\epsilon}/\lambda{}}{r^{1-\epsilon}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\\
&\leq\tau{}^{q+\epsilon}E_{-\epsilon}[\psi{}](\tau{})+\tau{}^{q-1+\epsilon}E_{1-\epsilon}[\psi{}](\tau{}).\end{split}

We have bounded the first two terms on the right in the first line by

E_{-\epsilon}[\psi{}](\tau{})
.

Step 3: Energy decay through the foliation
\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}
.
We will prove inductively that for
p\in(0,2-4B_{\alpha}{}s-\eta{}_{0}]
we have

(6.155)

E_{p}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})\leq\tau^{p-q_{\alpha}{}}C(A_{\alpha{},s},p)\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2},

where
q_{\alpha}{}=2-\eta{}_{0}-4\tilde{B}_{\alpha}{}s
for
\tilde{B}_{\alpha}{}\coloneqq{}\sum_{\beta{}\leq\alpha{}}B_{\beta}{}
.

We first show that
6.155
holds for
\alpha{}=0
with

q_{0}=2-\eta{}_{0}
. By
6.147
, we have

(6.156)

E_{p}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{2})+\int_{\tau{}_{1}}^{\tau{}_{2}}E_{p-1}[\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\leq C(A_{0,s},p)E_{p}[\varphi{}](\tau{}_{1})

for
p\in(0,2-\eta{}_{0}]
and
1\leq\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}_{2}
. Now
section

6.7
implies
that

(6.157)

E_{1-\eta{}_{0}}[\varphi{}](\tau{})+\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau}{}E_{-\eta{}_{0}}[\varphi{}](\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}\lesssim\tau{}^{-1}E_{2-\eta{}_{0}}[\varphi{}](1)\leq\tau{}^{-1}C(A_{0,s})\mathcal{D}_{0}^{2},\\

where we used
6.16
to control
E_{2-\eta_{0}}[\varphi{}](1)
by
initial data. Interpolate using
6.154
to obtain

(6.158)

E_{p}[\varphi{}](\tau{})\leq\tau{}^{p+\eta{}_{0}}E_{-\eta{}_{0}}[\varphi{}](\tau{})+\tau{}^{p-1+\eta{}_{0}}E_{1-\eta{}_{0}}[\varphi{}](\tau{}).

A pigeonhole argument applies to
6.157
implies that there is
\overline{\tau{}}\in[\tau{}/2,\tau{}]
such that

(6.159)

E_{-\eta_{0}}[\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})\leq\mathchoice{\frac{2}{\tau{}}}{\frac{2}{\tau{}}}{2/\tau{}}{2/\tau{}}\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau}{}E_{-\eta_{0}}[\varphi{}](\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}\leq\tau^{-2}C(A_{0,s})\mathcal{D}_{0}^{2}.

Substitute
6.159
and
6.157
into
6.158
to get

(6.160)

E_{p}[\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})\leq\tau{}^{p-2+\eta{}_{0}}C(A_{0,s})\mathcal{D}_{0}^{2}.

Use
6.160
and
6.156
over the interval

[\overline{\tau{}},\tau{}]
to obtain

(6.161)

E_{p}[\varphi{}](\tau{})\leq C(A_{0,s},p)E_{p}[\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})\leq\tau{}^{p-2+\eta{}_{0}}C(A_{0,s},p)\mathcal{D}_{0}^{2}.

Now we show the inductive step. Suppose
6.155
holds
for multi-indices
<\alpha{}
. Then
6.147
and the induction
hypothesis give

(6.162)

E_{p}[L\varphi{}](\tau_{2})+\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}E_{p-1}[L\varphi{}](\tau{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s},p)[E_{p}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau_{1})+\tau_{1}^{p+4B_{\alpha}{}s-q_{<\alpha{}}}A_{\alpha{},s}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}]

for
1\leq\tau{}_{1}\leq\tau{}_{2}
. Sum over
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
and use
section

6.7
to get

(6.163)

\begin{split}E_{1-\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})+\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau}{}E_{-\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}&\lesssim\tau^{1-\eta{}_{0}-q_{<\alpha{}}+4B_{\alpha}{}s}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2},\\
\end{split}

We have used
6.16
to control
E_{2-\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](1)
.
By a pigeonhole argument, there is
\overline{\tau{}}\in[\tau{}/2,\tau{}]
such that

(6.164)

E_{-\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})\leq\mathchoice{\frac{2}{\tau{}}}{\frac{2}{\tau{}}}{2/\tau{}}{2/\tau{}}\int_{\tau{}/2}^{\tau}{}E_{-\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\tau{}^{\prime}\lesssim\tau^{-\eta{}_{0}-q_{<\alpha{}}+4B_{\alpha}{}s}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}.

Interpolate using
6.154
and then use

6.163
and
6.164
to get

(6.165)

\begin{split}E_{p}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})&\leq\tau^{p+\eta_{0}}E_{-\eta_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})+\tau^{p-1+\eta_{0}}E_{1-\eta_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\overline{\tau{}})\leq\tau^{p-q_{<\alpha{}}+4B_{\alpha}{}s}C(A_{\alpha{},s},p)\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}.\end{split}

Apply
6.162
over the interval
[\overline{\tau{}},\tau{}]
to obtain

(6.166)

\begin{split}E_{p}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})&\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s},p)[E_{p}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\overline{\tau})+\overline{\tau}^{p+4B_{\alpha}{}s-q_{<\alpha{}}}A_{\alpha{},s}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}]\leq\tau{}^{p-(q_{<\alpha{}}-4B_{\alpha}{}s)}C(A_{\alpha{},s},p)\mathcal{D}_{\alpha{}}^{2}.\end{split}

This completes the inductive proof of
6.155
.

Step 4: Completing the proof of
6.145
.
Write
p_{\text{max}}\coloneqq{}2-\eta_{0}-4(B_{\alpha}{}+1)s-4\tilde{B}_{\alpha}{}s
, so that it is enough to show that

(6.167)

\mathcal{E}_{p_{\text{max}}}^{2}\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2},

For
\tau{}\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{S}_{\tau{}}
be the collection of
null curves whose past endpoint lies in
\Sigma_{\tau}
. Then

(6.168)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},p}^{2}\leq\sup_{\tau{}\geq 1}\sup_{\Sigma{}\in\mathcal{S}_{\tau}{}}\tau{}^{p}E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}]+\sup_{\tau{}\geq 1}\tau{}^{p-1-\eta{}_{0}}E_{1+\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}]+E_{p,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(1,\infty)].

Fix a null curve
\Sigma{}\in\mathcal{S}_{\tau}{}
, so that
\Sigma{}\subset\mathcal{R}(\tau{},\infty)
. Apply
the energy boundedness statement of
6.114
to
obtain

(6.169)

E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}]\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s})[E[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\Sigma{}_{\tau}{}]+E_{4s,\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{},\infty)]].

The
p_{\text{fut}}
term vanishes because
r\to\infty
along
\Sigma_{\tau}{}
and the
pointwise norm
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0}
appears on the right. By

6.15
,
6.152
and
6.155
, we
have

(6.170)

\begin{split}E[L\varphi{},\Sigma{}]&\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s})[E_{4s}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})+E_{4(B_{\alpha}{}+1)s}[\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})]\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s})E_{4(B_{\alpha}{}+1)s}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})\\
&\leq\tau^{-2+\eta{}_{0}+4(B_{\alpha}{}+1)s+4\tilde{B}_{\alpha}{}s}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}=\tau^{-p_{\text{max}}}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}\end{split}

By
6.155
, we have

(6.171)

E_{1+\eta{}_{0}}[\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})\leq\tau^{1+\eta{}_{0}-2+\eta{}_{0}+4\tilde{B}_{\alpha}{}s}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}\leq\tau^{1+\eta{}_{0}-p_{\text{max}}}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}.

Finally,
6.152
and
6.155
gives

(6.172)

E_{p_{\text{max}},\mathrm{bulk}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},\mathcal{R}(\tau{},\infty)]\leq C(A_{\alpha,s})E_{p_{\text{max}}+4B_{\alpha}{}s}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}](\tau{})\leq\tau^{-4(B_{\alpha}{}+1)s}C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C(A_{\alpha{},s})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}.

Combine
6.168
,
6.170
,
6.171
and
6.172
to obtain the desired

6.167
.
∎

7.
Pointwise estimates

In
section

7.1
, we estimate weighted
L^{\infty}
-norms similar to

P_{p}[\psi{}]
in terms of
\Box{}\psi{}
, the initial data norm
D[\psi{}]
,
and the energy norms
\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]
and
\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}]
. In

section

7.2
, we specialize the estimates in

section

7.1
to functions of the form
\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}
. This requires an understanding of
\Box{}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}
, as obtained in
section

2.8.3
, and culminates in
a proof of the following result:

{proposition}
[Estimate for pointwise norm]
Let
\alpha{}\geq 0
and
p\geq 0
. When
s>0
is sufficiently small depending on

\alpha{}
, we have

(7.1)

\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},p}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},p}).

Proof.

Combine
7.6
,
7.26
,
7.18
and
7.9
.
∎

7.1.
Estimates for general functions

{lemma}

We have

(7.2)

\lVert r^{1/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}].

Proof.

We use the fundamental theorem of calculus along a constant-
v
curve from

(u,v)
to
(1,v)\in C^{\text{in}}
, and then apply Cauchy-Schwarz, the
assumption
\min_{v\in[1,\infty)}r(1,v)\geq 1
, the change of variables

\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r=(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u
, and the monotonicity
r(u,v)\leq r(1,v)
. This gives

(7.3)

\begin{split}\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v)&\leq\lvert\psi{}\rvert(1,v)+\int_{1}^{u}\lvert\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\leq r^{-1}(1,v)D[\psi{}]\\
&\qquad+\Bigl(\int_{1}^{u}r^{-2}(u^{\prime},v)(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\int_{1}^{u}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\\
&\leq r^{-1/2}(1,v)\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\Bigl(\int_{r(u,v)}^{r(1,v)}r^{-2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr)^{1/2}\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]\leq r^{-1/2}(u,v)(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]).\end{split}

∎

{lemma}

Let
s>1
. Then

(7.4)

\lVert r\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},s)(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]+\lVert r^{s}\partial_{v}(r\psi{})\rVert_{L^{\infty}}).

Proof.

Fix
R\geq R_{0}
. We integrate
\partial_{v}(r\psi{})
in the
v
-direction from

(u,v_{R}(u))\in\{r=R\}
to
(u,v)
. To control the boundary term at

\{r=R\}
, we use
7.2
, and we estimate the integral using the
hypothesis, a change of variables, and the fact that
\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lambda{}^{-1}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
. Writing
A\coloneqq{}\lVert r^{s}\partial_{v}(r\psi{})\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
, we get

(7.5)

\begin{split}r\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v)&\leq R\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v_{R}(u))+\int_{v_{R}(u)}^{v}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq R^{1/2}(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}])+A\int_{v_{R}(u)}^{v}r^{-s}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq R^{1/2}(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}])+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})A\int_{R}^{r(u,v)}r^{-s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},s)(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]+A).\end{split}

∎

{lemma}

Let
p\geq 0
. If
\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]<\infty
, then

(7.6)

\lVert r^{1/2}\tau{}^{p/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq 3\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}].

Proof.

We first consider the case where
r(u,v)\geq R_{0}
, and then the case where

r(u,v)\leq R_{0}
. We can assume that
\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]<\infty
, as
otherwise
7.6
is trivial since the right side is infinite.

Step 1:
r(u,v)\geq R_{0}
.
Suppose that
r(u,v)\geq R_{0}
. Since
r(u,v^{\prime})\to\infty
as

v^{\prime}\to\infty
,
7.2
implies that
\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\to 0
as

v^{\prime}\to\infty
. We can therefore use the fundamental theorem of calculus in

v
from
(u,v)
to
(u,\infty)
, then use Cauchy-Schwarz and the lower
bound
1-\mu{}\geq 1/2
in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
from
4.12
to get

(7.7)

\begin{split}\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v)&\leq\int_{v}^{\infty}\lvert\partial{}_{v}\psi{}\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq\Bigl(\int_{v}^{\infty}r^{-2}\kappa{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\int_{v}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\Bigl(\int_{r(u,v)}^{\infty}r^{-2}(1-\mu{})^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr)^{1/2}\tau{}^{-p/2}\mathcal{E}_{p}\leq 2r^{-1}\tau{}^{-p/2}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}].\end{split}

Step 2:
r(u,v)\leq R_{0}
.
If
r(u,v)\leq R_{0}
, then we integrate on a
constant-
v
curve from
(u_{R_{0}}(v),v)
to
(u,v)
and use Cauchy-Schwarz
and a change of variables as before. The boundary term on
\{r=R_{0}\}
is
controlled by Step 1, and the integral decays in
\tau{}
. This gives

(7.8)

\begin{split}\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v)&\leq\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u_{R_{0}}(v),v)+\int_{u_{R_{0}}(v)}^{u}\lvert\partial{}_{u}\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\\
&\leq 2R_{0}^{-1/2}\tau{}^{-p/2}(u_{R_{0}}(v),v)\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}]+\Bigl(\int_{r(u,v)}^{R_{0}}r^{-2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\int_{u_{R_{0}}(v)}^{u}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\\
&\leq 3r^{-1/2}\tau{}^{-p/2}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}].\end{split}

In the last line we have used
\tau{}(u,v)=\tau{}(u_{R_{0}}(v),v)
for
r(u,v)\leq R_{0}
.
∎

{lemma}

Let
p\geq 1+\eta_{0}
. If
\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]<\infty
, then

(7.9)

\lVert r\tau{}^{p/2-1/2-\eta{}_{0}/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\varpi{}_{i},\eta{}_{0})\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}].

Proof.

In view of the previous lemma and
\tau{}\geq 1
, it is enough to obtain the result in

\{r\geq R_{0}\}
. As in the previous lemma, we integrate

\partial_{v}(r\psi{})
in the
v
-direction from
(u,v_{R_{0}}(u))\in\{r=R_{0}\}
to
(u,v)
. This time, we estimate the integral using the

p
-weighted energy. We get

(7.10)

\begin{split}r\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v)&\leq R_{0}\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u,v_{R_{0}}(u))+\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq R_{0}^{1/2}\tau{}^{-p/2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}]+\Bigl(\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}r^{-1-\eta{}_{0}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\int_{v_{R_{0}}(u)}^{v}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}}{\lambda{}}}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}/\lambda{}}{r^{1+\eta{}_{0}}/\lambda{}}(\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{}))^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\\
&\leq\tau{}^{-p/2}C(\varpi{}_{i})\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}]+C(\eta{}_{0})\tau{}^{-p/2+1/2+\eta{}_{0}/2}\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}].\end{split}

In passing to the last line, we used
R_{0}\leq C(\varpi{}_{i})
.
∎

{lemma}

Let
s\in{}[0,1]
. We have

(7.11)

\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})\rvert\leq r^{-3/2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}])+\int_{1}^{u}r\kappa{}(-\nu{})\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}.

Proof.

The wave equation
2.15
implies

(7.12)

\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})=r\kappa{}(-\nu{})\Box{}\psi{}+(\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}r)\psi{}=r\kappa{}(-\nu{})\Box{}\psi{}-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\psi{}.

Integrate
7.12
in
u
to get

(7.13)

\begin{split}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})\rvert(u,v)&\leq\underbrace{\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\psi{})\rvert(1,v)}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}}+\underbrace{\int_{1}^{u}\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\lvert\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(II)}}+\int_{1}^{u}r\kappa{}(-\nu{})\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}.\end{split}

For term
(I)
, use the monotonicity
r(1,v)\geq r(u,v)
and the fact
that
r
has a global lower bound to estimate
r^{-2}(1,v)

in terms of
r^{-3/2}(u,v)
. We also use the fact that
\lambda{}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
on
C^{\mathrm{out}}
. For term
(II)
, use

\kappa{},\varpi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}
, change variables
(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u=\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r
, and use
7.2
.
This gives

(7.14)

\begin{split}\text{(I)}+\text{(II)}&\leq r^{-2}(1,v)\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\lVert r^{1/2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\int_{1}^{u}r^{-1-3/2}(-\nu{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\\
&\leq r^{-3/2}(u,v)C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]).\end{split}

Substitute
7.14
into
7.13
to get the result.
∎

{lemma}

We have

(7.15)

\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}r^{2}\lvert U\psi{}\rvert^{2}(u,v)+\int_{1}^{v}c_{\mathcal{H}}(U\psi{})^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathfrak{D}_{0}[\psi{}]^{2}+\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]^{2})+2\int_{1}^{v}r^{2}\kappa{}U\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}.

Proof.

It is enough to show the pointwise inequality

(7.16)

\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\partial{}_{v}(rU\psi{})^{2}+c_{\mathcal{H}}(U\psi{})^{2}\leq 2r^{2}\kappa{}U\psi{}\Box{}\psi{}+C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\psi{})^{2}.

Indeed,
7.15
follows immediately from
7.16
after recalling
the definitions of
[\psi{}]
and
\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]
.

We now show
7.16
. Apply the redshift inequality
\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r\geq c_{\mathcal{H}}>0
(see
4.1
) to the wave equation to compute

(7.17)

\partial{}_{v}(rU\psi{})=r\kappa{}\Box{}\psi{}+\partial{}_{v}\psi{}-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}rU\psi{}\leq r\kappa{}\Box{}\psi{}+\partial{}_{v}\psi{}-2c_{\mathcal{H}}\kappa{}r^{-1}U\psi{}.

To conclude
7.16
, multiply both sides by
rU\psi{}
, use Young’s
inequality for the term
r\partial{}_{v}\psi{}U\psi{}
, and apply
\kappa{}^{-1},c_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
.
∎

7.2.
Controlling the pointwise norm
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},p}

{lemma}

For
\alpha{}\geq 0
, let
B_{\alpha}{}\coloneqq{}\#{}\{\beta{}:0\leq\beta{}<\alpha{}\}
be the number of
multi-indices smaller than
\alpha{}
. For
s>0
small enough depending on

\alpha{}
, we have

(7.18)

\lVert r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert r^{2}\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}+\lVert r^{3/2-B_{\alpha}{}s}\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}).

Proof.

It is enough to establish that

(7.19)

\lVert r^{3/2-B_{\alpha{}}s}\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}).

Indeed, the estimate for
\lVert r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}
follows from
7.4
and

7.19
. Moreover, the estimate for

\lVert r^{2}\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rVert
follows from

(7.20)

r^{2}\partial_{v}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\leq r\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{})-\lambda{}r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}

and
\lambda{}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
. Note that here we use the smallness of
s
with
respect to
\alpha{}
to conclude that
3/2-B_{\alpha}{}s>1
.

We prove
7.19
by induction on
\alpha{}
. When
\alpha{}=0
,

7.19
is an immediate consequence of
7.11

applied to
\varphi{}
. Now suppose that
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
and

7.19
holds for multi-indices
<\alpha{}
with the
exponent
B_{\alpha}{}=B_{<\alpha{}}+1
. We want to show that

7.19
holds for the multi-index
\alpha{}
.

Fix a point
(u,v)
at which we want to estimate
\lvert\partial_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert
. By

7.11
and
7.2
, we have

(7.21)

\lvert\partial{}_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert\leq r^{-3/2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{})+\int_{1}^{u}r\kappa{}(-\nu{})\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}.

The estimate for
\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert
in
2.72
and the
inequality
\kappa{}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
gives

(7.22)

\begin{split}\int_{1}^{u}r\kappa{}(-\nu{})\lvert\Box{}\psi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})\underbrace{\int_{1}^{u}r^{-1+s}\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert(-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}}_{\text{:=(I)}}\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})\underbrace{\int_{1}^{u}r^{-1+s}\bigl[\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert\bigr](-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}}_{\text{:=(II)}}\\
\end{split}

Since
r(u,v)\leq r(u^{\prime},v)
for all
u^{\prime}\in[1,u]
, we have

(7.23)

\text{(I)}\leq\Bigl(\int_{r(u,v)}^{r(1,v)}r^{-4+2s}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\int_{1}^{u}r^{2}(U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{})^{2}(-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\Bigr)^{1/2}\leq r^{-3/2+s}(u,v)\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}.

We now turn to term
(II)
. Use
\lvert\partial_{v}\psi{}\rvert\leq r^{-1}\lvert\partial_{v}(r\psi{})\rvert+r^{-1}\lambda{}\lvert\psi{}\rvert
, the induction hypothesis, and
7.2
to get

(7.24)

\begin{split}\text{(II)}&\leq\int_{1}^{u}[r^{-1+s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-2+s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert](-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\\
&\leq\int_{1}^{u}[r^{-1-3/2+(B_{<\alpha{}}+1)s}C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\mathfrak{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathfrak{E}_{\alpha}{})+r^{-2+s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert](-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\mathfrak{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{})r^{-3/2+(B_{<\alpha{}}+1)s}(u,v)+\int_{1}^{u}r^{-2+s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert(-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}.\end{split}

By
7.21
,
7.22
,
7.23
and
7.24
, we obtain

(7.25)

\begin{split}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert(u,v)&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\mathfrak{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{})r^{-3/2+(B_{<\alpha{}}+1)s}(u,v)\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})\int_{1}^{u}r^{-2+2\eta{}_{0}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(rL\varphi{})\rvert(-\nu{})(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}.\end{split}

Since
r^{-3/2+(B_{<\alpha{}}+1)}(\cdot,v)
is non-decreasing (in
u
), Grönwall’s
inequality implies
7.19
with
B_{\alpha}{}=B_{<\alpha{}}+1
, as
desired.
∎

{lemma}

Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
for some
\alpha{}\geq 0
. For
s>0
small enough depending on
\alpha{}
,
we have

(7.26)

\lVert rUL\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}}\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}).

Proof.

Step 1: Preliminary observation.
Observe that
7.18
implies that
for
s
small enough depending on
\alpha{}
, we have

(7.27)

r^{2s}\lvert\partial_{v}(r\Gamma^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert^{2}\leq r^{-2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{D}_{<\alpha{}}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{}}^{2}).

Step 2.
We prove
7.26
by induction on
\alpha{}
. When
\alpha{}=0
,

7.15
follows from
7.26
(for
\psi{}=\varphi{}
). Now
suppose inductively that
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
and
7.26
holds for
all multi-indices
<\alpha{}
. Use
7.15
(for
\psi{}=L\varphi{}
), the estimate for
UL\varphi{}\Box{}L\varphi{}
in

2.71
, an
r
-weighted Young’s inequality with
\epsilon{}
,
the fact that
\kappa{},\kappa^{-1},c_{\mathcal{H}}^{-1},r^{-1}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})
, the induction hypothesis, and
7.27
to
obtain

(7.28)

\begin{split}&r^{2}\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u,v)+\int_{1}^{v}2c_{\mathcal{H}}(UL\varphi{})^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}^{2})+\epsilon{}\int_{1}^{v}(UL\varphi{})^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\qquad+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})\int_{1}^{v}\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}r^{2s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})\rvert^{2}+r^{2s}\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\Bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}^{2})+\epsilon{}\int_{1}^{v}(UL\varphi{})^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})\int_{1}^{v}\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2+2s}(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}^{2})\Bigr]\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq\epsilon{}\int_{1}^{v}(UL\varphi{})^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s},\epsilon{})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}^{2}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}^{2}).\\
\end{split}

To conclude, absorb the first term on the right to the left by taking
\epsilon{}>0
small enough
depending on
c_{\mathcal{H}}
.
∎

8.
Estimates for differentiated geometric quantities

The goal of this section is to establish the following result:

{proposition}
[Estimates for higher order geometric quantities]
For
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
and
s>0
sufficiently small, we have

(8.1)

\displaystyle C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{0},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}),

(8.2)

\displaystyle C(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{0},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}),

(8.3)

\displaystyle C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{0},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}),

(8.4)

\displaystyle C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{0},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}).

Proof.

Equation

8.9
and

8.40
,
8.3
and
8.4
imply that for

\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
we have

(8.5)

\displaystyle C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}}),

(8.6)

\displaystyle C(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}),

(8.7)

\displaystyle C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}),

(8.8)

\displaystyle C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}).

Substitute
8.5
into
8.6
and
substitute
8.6
and
8.7
into

8.8
and induct on
\alpha{}
to obtain

8.1
,
8.2
,
8.3
and
8.4
.
∎

8.1.
Preliminary estimate for schematic geometric quantities

To estimate the geometric quantities
\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
,
\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}
,

\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}
, and
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
, it is enough to estimate the zeroth order geometric
quantities (which has been done in
section

4
) and estimate
derivatives of
\varpi{}
,
\log\kappa{}
, and
\log(-\gamma{})
.

{lemma}

For
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, we have

(8.9)

\begin{split}C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varpi{},\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{},\mathbf{1}_{\beta{}_{U}>0}r\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\log\kappa{}|_{\beta{}\leq\alpha{}},\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})),\\
C(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},v\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{},r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+U}\log\kappa{},r\tau{}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})),\\
C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varpi{},\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}r^{1-s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})),\\
C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\alpha{},\mathfrak{B}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varpi{},r^{1-s}\tau{}\mathbf{1}_{\beta{}_{U}>0}\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\log\kappa{}|_{\beta{}\leq\alpha{}}).\\
\end{split}

The proof is provided at the end of the section.

{lemma}

Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, and let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. We have

(8.10)

\displaystyle Lr

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(1)r,

(8.11)

\displaystyle Lv-\mathbf{1}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=\alpha{}_{S}}v

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0})[1+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{S}>0}(-v+u+r)],

(8.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}Lu

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0})[1+\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{S}>0}\{\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}],

(8.13)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L(-v+u+r)

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0}.

Proof.

First,
8.10
follows from the calculation
2.21
.
For the remaining estimates, we use an inductive argument.

Step 1: The case
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.
We first establish
8.11
,
8.12
and
8.13
for
the case
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.

Step 1a: Proof of
8.11
when
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.
We need to control
Uv
,
Vv
,
and
Sv-v
. A computation reveals

(8.14)

Uv=0\qquad Vv=\chi{}+(1-\chi{})\lambda{}^{-1}\qquad Sv-v=(1-\chi{})((-v+u+r)-r(1-\lambda{}^{-1})-\mathchoice{\frac{u}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{u}{\kappa{}}}{u/\kappa{}}{u/\kappa{}}\bigl(\kappa{}-(-\gamma{})\bigr)).

It follows that

(8.15)

Dv=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}\qquad Sv-v=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}[1+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}(-v+u+r)].

Step 1b: Proof of
8.12
when
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.
We need to control
Uu
,
Vu
,
and
Su
. We compute

(8.16)

Uu=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})}}{1/(-\nu{})}{1/(-\nu{})}\qquad Vu=\chi{}\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}\qquad Su=\chi{}v\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}+(1-\chi{})u

It follows that

(8.17)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}Du=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}\qquad\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}Su=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}\{\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}.

Step 1c: Proof of
8.13
when
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.
By

8.15
and
8.17
and
Ur,Vr=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(1)
we
have

(8.18)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}D(-v+u-r)=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}.

From
8.15
and the discussion that follows in Step 1a, we
can express

(8.19)

Sv=v+(1-\chi{})(-v+u+r)+\mathcal{E}=\chi{}v+(1-\chi{})r+(1-\chi{})u+\mathcal{E}

for
\mathcal{E}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}
. Together with
8.17

and
Sr=(1-\chi{})r
, we compute

(8.20)

\begin{split}S(-v+u+r)&=\mathchoice{\frac{\chi{}v}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\chi{}v}{(-\gamma{})}}{\chi{}v/(-\gamma{})}{\chi{}v/(-\gamma{})}(\kappa{}-(-\gamma{}))-\mathcal{E}.\end{split}

It follows as in Step 1a that
v(\kappa{}-(-\gamma{}))=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}
. Since

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}(-\gamma{})^{-1}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}
, we get

(8.21)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}S(-v+r+r)=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}.

We conclude from
8.18
and
8.21
that

8.13
holds when
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.

Step 2: Inductive step.
Let
\lvert\alpha_{1}\rvert,\lvert\alpha_{2}\rvert\geq 1
, and let
L_{i}\in\Gamma^{\alpha_{i}}
. Write
\alpha{}=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
. Suppose that

8.11
,
8.12
and
8.13
hold for multi-indices
\beta{}
with
1\leq\lvert\beta{}\rvert<\lvert\alpha{}\rvert
. We will show that they hold for
L_{1}L_{2}\in\Gamma^{\alpha{}}
. Recall that by definition
\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}
. Differentiating
8.11

for the multi-index
\alpha_{2}
by
L_{1}\in\Gamma^{\alpha_{1}}
and using

8.11
for multi-indices
\leq\lvert\alpha_{1}\rvert
gives

(8.22)

\displaystyle L_{1}L_{2}v-\mathbf{1}_{(\alpha{}_{2})_{S}=\lvert\alpha{}_{2}\rvert}L_{1}v

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}_{1}+\alpha{}_{2}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0})[1+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{S}>0}(-v+u+r)].

Use
8.11
for
L_{1}\in\Gamma^{\alpha_{1}}
to handle the second term on the left
and use
\mathbf{1}_{(\alpha{}_{2})_{S}=\lvert\alpha{}_{2}\rvert}\mathbf{1}_{(\alpha{}_{1})_{S}=\lvert\alpha{}_{1}\rvert}v=\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{S}=\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}
to conclude
8.11
for the multi-index
\alpha{}
. A consequence of
8.11
for the multi-index
\alpha{}
and the fact that

r^{-1}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(1)
is that

(8.23)

\begin{split}Lv&=_{\mathrm{s}}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0})\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u,v\}.\end{split}

Differentiate
8.11
for the multi-index
\alpha_{2}
by
L_{1}\in\Gamma^{\alpha_{1}}
and use
8.23
and
8.12
for
multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}_{1}
to conclude
8.12
for the multi-index
\alpha{}
.
Finally, it is clear how to prove
8.13
inductively.
∎

Proof of
8.9
.

Define

(8.24)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{0}\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{b}_{0},\quad\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{0}\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{B}_{0},\quad\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{0}\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{g}_{0},\quad\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{0}\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{G}_{0},

and for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, define the
following schematic quantities that capture the top order terms in

\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
,
\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}
,
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}
, and
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
:

(8.25)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(r^{-1}\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varpi{},\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{},\mathbf{1}_{\alpha_{U}>0}r\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{},\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})),

(8.26)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}u,v\}\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{},r\Gamma^{\alpha{}+U}\log\kappa{},\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}ru,v\}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})),

(8.27)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\{1,\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varpi{},r\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})\},

(8.28)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{}\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}\{1,\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}ru,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{U}>0}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}\}.

Step 1: Relating the original schematic geometric quantities to the ones with
tildes.
Observe that for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1

(8.29)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}&=\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{G}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}}),\\
\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}&=\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{G}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}}),\\
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}&=\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\{1,\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}<\alpha{}}\},\\
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}&=\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}\{1,\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}},\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}}\}.\end{split}

This can be seen from the definitions of the original and tilded quantities and the
consequence of the chain rule that
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}f=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log f,f,f^{-1})
for any positive
function
f
.

Step 2.
We claim that

(8.30)

\displaystyle\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}}),

(8.31)

\displaystyle\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}}),

(8.32)

\displaystyle\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}})\{1,\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\leq\alpha{}}\},

(8.33)

\displaystyle\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}})\{1,\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\leq\alpha{}}\}.

Step 2a: Proof of
8.30
and
8.32
.
This follows from
8.10
and
differentiating
\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{0}=\mathfrak{b}_{0}
(see
2.34
) in the case

\beta{}^{\prime}=0
and
\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}

(see
8.25
) in the case
\lvert\beta{}^{\prime}\rvert>0
. The proof of

8.32
is similar.

Step 2b: Proof of
8.31
and
8.33
.
Differentiate
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
from

2.36
use
8.11
,
8.12
and
8.13
to control the differentiated
weights
r
,
u
, and
v
, and use
8.30
to arrive at

(8.34)

\begin{split}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{0}=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\mathfrak{B}_{0}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\mathfrak{b}_{0},\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}})\end{split}

Differentiate
8.26
and use
8.11
,
8.12
and
8.13
to get

(8.35)

\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathcal{\widetilde{G}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{},\lvert\beta{}^{\prime}\rvert>0}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}}).

Combine
8.34
and
8.35
with
8.30
for

\beta{}^{\prime}=0
to conclude

(8.36)

\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\beta{}^{\prime}}|_{\beta{}+\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}}).

To prove
8.31
, induct on
\alpha{}
with trivial base case
\alpha{}=0
and
inductive step given by
8.36
. The proof of
8.33

is similar.

Step 3: Completing the proof.
The results of Steps 1 and 2 imply that for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, we have

(8.37)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}),\\
\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}),\\
\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\leq\alpha{}})\{1,\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{<\alpha{}}\},\\
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}_{\alpha}{}(\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\leq\alpha{}})\{1,\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\leq\alpha{}},\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\leq\alpha{}}\}.\end{split}

It is immediate from
8.25
and
8.27
that for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
we have

(8.38)

\begin{split}C(\mathfrak{\tilde{b}}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(r^{-1}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{},\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{},\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{U}>0}r\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{},\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})),\\
C(\mathfrak{\tilde{g}}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{})(1+\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}\rvert+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lvert r\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert),\\
\end{split}

Since
\eta_{0}<1/2
and
G_{\eta_{0}}\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})
, applying

4.25
to
8.26
and
8.28
implies that for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
we have

(8.39)
