\begin{split}C(\mathfrak{\tilde{B}}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0},v\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{},r\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}+U}\log\kappa{},r\tau{}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})),\\
C(\mathfrak{\tilde{G}}_{\alpha}{})&\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha}{})(1+\lvert r\tau{}\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{U}>0}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}\rvert).\end{split}

To complete the proof of
8.9
, combine

8.37
with
8.38
and
8.39
.
∎

8.2.
Estimates for
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi

{proposition}

[Estimate for
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}
]
For
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, we have

(8.40)

\max(r^{-s-\eta{}_{0}},\tau{}^{-s-\eta{}_{0}})\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}})\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}.

Proof.

Step 1: Formula for
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}
.
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
. We begin by showing that

(8.41)

\begin{split}\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-1}\bigl[r^{2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\varphi{}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\varphi{}+r^{2}\{1,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v,v/r\}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\\
&\qquad+r^{2}\{1,r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\bigr].\end{split}

Step 1a:
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.
In this step we compute
U\varpi{}
,
V\varpi{}
, and
S\varpi{}
. Use
the transport equation for
\varpi{}
in the
u
-direction (see

2.12
) to get

(8.42)

U\varpi{}=\mathfrak{b}_{0}r^{2}(U\varphi{})^{2}.

The transport equation for
\varpi{}
in the
v
-direction (see
2.12
) and the identities in

section

2.8.1
relating the
(U,\partial{}_{v})
derivatives and the

(U,V)
derivatives give

(8.43)

V\varpi{}=\mathfrak{b}_{0}[r^{2}(V\varphi{})^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}r^{2}V\varphi{}U\varphi{}+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}r^{2}(U\varphi{})^{2}].

Finally, we express
S
in terms of
U
and
V
using

2.49
, and then use
8.42
and
8.43
to compute

(8.44)

\begin{split}S\varpi{}&=\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\{\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}U\varpi{}+\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\}V\varpi{}]\\
&=\mathfrak{b}_{0}[r^{2}\{1,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}D\varphi{}D\varphi{}+r^{2}\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\}(V\varphi{})^{2}].\\
\end{split}

Combining
8.42
,
8.43
and
8.44
gives

8.41
for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
.

Step 1b:
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
.
The following consequences of
section

8.1
hold for all

\lvert\beta{}\rvert\geq 0
:

(8.45)

\begin{split}\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{\leq\beta{}-1}\{r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\},\\
\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}\{\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{\leq\beta{}-1}\{1,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v\},\\
\Gamma{}^{\beta}{}(v/r)&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{\leq\beta{}-1}\{1,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u/r,v/r\}.\end{split}

Indeed, the first two lines follow directly from
section

8.1
, and the third
line follows from the first. Differentiate
8.41
for a
multi-index
\alpha_{2}
of size
1
by
\Gamma^{\alpha_{1}}
, and then use

8.45
and the commutation formulas

2.59
and
2.58
to conclude
8.41
for the
multi-index
\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
. By induction, we have established

8.41
for all
\alpha{}\geq 1
. The reason for the
weight
v/r
in front of general derivatives of
\varpi{}
(which does not
appear when
\alpha{}=1
) is because the weight
v
appears in front of
derivatives starting with
V
, and commuting
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}
past
V

(in the third term) can produce derivatives starting with
U
, but only with
an
r
-weight that decays faster than
r^{-1}
.

Step 2: Completing the proof.
It follows from
4.25
that

(8.46)

\begin{split}\{1,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}v,v/r\}&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq v/2}v\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})\tau{},\\
\{1,r,\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}u,v\}&\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})v.\\
\end{split}

Applying
8.46
to
8.41
(and noting

\alpha{}>0
) gives

(8.47)

\begin{split}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}\rvert&\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}})[r^{2}\lvert D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{2}\tau{}\lvert D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{2}v\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}].\end{split}

To complete the proof, note that the first term of
8.47

appears in
\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}
. Control the
v
-weight on the
final term by
\tau{}+r
(see
4.25
). This produces a term in
the form of the second term and a new term
r^{3}\lvert V\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}
that is controlled by

C(R_{\bullet},\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}
. The remaining term

r^{2}\tau{}\lvert D\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}
is of the form

r^{2}\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}
, which is controlled by

r^{s+\eta{}_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}
, or by
\tau^{s+\eta_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}
.
∎

8.3.
Estimates for
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\kappa

The main result of this section is
section

8.3
, whose proof we now
outline. The gauge condition on
v
normalizes
\log\kappa{}=0
on the
curve
\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}
. In particular, derivatives of
\kappa{}

involving only
V
or
S
vanish on this curve, since in the region

\{r\leq r_{\mathcal{H}}\leq R_{\bullet}\}
, the vector fields
V
and
S
are
tangent to curves of constant
r
. We estimate
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}
by computing
U\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}
with the

U
-transport equation for
\log\kappa{}
in
8.51
and
integrating towards
\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}
in
section

8.3
. In
the case where
\alpha_{U}>0
, we can rearrange
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}=U\Gamma^{\alpha{}-U}\log\kappa{}
and use the
U
-transport equation
directly, without integrating it.

{lemma}

We have

(8.48)

\lvert S\psi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})(\tau{}\lvert U\psi{}\rvert+v\lvert V\psi{}\rvert)

and

(8.49)

\lvert V\psi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})(v^{-1}\lvert S\psi{}\rvert+\lvert U\psi{}\rvert).

Proof.

We omit the computation.
∎

{lemma}

[Estimates for
U\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}
]
Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 0
. For sufficiently small
s>0
, we have

(8.50)

\lvert UL\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[r\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert],

and if
\alpha_{V}+\alpha_{S}>0
, then

(8.51)

\lvert UL\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[rv\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-3}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert].

Proof.

Step 0: Controlling the commutator
[U,L]\psi{}
.
Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. We claim that

(8.52)

\lvert[U,L]f\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}f\rvert+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}f\rvert].

By
2.57
, we have

(8.53)

\lvert[U,L]f\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}f\rvert+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}f\rvert+r^{-1+s}\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}f\rvert].

Use
8.49
and
4.25
to control the last term by the
first two terms.

Step 1: Preliminary estimate for
\lvert UL\log\kappa{}\rvert
.
We show by induction on
\alpha{}

that

(8.54)

\lvert UL\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq r\lvert LU\varphi{}\rvert\lvert U\varphi{}\rvert+C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[r\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-3+2s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert].

When
\alpha{}=0
,
8.54
follows from the equation
U\log\kappa{}=-r(U\varphi{})^{2}
. For the inductive step, use
8.52
, the
equation for
U\kappa{}
, the estimate
\lvert\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}r\rvert\leq C_{\alpha}{}r
, and
8.50
for multi-indices
<\alpha{}
to
estimate

(8.55)

\begin{split}\lvert UL\log\kappa{}\rvert&\leq\lvert LU\log\kappa{}\rvert+\lvert[U,L]\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq\lvert L(r(U\varphi{})^{2})\rvert+C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert]\\
&\leq\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{kappa-alpha-step-1}\text{{)}}.\end{split}

Step 2: Proof of
8.50
.
Use Young’s inequality and

8.52
on the first term in
8.54
, and control
the term involving
\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}
using the
pointwise norm defined in
section

2.7
.

Step 3: Proof of
8.51
.
In Steps 3ab we show
that if
\alpha_{V}+\alpha_{S}>0
, we have

(8.56)

\lvert LU\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[v\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-2+s}v\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert V\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert].

In Step 3c we deduce
8.51
from
8.56
.

Step 3a: Proof of
8.56
when
\alpha_{V}>0
.
Use

2.62
to bring
V
to the end of
L
, then use the wave
equation
2.63
and the commutation formulas
2.59
and
2.57
to
get

(8.57)

LU\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}VU\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}(\mathfrak{b}_{0}[D\varphi{}+U^{2}\varphi{}])+\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}[U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}+V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}\varphi{}].

Step 3b: Proof of
8.56
when
\alpha_{S}>0
.
First,

8.52
implies that

(8.58)

V\Gamma^{\alpha{}-S}U\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}U\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}+r^{-2+s}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}],

Now use
2.62
to bring
S
to the front of
L
,

8.48
to rewrite
S
in terms of
U
and
V
, and

8.58
to rewrite
V\Gamma^{\alpha{}-S}U\varphi{}
,
and hence obtain

(8.59)

\begin{split}\lvert LU\varphi{}\rvert&\leq\lvert S\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}-S}U\varphi{}\rvert+C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}})\lvert D\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}})[\tau{}\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}-S}U\varphi{}\rvert+v\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}-S}U\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert D\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert]\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[v\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-2+s}v\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert V\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert].\end{split}

Step 3c: Deducing
8.51
from
8.56
.
First,

8.56
and Young’s inequality imply

(8.60)

r\lvert LU\varphi{}\rvert\lvert U\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[rv\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-3+2s}v\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r\lvert V\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}].

Combine
8.60
and
8.54
to get

(8.61)

\lvert UL\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[rv\lvert U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-3+2s}v\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r\lvert V\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert].

We have the pointwise estimates

(8.62)

r^{-3+2s}v\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\leq v/2}r^{-4+2s}\tau{}^{-1}v\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq v/2}r^{-5+2s}v\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{B}_{0})r^{-4+2s}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}})r^{-3}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2},

where we used
4.25
and
s<1/2
, and

(8.63)

r\lvert V\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0},R_{\bullet})r^{-3}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}})r^{-3}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}.

Substitute
8.62
and
8.63
into
8.61
to
obtain
8.51
.
∎

Before we integrate
8.51
towards
\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}
, we introduce an
integration lemma.

{lemma}

We have

(8.64)

\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r}{(-\nu{})}}{r/(-\nu{})}{r/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\leq v^{-1}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})\mathcal{E}_{1}[\psi{}]^{2}.

Proof.

Fix
(u,v)
. Define
u_{\ast}{}\in[u,\infty)
so that
r(u_{\ast}{},v)=v/2
if such a

u_{\ast}{}
exists, and otherwise set
u_{\ast}{}=u
. Split the integration
range as
[u,\infty)=[u,u_{\ast}{}]\cup[u,\infty)
. The
r
-weight in the
energy
\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]^{2}
is one power stronger than in the integral on
the left of
8.64
, so by the monotonicity of
r
we
gain one power of decay in
r(u_{\ast}{},v)
when integrating over

[u,u_{\ast}{}]
. The energy
\mathcal{E}_{p}[\psi{}]^{2}
controls the integral
over
[u_{\ast}{},\infty)
with a weight
\tau{}(u_{\ast}{},v)
, which is
comparable to
v
by
4.25
. Thus:

(8.65)

\begin{split}\int_{u}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r}{(-\nu{})}}{r/(-\nu{})}{r/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}&=r^{-1}(u_{\ast}{},v)\int_{u}^{u_{\ast}{}}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}+r_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1}\int_{u_{\ast}{}}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{r^{2}}{(-\nu{})}}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}{r^{2}/(-\nu{})}(\partial{}_{u}\psi{})^{2}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\\
&\leq 2v^{-1}\mathcal{E}[\psi{}]^{2}+r_{\mathrm{min}}^{-1}\tau{}^{-1}(u_{\ast}{},v)E_{1}[\psi{}]^{2}\leq v^{-1}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})\mathcal{E}_{1}[\psi{}]^{2}.\end{split}

∎

{proposition}

[Estimates for
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}
]
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, and let
s>0
be sufficiently small. We have decay estimates
for order-
\alpha{}
derivatives of
\log\kappa{}
when norms of
\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}
are
bounded:

(8.66)

\displaystyle v\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}),

(8.67)

\displaystyle r^{1-s-\eta{}_{0}}\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},2-s}^{2})\quad\text{if }\alpha{}_{U}>0,

(8.68)

\displaystyle r\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}+U}\log\kappa{}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}),

Moreover, order-
\alpha{}
derivatives of
\log\kappa{}
are bounded if lower order
derivatives of
\varphi{}
are:

(8.69)

\displaystyle\lvert\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}).

(8.70)

\displaystyle r\lvert\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2})\quad\text{if }\alpha{}_{U}>0.

Proof.

In Step 1, we establish a preliminary estimate. In Step 2, we use Step 1 and

8.51
to show
8.66
. In Step 3,
we prove
8.69
. In Step 4 we use the results of
the previous steps to show

8.67
,
8.68
and
8.70
.

Step 1: Estimate for
\Gamma^{\alpha{}+U}\log\kappa{}
in terms of
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}
.
Use

2.61
to move the
U
in
\Gamma^{\alpha{}+U}
to
the front, and then use
8.50
to obtain

(8.71)

\begin{split}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}+U}\log\kappa{}\rvert&\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert+r^{-1+s}\lvert V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}\log\kappa{}\rvert]\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[r\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+r^{-1+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert].\end{split}

Step 2: Proof of
8.66
.
We will write, for
example,
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}}
to mean the
collection of statements
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\beta}{}
for
multi-indices
\beta{}
such that
\beta{}\leq\alpha{}
and

\lvert\beta{}\rvert\geq 1
. In Step 2a, we show that Step 1 reduces us to estimating

U\log\kappa{}
and
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}
for
\alpha_{U}=0
.
The first case (Step 2b) is easy, and in the second case (Step 2c), we can
integrate
8.51
to
\{r=R_{0}\}
, since
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log\kappa{}

vanishes there when
\alpha_{U}=0
.

Step 2a: Proof that
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}}
implies

(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}+nU}
for all
n\geq 0
.
By
induction on
n
, it is enough to obtain the conclusion

(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}+U}
. Since

\beta{}\leq\alpha{}+U
if and only if
\beta{}=\alpha{}
or
\beta{}=\beta{}^{\prime}+U
for some
\beta{}^{\prime}\leq\alpha{}
, we reduce to showing that

(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}}
implies

(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\alpha{}+U}
. This follows from
substituting
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}}
into

8.71
and using

(8.72)

r\lvert U\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\leq v/2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{}+U,1}^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq v/2}r^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0}^{2}\leq v^{-1}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{}+U,1}^{2}.

and
4.25
and
s<1
.

Step 2b: Proof of
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\alpha{}}
for
\alpha{}=U
.

The transport equation for
\log\kappa{}
is
U\log\kappa{}=-r(U\varphi{})^{2}
. To complete the proof, control the right side by

v^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{U,1}^{2}
as in
8.72
.

Step 2c: Proof of
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\alpha{}}
if
\alpha_{U}=0
.

Fix
(u,v)
. Write
r_{0}=r(u,v)
. Integrate
8.50
to

\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}
, noting that
L\log\kappa{}|_{\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}}=0
because
\alpha{}_{U}=0
:

(8.73)

\begin{split}\lvert L\log\kappa{}\rvert(r=r_{0},v)&\leq C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})\Bigl[\underbrace{\int_{\min(r_{0},r_{\mathcal{H}})}^{\max(r_{0},r_{\mathcal{H}})}r\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}}\\
&\qquad+\underbrace{\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}\int_{r_{\mathcal{H}}}^{r_{0}}r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(II)}}+\int_{\min(r_{0},r_{\mathcal{H}})}^{\max(r_{0},r_{\mathcal{H}})}r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r\Bigr].\\
\end{split}

Terms
(I)
and
(II)
decay like
v^{-1}
. Indeed, for term

(\text{I})
, use
8.64
, and for term
(II)
, split
the integral into the regions
r\leq v/2
and
r\geq v/2
and use

4.25
. Thus

(8.74)

\lvert L\log\kappa{}\rvert(r=r_{0},v)\leq v^{-1}C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})(\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2})+C(\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-s}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})\int_{\min(r_{0},r_{\mathcal{H}})}^{\max(r_{0},r_{\mathcal{H}})}r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r.

Sum over
L\in\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}
and apply Grönwall’s inequality (noting that
s<1
) and recall the
definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}{}
(see
2.43
) to complete the proof.

Step 2d: Completing the proof by induction.
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, and suppose we have
shown
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{<\alpha{}}
. Write
\alpha{}=\beta{}+nU
for
\lvert\beta{}\rvert\geq 1
and
n\geq 0
maximal. Then either

\beta{}=U
or
\beta_{U}=0
, so by Step 2b or Step 2c we obtain

(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\beta{}}
. Together with the
induction hypothesis, this implies
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\beta{}}
, so Step 2a now implies
(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\beta{}+nU}=(\ref{kappa-alpha-bound-after-energy})_{\leq\alpha{}}
, which
completes the induction.

Step 3: Proof of
8.69
.
Follow Step 2, but instead
integrate
8.51
(which contains only lower order norms on the
right side, as opposed to
8.50
) as in Step 2c. We omit the
details.

Step 4: Proof of
8.67
,
8.68
and
8.70

from
8.66
and
8.69
.
Let

\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
satisfy
\alpha_{U}>0
. Start
with
8.71
to obtain

(8.75)

\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{<\alpha{}})[r\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}+r^{-1+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert].\\

To show
8.67
estimate

(8.76)

\lvert U\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-U}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq r^{-2+s+\eta{}_{0}}\tau^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},2-s}^{2}

and use
8.66
to control
\lvert\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\log\kappa{}\rvert
. The same argument establishes

8.68
and
8.70
, but we
estimate
\lvert U\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert
(resp.
U\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-U}
) by
r^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0}

instead.
∎

8.4.
Estimates for
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}(-\gamma{})

The main result of this section is
section

8.4
. The gauge condition
on
u
normalizes
\log(-\gamma{})=0
on
\mathcal{I}
. In fact,

\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})=0
on
\mathcal{I}
for all
\alpha{}
,
which we establish as part of the proof of
section

8.4
. To complete
estimates in which we integrate the transport equation for
\Gamma^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})
(see
8.81
) to
\mathcal{I}
, we first
formulate a variant of Grönwall’s inequality.

{lemma}
[Grönwall inequality on half-infinite interval]
Let
I=[t_{0},\infty)
. Suppose
f,g,h:I\to\mathbf{R}
are continuous functions such that
f

is bounded,
g
is non-increasing,
h
is non-negative and integrable on

I
, and

(8.77)

f(t)\leq g(t)+\int_{t}^{\infty}h(s)f(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\quad\text{for }t\in I.

Then

(8.78)

f(t)\leq g(t)\exp\Bigl(\int_{t}^{\infty}h(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Bigr)\quad\text{for }t\in I.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon{}>0
. Since
h\in L^{1}
and
f\in L^{\infty}
, we have
hf\in L^{1}
, so there is

T>t_{0}
such that
\lvert\int_{T}^{\infty}hf\rvert\leq\epsilon{}
. The assumption now implies

(8.79)

f(t)\leq(g(t)+\epsilon{})+\int_{t}^{T}h(s)f(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s

for
t\in[t_{0},T]
. The usual Grönwall inequality (after a change of variables

t\mapsto T-t
) and the non-negativity of
h
give

(8.80)

f(t)\leq(g(t)+\epsilon{})\exp\Bigl(\int_{t}^{T}h(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Bigr)\leq(g(t)+\epsilon{})\exp\Bigl(\int_{t}^{\infty}h(s)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}s\Bigr).

We are done since
\epsilon{}>0
is arbitrary.
∎

{lemma}

[Estimate for
\overline{\partial}_{r}L\log(-\gamma{})
]
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 0
and let
L\in\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}
. We have

(8.81)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})[r\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-3+2s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert].

Proof.

Step 1: Computing the commutator
[\overline{\partial}_{r},L]
. We have

(8.82)

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}[\overline{\partial}_{r},L]=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{<\alpha{}})[\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}+r^{-2+s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]\end{split}

We omit the proof, but the argument is an induction with base case

section

2.8.2
. A similar argument is done in detail in the proof of
A.27
.

Step 2: Proof of the desired
8.81
.
It is enough to show

(8.83)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\overline{\partial}_{r}L\log(-\gamma{})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s}[r\{\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{},r^{-2+s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\}^{2}+r^{-2+s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})].

When
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=0
,
8.83
follows from the equation
\overline{\partial}_{r}\log(-\gamma{})=r(\overline{\partial}_{r}\varphi{})^{2}
. From this equation and
8.82
, we
get

(8.84)

\begin{split}\overline{\partial}_{r}L\log(-\gamma{})&=L\overline{\partial}_{r}\log(-\gamma{})+[\overline{\partial}_{r},L]\log(-\gamma{})\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s}[r\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\overline{\partial}_{r}\varphi{}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\overline{\partial}_{r}\varphi{}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})+r^{-2+s}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})].\\
\end{split}

To complete the inductive proof of
8.83
for
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
, use

8.82
for the first term on the right of

8.84
and for the second term use
8.83

for multi-indices
<\alpha{}
.
∎

{lemma}

[Estimate for
\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})
]
Let
n\geq 0
. We have

(8.85)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lvert\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq r^{-2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<n}})\mathcal{P}_{U^{<n},0}^{2}.

Proof.

Step 1: Computing
\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\varphi{}
.
We claim that for
n\geq 1
, we have

(8.86)

\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-1}\mathcal{O}(\partial{}_{u}^{\leq n}\lambda{},\partial{}_{u}^{<n}(-\nu{}))[\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n}\varphi{}+\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}].

When
n=1
, this follows from the wave equation,

(8.87)

\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}\varphi{}=\kappa{}(-\nu{})\Box{}\varphi{}+r^{-1}(-\lambda{}\partial{}_{u}\varphi{}+(-\nu{})\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})=r^{-1}(-\lambda{}\partial{}_{u}\varphi{}+(-\nu{})\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}),

and the inductive step follows since
\partial{}_{u}r^{-1}=r^{-1}\cdot r^{-1}(-\nu{})
.

Step 2: Estimating
\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})
.
We show that for
n\geq 1
, we have

(8.88)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lvert\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq r^{-3}C(\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<n}})\mathcal{P}_{U^{<n},0}^{2}.

We first show that

(8.89)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<n}}[r^{-1}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n}\varphi{}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n}\varphi{}+\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}+(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}].

For the case
n=1
, we compute

(8.90)

\begin{split}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}\log(-\gamma{})&=\partial{}_{u}(\lambda{}^{-1}r(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2})\\
&=(-\nu{})\lambda{}^{-1}(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}-2r^{-1}\lambda{}^{-1}\kappa{}(-\nu{})(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+\partial{}_{u}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}+(-\nu{})\lambda{}^{-1}(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\lambda{}^{-1},(-\nu{}),\kappa{},r^{-1}\varpi{})[\partial{}_{u}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}+(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}],\end{split}

so that

(8.91)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}\log(-\gamma{})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\partial{}_{u}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}+(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}].

For the inductive step, we have

(8.92)

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}\log(-\gamma{})&=_{\mathrm{s}}\partial{}_{u}^{\leq 1}\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<n}}[r^{-1}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n+1}\varphi{}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n+1}\varphi{}+\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n+1}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}\\
&\qquad+(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{u}^{<n+1}\varphi{}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}+\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}].\end{split}

The first three terms are of the desired form. For the last two terms, use the
wave equation
8.87
. Since
\partial{}_{u}=(-\nu{})U
, the statement

8.89
follows.

The statements

(8.93)

\partial{}_{u}^{n}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(U^{<n}(-\nu{}))UU^{<n}\qquad\lambda{},(-\nu{}),\partial{}_{u}\lambda{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}\varpi{},\kappa{},(-\gamma{}))

follow from an induction argument starting from
\partial_{u}=(-\nu{})U
and from the
equation for
\partial{}_{u}\lambda{}
, respectively. Apply

8.93
to
8.89
to get

(8.94)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\partial{}_{v}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<n}}[r^{-1}UU^{<n}\varphi{}UU^{<n}\varphi{}+UU^{<n}\varphi{}\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}+(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}].

The desired
8.88
now follows from the definition of the
pointwise norm.

Step 3: Showing
\lim_{v\to\infty}\partial{}_{u}^{n}\log(-\gamma{})(u,v)=0
.
Let
n\geq 1
. We show
that a boundedness statement
C(\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<n}})\mathcal{P}_{U^{<n},0}^{2}<\infty
implies the qualitative result
\lim_{v\to\infty}\partial_{u}^{\leq n}\log(-\gamma{})(u,v)=0
. The hypothesis together with Step 2 implies that

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lvert\partial_{v}\partial_{u}^{\leq n}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert
is
integrable in
v
towards infinity (uniformly on compact subsets of
u
),
and so
\partial{}_{u}^{\leq n}\log(-\gamma{})(u,v)
converges uniformly as

v\to\infty
on compact subsets of
u
. A basic fact from real analysis is
that if
f:\mathbf{R}^{2}_{u,v}\to\mathbf{R}
is such that
\lim_{v\to\infty}f(u,v)

exists and
(\partial{}_{u}f)(u,v)
converges uniformly as
v\to\infty
, then

\partial{}_{u}\lim_{v\to\infty}f(u,v)
exists and is equal to
\lim_{v\to\infty}\partial{}_{u}f(u,v)
. Since
\lim_{v\to\infty}\log(-\gamma{})(u,v)=0
, an induction argument completes the proof.

Step 4: Completing the proof of
8.85
.
Let
r(u,v)\geq R_{0}
.
Integrate
8.88
in
v
on
[v,\infty)
. The boundary term
at infinity vanishes by Step 3c, so we conclude
8.85
.
∎

{lemma}

For
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, we have

(8.95)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\partial{}_{u}^{\leq\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}]

Proof.

We will in fact show that for a a multi-index
\alpha{}
and a non-negative integer

k
, we can rewrite
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
as follows:

(8.96)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L=\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})[\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}].

Schematically write
\text{good}_{\alpha{},k}
for terms appearing on the right of

8.96
for the pair
(\alpha{},k)
. The strategy is to induct
on the pair
(\alpha{},k)
.

Step 1: Preliminary observations and base case.
Observe that

8.96
for
(\alpha{},0)
is trivial. Moreover, since

\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-kU}=\{0\}
when
k\geq\lvert\alpha{}\rvert
, we know

8.96
for
(\alpha{},\lvert\alpha{}\rvert)
implies

8.96
for
(\alpha{},k)
whenever
k\geq\lvert\alpha{}\rvert
.

We now prove
8.96
for pairs
(\alpha{},1)
. Let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. Since
L
begins with either
U
,
V
, or
S
, we
have

(8.97)

\begin{split}L&=_{\mathrm{s}}U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}+V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}.\\
\end{split}

After rewriting the first derivative that makes up
L
using

2.45
,
2.47
and
2.49
, we get

(8.98)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\partial{}_{u}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}]=\text{good}_{\alpha{},1}.

as desired. We have used
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
to conclude
\mathfrak{b}_{0}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U}
.

Step 2: Inductive step.
Now suppose
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 2
and
1\leq k<\lvert\alpha{}\rvert
and
8.96
holds for pairs
(\beta{},j)

such that
\beta{}<\alpha{}
or such that
\beta{}=\alpha{}
and
0\leq j\leq k
. We will show that
8.96
holds for the pair

(\alpha{},k+1)
. First,
8.96
for the pair

(\alpha{},k)
gives

(8.99)

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})[\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU}+\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}.\end{split}

Now use
8.96
for the pair
(\alpha{}-kU,1)
to get

(8.100)

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})\\
&\qquad\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}[\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-(k+1)U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-(k+1)U})[\partial{}_{u}^{\leq 1}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-(k+1)U}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-V}+S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-S}]]\\
&\qquad+\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})(\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-(k+1)U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-(k+1)U}))[\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k+1}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-(k+1)U}\\
&\qquad+\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-V}+\partial{}_{u}^{\leq k}S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-S}]+\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}.\end{split}

Apply
\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\partial{}_{u}^{k}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<k}}U^{\leq k}
(see
8.93
) and

\mathfrak{b}_{U^{<k}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U}
(which holds since

k<\lvert\alpha{}\rvert
) to get

(8.101)

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}L&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})[U^{\leq k}\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-V}+U^{\leq k}S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-S}]+\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})[[\overline{\partial}_{r},U^{\leq k}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-V}+[S,U^{\leq k}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-S}]+\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}.\\
\end{split}

We now compute the commutator terms associated to
\overline{\partial}_{r}
and to

S
. By
8.82
, we have

(8.102)

\begin{split}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}[\overline{\partial}_{r},U^{\leq k}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-V}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{kU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{<kU})[\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})[\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}]=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}.\end{split}

We can pass to the second line because
kU\leq\alpha{}-U
(since

\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq k+1
and
(k+1)U
is the smallest multi-index of
order
k+1
). In the final identity we used
8.96
for
pairs
(<\alpha{},k+1)
.

Next, one can easily prove by induction on
k
, with base case
k=1
given
by
2.55
, that

(8.103)

[S,U^{k}]=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq kU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq kU})\Gamma{}^{\leq(k-1)U+V}.

It follows that

(8.104)

[S,U^{k}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-kU-S}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq kU},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq kU})\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-S+V}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{b}_{\leq\alpha{}-U},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\leq\alpha{}-U})\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{},k+1}.

In the second last identity we used
\alpha{}\geq kU
(since
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq k+1
). In the
final identity we used
8.96
for pairs
(<\alpha{},k+1)
.

Substitute
8.102
and
8.104
into

8.101
to establish
8.96
for

(\alpha{},k+1)
and hence complete the induction.
∎

{proposition}

[Estimates for
\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\log(-\gamma{})
]
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 0
. For
s>0
sufficiently small, we have

(8.105)

\displaystyle r\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})[\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R_{0}\},

(8.106)

\displaystyle r^{1-s-\eta{}_{0}}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}]

\displaystyle\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R_{0}\}.

Proof.

We first explain the logic of the proof. Consider the following auxiliary
qualitative decay statement for a multi-index
\alpha{}
with
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 0
:

(8.107)

\lim_{v\to\infty}L\log(-\gamma{})(u,v)=0\text{ for }L\in\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\text{ and }u\in[1,\infty).

To prove the quantitative decay estimates
8.106
and
8.105
, we
establish the following implications:

\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-qualitative-decay}_{<\alpha{}}\implies\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-bound-strong}_{<\alpha{}}\implies\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-bound-weak}_{\alpha}{}\implies\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-qualitative-decay}_{\alpha{}}.

That is,
8.107
for multi-indices
<\alpha{}
implies

8.105
for the multi-index
\alpha{}
, and so on. Since

8.107
holds when
\alpha{}=0
by the normalization at

\mathcal{I}
of the
u
-coordinate, an induction argument establishes

8.106
and
8.105
for all multi-indices
\alpha{}
. The final
implication is clear, so we prove the first two implications.

Step 1: Proof that
\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-qualitative-decay}_{\alpha{}}
implies

\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-bound-strong}_{\alpha{}}
.
Let
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 0
and let
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. By

8.81
and the definition of the pointwise norm, we have

(8.108)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})[r\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert].

Fix
(u,v)
such
that
r(u,v)\geq R_{0}
and integrate
8.108
to get

(8.109)

\begin{split}\lvert L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert(u,v)&\leq\limsup_{v^{\prime}\to\infty}\lvert L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert(u,v^{\prime})+\int_{v}^{\infty}\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})\Bigl[\int_{v}^{\infty}r\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}\int_{v}^{\infty}r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\qquad+\int_{v}^{\infty}r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\Bigr]\\
&\leq r^{-1}\tau{}^{-1}(u,v)C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})(\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2})+C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})\int_{v}^{\infty}r^{-2+s}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert(u,v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}.\end{split}

Sum over
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
, take
s<1
, and use the Grönwall inequality in

8.78
(which applies because of the assumed

\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-qualitative-decay}_{\alpha{}}
) to conclude

(8.110)

r\tau{}\lvert L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha{},s})(\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}^{2}).

Step 2: Proof that
\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-bound-strong}_{<\alpha{}}
implies

\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-bound-weak}_{\alpha}{}
.
To ease the notation, all estimates in
this section will be done in the region
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
; that is, all
estimates should be understood to have an implicit
\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}
at
the front of each term. It is enough to prove that

(8.111)

\lvert L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq r^{-1+s+\eta{}_{0}}C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}+r\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert],

since the final term can be handled with
\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{gamma-bound-strong}_{<\alpha{}}
.
First,
8.95
implies

(8.112)

\lvert L\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}})[\lvert\partial{}_{u}^{\leq\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert+\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert+\lvert S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert].

Observe that the following three estimates together with
8.112

imply
8.111
:

(8.113)

\displaystyle\lvert\partial{}_{u}^{\leq\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq r^{-2}C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}})\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}

(8.114)

\displaystyle\lvert\partial{}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}})[r^{-3}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}+r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1}^{2}+r^{-2}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert]

(8.115)

\displaystyle\lvert S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}})[r^{-1+s+\eta{}_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}+\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert].

Now
8.113
and
8.114
follow from

8.85
and
8.81
, respectively. We now
establish
8.115
. Rewrite
S
in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
using

4.25
,
2.47
and
2.49
, and then use

8.81
:

(8.116)

\begin{split}\lvert S\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})[v\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert+\tau{}\lvert U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert]\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}})[rv\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+r^{-3+2s}v\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert]\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}})[r^{-1+s+\eta{}_{0}}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}+\tau{}\lvert\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert].\end{split}

In the last line, we used
\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\leq v/2}r^{-2+s+\eta{}_{0}}\tau^{-1}v\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s}^{2}+\mathbf{1}_{r\geq v/2}r^{-4}\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},0}^{2}
.
∎

9.
Putting it all together: proof of
theorem

3.1

Our main theorem (
theorem

3.1
, see also the
rough versions
theorem

1.1
and
section

1.2.2
) follows
immediately from
9.1
and
9.22
.

9.1.
Control of
S^{k}\varphi
along the horizon

{proposition}

For
N\geq 0
and
\epsilon{}>0
, we have

(9.1)

\lvert S^{N}\varphi{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\epsilon{},N,\mathfrak{D}_{N},\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\mathrm{min}})\mathfrak{D}_{N}v^{-1+\epsilon{}}.

Proof.

In view of
4.25
, it is enough to show that

(9.2)

\mathcal{P}_{NS,2-C_{NS}^{\prime}\eta{}_{0}}\leq C(N,\eta{}_{0},\mathfrak{D}_{N},\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\text{min}})\mathfrak{D}_{N}

when
\eta_{0}
is sufficiently small based on
N
and then fix
\eta_{0}
small
based on
\epsilon{}
and
C_{NS}^{\prime}=C(N)
.

We now establish
9.2
. The results of

5.2
,
6
,
7.1
and
8

imply that we can fix
R_{\bullet}\geq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},N)

large enough that for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\leq N
and
s
sufficiently small
depending on
N
, we have

(9.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{<\alpha{}},r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{<\alpha{}})\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert},

(9.4)

\displaystyle C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(N,\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}),

(9.5)

\displaystyle C(r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})

\displaystyle\leq C(N,\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},1},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},2-s+\eta{}_{0}}),

(9.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\alpha}{}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})[\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}+\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},4s}],

(9.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},p}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{})(\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}+\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},p})

(9.8)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},2-\eta{}_{0}-C_{\alpha}{}s}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{},r^{-s}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha}{},\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},0},\mathcal{P}_{<\alpha{},1+\eta{}_{0}})\mathcal{D}_{\alpha}{}

for explicit constants
C_{\alpha}{}
. In particular, the above equations imply

(9.9)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},2-\eta{}_{0}-C_{\alpha}{}(s+\eta{}_{0})}\leq C(N,\mathfrak{D}_{N},\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{<\alpha{},2-s})\mathfrak{D}_{N}

for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\leq N
. By an induction argument (starting with
s=\eta_{0}
when
\alpha{}=0
), there are constants
C_{\alpha}^{\prime}
depending only on
\alpha{}
such that if
\eta_{0}

is small enough depending on
N
, we have

(9.10)

\mathcal{E}_{\alpha{},2-C_{\alpha}^{\prime}\eta{}_{0}}\leq C(N,\mathfrak{D}_{N},\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0,2-\eta{}_{0}})\mathfrak{D}_{N}

Substituting
9.10
into the above equations (and performing
another induction argument) gives

(9.11)

\mathcal{P}_{NS,2-C_{NS}^{\prime}\eta{}_{0}}\leq C(N,\mathfrak{D}_{N},\mathfrak{B}_{0},\mathfrak{G}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0,2-\eta{}_{0}})\mathfrak{D}_{N}.

The zeroth order estimates, due to

4.21
,
5.2
,
6
,
7.1
and
8
,
are

(9.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{D}_{0},

(9.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\text{min}})\mathcal{D}_{0},

(9.14)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathcal{E}_{0},\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\text{min}},R_{0},\eta{}_{0}),

(9.15)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0,0}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})[\mathcal{D}_{0}+\mathcal{E}_{0}],

(9.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{0,2-\eta{}_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{0,0})\mathcal{D}_{0},

(9.17)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{0,2-2\eta{}_{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})[\mathcal{D}_{0}+\mathcal{E}_{0,2-2\eta{}_{0}}],

(9.18)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{0}^{\circ}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0},\mathcal{E}_{0,1}),

(9.19)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C(R_{\bullet},\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathcal{E}_{0,1+\eta{}_{0}}),

(9.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0}),

(9.21)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{G}_{0}

\displaystyle\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0}).

Noting the value of
R_{\bullet}
that we have fixed and the value of
R_{0}=R_{0}(\varpi_{i})
that we have fixed (in
section

4
),
chaining the zeroth order estimates with
9.11
(for
\alpha{}=NS
)
gives
9.2
. Another induction argument in fact shows that all the
schematic quantities are controlled by

C(\epsilon{},N,\mathfrak{D}_{N},\varpi{}_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\mathrm{min}})
.
∎

9.2.
Control of
(\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}})^{k}\varphi{}
along the horizon

The goal of this section is to establish the following result comparing
S^{k}

(in the constant-
r
normalized gauge)and
(\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}})^{k}
(in the Eddington–Finkelstein-type gauge
normalized on the horizon).

{proposition}

For
0\leq k\leq 4
, we have

(9.22)

\lvert S^{k}\varphi{}-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{k}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\epsilon{},k,\varpi{}_{i},r_{\mathrm{min}},c_{\mathcal{H}},\mathfrak{D}_{k})v^{-1+\epsilon{}}.

In this section, we allow all constants to depend on
\varpi_{i}
,

c_{\mathcal{H}}
, and
r_{\text{min}}
. We will also freely apply the
results obtained in (the proof of)
9.1
, namely that our schematic energy
and geometric quantities of order
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=k
are controlled by the
data quantity
\mathfrak{D}_{k}
. We first introduce an integration lemma.

{lemma}

For
\alpha{}\geq 0
,
c>0
, and
v\geq 1
, we have

(9.23)

\int_{v}^{\infty}e^{-c(v^{\prime}-v)}v^{\prime-\alpha{}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq c^{-1}v^{-\alpha{}}.

Proof.

We have

(9.24)

\int_{v}^{\infty}e^{-c(v^{\prime}-v)}v^{\prime-\alpha{}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq v^{-\alpha{}}\int_{v}^{\infty}e^{-c(v^{\prime}-v)}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}=c^{-1}v^{-\alpha{}}.

∎

To estimate
\lambda{}
along the horizon, we use an ODE argument similar to the one
used in
[
31
, Prop. B.1]
(albeit in a different gauge).

{lemma}
[Decay of
\lambda
along the horizon]
We have

(9.25)

\lambda{}|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\epsilon{},\varpi{}_{i},r_{\mathrm{min}},c_{\mathcal{H}},\mathfrak{D}_{1})v^{-4+\epsilon{}}.

Proof.

Since
\lambda{}=\kappa{}(1-\mu{})\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})(1-\mu{})
, it is enough to obtain the claimed
estimate for
(1-\mu{}){|_{\mathcal{H}}}
in place of

\lambda{}|_{\mathcal{H}}
.

Step 1: Estimate for
(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}
.
The goal of this step is to use
the transport equation for
1-\mu{}
along the horizon to obtain

(9.26)

(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\leq\int_{v}^{\infty}e^{-c_{\ast}{}(v^{\prime}-v)}r(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}

for a constant
c_{\ast}{}>0
. The equation for
(1-\mu{})
(see

2.10
and
2.12
) gives

(9.27)

\partial{}_{v}(1-\mu{})-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}(1-\mu{})=-\mathchoice{\frac{r}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r}{\kappa{}}}{r/\kappa{}}{r/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}.

The method of integrating factors now gives

(9.28)

\begin{split}(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)&=\lim_{v^{\prime}\to\infty}\exp\Bigl(\int_{v}^{v^{\prime}}-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime\prime}\Bigr)(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v^{\prime})\\
&\qquad+\int_{v}^{\infty}\exp\Bigl(-\int_{v}^{v^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime\prime}\Bigr)r(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}.\end{split}

By
4.1
and
4.6
, we have

(9.29)

\exp\Bigl(-\int_{v}^{v^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime\prime}\Bigr)\leq e^{c_{\ast}{}(v^{\prime}-v)},

where
c_{\ast}{}
is a constant depending on
\varpi{}_{i}
,
c_{\mathcal{H}}
, and

\mathcal{E}_{0}
. Since
\lim_{v\to\infty}(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)=0

(see
[
31
, Prop. B.1]
), it follows from
9.29
that the first term on the right
side of
9.28
vanishes. Noting this observation and

9.29
, return to
9.28
to conclude the proof of
9.26
.

Step 2: Preliminary decay for
(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}
.
In this step we show
that

(9.30)

(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{0})v^{-2+\epsilon{}}.

Control the exponential in
9.26
by
1
and use the decay of
the energy to obtain

(9.31)

(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\leq\int_{v}^{\infty}r(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\int_{v}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{r^{2}}{\kappa{}}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}{r^{2}/\kappa{}}(\partial{}_{v}\varphi{})^{2}(v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq\tau{}^{-p}(\infty,v)C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0,p}\leq C(p,\mathfrak{D}_{0})v^{-p}

for
p<2
.

Step 3: Strong decay for
(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}
.
We now show that

(9.32)

(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{1})v^{-4+\epsilon{}}.

Use
S=v\partial{}_{v}+v\lambda{}U
and
9.30
to obtain

(9.33)

\lvert\partial{}_{v}\varphi{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq v^{-1}\lvert S\varphi{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}+\kappa{}(1-\mu{})|_{\mathcal{H}}\lvert U\varphi{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq\mathcal{P}_{S,2-\epsilon{}}v^{-2+\epsilon{}}+C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{b}_{0})\mathcal{P}_{U,2-\epsilon{}}v^{-3+\epsilon{}}\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{1})v^{-2+\epsilon{}}.

Substitute
9.33
into
9.26
and use

9.23
to conclude the proof.
∎

To estimate
S^{n}\lambda{}
along the horizon, we use the relation
\lambda{}=\kappa{}(1-\mu{})
and
estimate separately
S^{n}\kappa{}
and
S^{n}\mu{}
.

{lemma}
[Decay for
S^{n}\lambda
along the horizon]
Let
n\geq 1
. We have

(9.34)

\lvert S^{n}\lambda{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\epsilon{},\varpi{}_{i},r_{\mathrm{min}},c_{\mathcal{H}},\mathfrak{D}_{n})v^{-3+\epsilon{}}.

Proof.

Suppose for the sake of induction that the lemma has been proven for
0\leq m<n
. This holds for
n=1
by
9.25
.

Step 1: Decay for
(S^{n}\kappa{})|_{\mathcal{H}}
.
We first show that for
n\geq 1
we have

(9.35)

\lvert S^{n}\kappa{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\lesssim C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{n})v^{-5+\epsilon{}}.

Write
r_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\coloneqq{}r(u=\infty,v)
(so that
r_{\mathcal{H}}=r_{\mathcal{H}}(\infty)
). The strategy is to integrate the equation for

US^{n}\log\kappa{}
to
\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}
and use the decay of

\lvert r_{\mathcal{H}}-r_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\rvert
.

Step 1a: Decay of
\lvert r_{\mathcal{H}}-r_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\rvert
.
By

9.25
, we have

(9.36)

\lvert r_{\mathcal{H}}-r_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\rvert=\int_{v}^{\infty}\partial{}_{v}r\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{1})\int_{v}^{\infty}v^{-4+\epsilon{}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{1})v^{-3+\epsilon{}}.

Step 1b: Decay of
S^{n}\kappa{}
.
By a computation using the chain rule,
S^{n}\log\kappa{}-\kappa{}^{-1}S^{n}\kappa{}
is a polynomial in
\kappa{}^{-1}S^{i}\kappa{}
for

1\leq i<n
with no constant term, so it is enough to obtain decay for

S^{n}\log\kappa{}
. Since
U
and
S
commute near the horizon, we have

(9.37)

US^{n}\log\kappa{}=S^{n}U\log\kappa{}=S^{n}(r(U\varphi{})^{2})=_{\mathrm{s}}rUS^{\leq n}\varphi{}US^{<n}\varphi{},

for
n\geq 0
and so

(9.38)

\sup_{r\in[r_{\mathcal{H}}(v),r_{\mathcal{H}}]}\lvert US^{n}\log\kappa{}\rvert(r,v)\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})v^{-2+\epsilon{}}\mathcal{P}_{nS,2-\epsilon{}}^{2}\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{n})v^{-2+\epsilon{}}.

Since
S^{n}\log\kappa{}|_{\{r=r_{\mathcal{H}}\}}=0
, we can integrate

9.37
to get

(9.39)

S^{n}\log\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\leq\lvert r_{\mathcal{H}}-r_{\mathcal{H}}(v)\rvert\sup_{r\in[r_{\mathcal{H}}(v),r_{\mathcal{H}}]}\lvert US^{n}\log\kappa{}\rvert(r,v)\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{n})v^{-5+\epsilon{}}

for
n\geq 0
.

Step 2: Decay for
S^{n}\mu{}
.
In this step we show that for
n\geq 1
we have

(9.40)

\lvert S^{n}\mu{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{n})v^{-3+\epsilon{}},

By
2.10
and
Sr=0
near the
horizon, we have
S\mu{}=2r^{-1}S\varpi{}
near the horizon, so it is enough to prove
the estimate for
S\varpi{}
. Using the
transport equation for
\varpi{}
, we compute

(9.41)

S\varpi{}=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}r^{2}v\Bigl[\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}(\partial_{v}\varphi{})^{2}+\lambda{}(1-\mu{})(U\varphi{})^{2}\Bigr]=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{2}v\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\{v^{-1}S\varphi{},\lambda{}U\varphi{}\}^{2}].

For
n\geq 1
, it follows that

(9.42)

S^{n}\varpi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{2}v\mathfrak{b}_{(n-1)S}\{v^{-1}S^{\leq n}\varphi{},S^{<n}\lambda{}US^{<n}\varphi{}\}^{2}.

By the inductive hypothesis and the control on the geometric and pointwise
quantities, we have

(9.43)

\lvert S^{n}\varpi{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\lesssim C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{n})v^{-3+\epsilon{}},

and so

Step 3: Closing the induction.
We are done by the relation
\lambda{}=\kappa{}(1-\mu{})
, the
results of Steps 1 and 2, and the product rule.
∎

Proof of
9.22
.

The claim follows from
9.45
,
9.46
,
9.47
and
9.48
below
together with
9.61
,
9.34
,
9.25
and
9.1
.

Step 1: Schematic expressions for
S^{k}-(\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}})^{k}
for
1\leq k\leq 4
.
Define a function of
v
alone by

(9.44)

\rho{}\coloneqq{}1-\mathchoice{\frac{\bar{v}}{v}}{\frac{\bar{v}}{v}}{\bar{v}/v}{\bar{v}/v}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{1/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}{1/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}.

We show that

(9.45)

\displaystyle S-\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}}

\displaystyle=\rho{}S+(1-\rho{})v\lambda{}U,

(9.46)

\displaystyle S^{2}-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{2}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}(S^{\leq 1}\rho{})^{\leq 1}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}U(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 1}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 2},

(9.47)

\displaystyle S^{3}-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{3}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}(S^{\leq 2}\rho{})^{\leq 3}(S^{\leq 2}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 2}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{})S^{\leq 1}U(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}(U^{\leq 2}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 2})^{\leq 2}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 3},

\displaystyle S^{4}-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{4}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}(S^{\leq 3}\rho{})^{\leq 4}(S^{\leq 3}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 3}(S^{\leq 2}(v\lambda{})(S^{\leq 2}U(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 2})^{\leq 2}

(9.48)

\displaystyle\qquad(v\lambda{}(S^{\leq a}U^{\leq b}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 3})^{\leq 3}|_{a\leq 2,b\leq 3,a+b\leq 4}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 4}.

Indeed, we have

(9.49)

S=v\partial_{v}+v\lambda{}U,\quad\text{and}\quad\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}}=\mathchoice{\frac{\bar{v}}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{\frac{\bar{v}}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{\bar{v}/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}{\bar{v}/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}\partial{}_{v}.

It follows that

(9.50)

S-\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}}=\Bigl(v-\mathchoice{\frac{\bar{v}}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{\frac{\bar{v}}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{\bar{v}/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}{\bar{v}/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}\Bigr)\partial_{v}+v\lambda{}U=\Bigl(1-\mathchoice{\frac{\bar{v}}{v}}{\frac{\bar{v}}{v}}{\bar{v}/v}{\bar{v}/v}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}}{1/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}{1/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}}\Bigr)(S-v\lambda{}U)+v\lambda{}U=\rho{}S+(1-\rho{})v\lambda{}U,

which is
9.45
. We now explain how to compute
9.46
. From

9.50
, one obtains

(9.51)

S^{2}-S(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b\leq 1\\
a+b\geq 1\end{subarray}}(S^{\leq 1}\rho{})^{a}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}))^{b}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 2}

and

(9.52)

\begin{split}S(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{2}&=_{\mathrm{s}}(\rho{}S+(1-\rho{})v\lambda{}U)((1-\rho{})S+(1-\rho{})v\lambda{}U)\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a\leq 2,b,c\leq 1\\
a+b+c\geq 1\end{subarray}}(S^{\leq 1}\rho{})^{a}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}))^{b}(v\lambda{}U(v\lambda{}))^{c}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 2},\end{split}

where in
9.52
we used the already computed expression for
S-\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}}
. Adding
9.50
and
9.52
gives

9.46
. One computes
9.47
and
9.48
similarly.

Step 2: Estimate for
S^{k}(\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}})
when
1\leq k\leq 3
.
We show that

(9.53)

S^{k}\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{3})v^{-2+\epsilon{}}\quad\text{for }1\leq k\leq 3.

This follows from

9.54
,
9.58
,
9.60
,
9.54
,
9.55
and
9.56
, which are
proven below.

Step 2a: Schematic expressions for
S^{k}(\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}})
and
1\leq k\leq 3
.
Since
\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}
is a function of
v
alone, we have

U(\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}})=0
. It follows that

(9.54)

S^{k}(\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}})=(v\partial{}_{v})^{k}\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}=((v\partial{}_{v})^{k}\kappa{})|_{\mathcal{H}}=((S-v\lambda{}U)^{k}\kappa{})|_{\mathcal{H}}.

We compute

(9.55)

(S-v\lambda{}U)^{2}=S^{2}+S(v\lambda{})U+v\lambda{}SU+(v\lambda{})^{2}U^{2}+v\lambda{}U(v\lambda{})U=_{\mathrm{s}}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 2}(v\lambda{}U(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 1}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 2}

and

(9.56)

(S-v\lambda{}U)^{3}=_{\mathrm{s}}(S^{\leq 2}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 3}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{})(S^{\leq 1}U^{\leq 2}(v\lambda{}))^{\leq 2})^{\leq 2}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 3}.

Step 2b: Estimate for derivatives of
\lambda{}
.
The transport equation for
\lambda{}
is

(9.57)

U\lambda{}=\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}.

Using
9.25
and
9.34
for the
b=0
case, it
follows that

(9.58)

\lvert S^{a}U^{b}\lambda{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{a+b}).

Step 2c: Estimate for derivatives of
\kappa{}
along the horizon.

It is easy to compute from the transport equation for
\kappa{}
that for
b\geq 1

one has

(9.59)

S^{a}U^{b}\log\kappa{}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{\leq 1}S^{\leq a}U^{\leq b}\varphi{}S^{\leq a}U^{\leq b}\varphi{}.

Together with
9.35
for the case
b=0
(and a chain rule
argument), we obtain (for
a,b\geq 0
and
a+b\geq 1
)

(9.60)

\lvert S^{a}U^{b}\kappa{}\rvert|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{a+b})v^{-2+\epsilon{}}.

Step 3: Estimate for
S^{k}\rho{}
.
We claim that

(9.61)

\lvert S^{k}\rho{}\rvert\leq v^{-1}C(k,\mathfrak{D}_{k})\quad\text{for }1\leq k\leq 3.

We first consider the case
k=0
. Observe that

(9.62)

\rho{}=v^{-1}(v-\bar{v})+\mathchoice{\frac{\bar{v}}{v}}{\frac{\bar{v}}{v}}{\bar{v}/v}{\bar{v}/v}(1-1/\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}).

Since
\lvert 1-1/\kappa{}{|_{\mathcal{H}}}\rvert\lesssim\log\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{0})v^{-4}
by Step 1b of

9.34
, it is enough to show that
\bar{v}(v)=v+C(\mathfrak{D}_{0})
. From
\mathchoice{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}}{\frac{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}/\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}{\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}\bar{v}/\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}v}(v)=\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)

and
\bar{v}(v=1)=1
, one obtains

(9.63)

\begin{split}\lvert\bar{v}(v)-v\rvert&\leq\Big\lvert 1-v+\int_{1}^{v}\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}(v^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\Big\rvert\leq\int_{1}^{v}\lvert\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}(v^{\prime})-1\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq C(\mathfrak{b}_{0})\int_{1}^{v}\log\kappa{}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{0})\int_{1}^{v}v^{-4}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{0}).\end{split}

This establishes the cases
k=0
. We now show that for
k\geq 1
we have

(9.64)

S^{k}\rho{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b\geq 1\\
a\leq 1,b\leq k\end{subarray}}\rho{}^{a}(S^{\leq k}\log\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}})^{b}

Indeed, in the case
k=1
, one computes explicitly

(9.65)

S\rho{}=-\rho{}-\rho{}S\log\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}}+S\log\kappa{}|_{\mathcal{H}},

and the general case follows by an easy induction. Now

9.53
and
9.64
and the case
k=0
together
establish
9.61
.
∎

10.
Late-time tails

The goal of this section is to prove

theorem

10.2
(the precise version of
theorem

1.12
), as an
application of
[
29
, Main Theorem 4]
. The estimates we have already
obtained do not suffice to satisfy the assumptions of
[
29
]
. In
particular, we require additional control of

(r\overline{\partial}_{r})
-derivatives of the scalar field and of geometric
quantities, together with precise asymptotics for geometric quantities in the
region
\{u\lesssim r\}
. We prove such estimates in

section

10.3
. The estimates for

(r\overline{\partial}_{r})
-derivatives of the scalar field are coupled to the
corresponding estimates for the geometry. To obtain such estimates from control
of
S
-derivatives of the scalar field and the geometry (which has been done
in the preceding sections), we introduce the spacetime elliptic estimates (see

section

10.1
) and Klainerman’s Sobolev-type inequality
(see
section

10.2
) used in
[
29
]
.

In this section we will freely use the results of

section

9
, namely that our schematic quantities of order

\alpha{}
(see
section

2.7.4
) are controlled by data

\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert}
. We also allow our constants to depend on

\varpi{}_{i}
,
c_{\mathcal{H}}
, and
r_{\text{min}}
.

10.1.
Spacetime elliptic estimate

We first formulate an abstract second order weighted elliptic estimate in two dimensions.

{lemma}
[Abstract weighted elliptic estimate]
Let
U\coloneqq{}(0,\infty)_{x_{1}}\times(0,\infty)_{x_{2}}\subset\mathbf{R}^{2}_{x_{1},x_{2}}
. Fix a point
\overline{x}\in U
. For
\theta{}\in(0,1)
, write
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}=\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}(\overline{x})\coloneqq{}\{x\in U:\lvert x_{i}-\overline{x}_{i}\rvert<\theta{}\overline{x}_{i}\text{ for }i=1,2\}
. Fix
\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime}\in(0,1)

such that
\theta{}<\theta{}^{\prime}
. Let
L
be a differential operator on

\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}
of the form

(10.1)

L\psi{}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}w_{ij}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}w_{i}\partial{}_{i}\psi{}

for smooth coefficients
w_{ij},w_{i}
satisfying
w_{ij}=w_{ji}
. Suppose
that there are
\delta{}\in(0,1)
and
A\geq 1
such that the following
conditions hold on
\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}
:

(10.2)

\displaystyle w_{ii}

\displaystyle>0

(10.3)

\displaystyle\lvert w_{12}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\delta{})w_{11}w_{22},

(10.4)

\displaystyle\lvert\partial{}_{i}w_{ij}\rvert^{2}+\lvert x_{i}^{-1}w_{ij}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq A^{2}w_{jj}

(10.5)

\displaystyle\lvert w_{i}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq A^{2}w_{ii}

(10.6)

\displaystyle w_{jj}\lvert\partial{}_{j}w_{ii}\rvert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq A^{2}w_{ii}^{2}.

Then

(10.7)

\sum_{i}\lVert w_{ii}^{1/2}\partial{}_{i}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}+\sum_{i,j}\lVert w_{ii}^{1/2}w_{jj}^{1/2}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}\lesssim_{\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime},\delta{}}A(\lVert\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}+\lVert L\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}).

10.1
.

Observe that conditions

10.4
,
10.5
and
10.6
“scale correctly,”
in the sense that the indices
i
and
j
appear the same number of times on
each side when counted with multiplicity, where a derivative counts with
multiplicity
-1
.

Proof.

The result follows from
10.9
and
10.13
below. For ease
of notation, we will assume
\theta{}^{\prime}=4\theta{}
. Let
\chi{}
be a
smooth function satisfying

(10.8)

0\leq\chi{}\leq 1,\quad\chi{}\equiv 1\text{ on }\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{},\quad\Supp\chi{}\subset\mathcal{R}_{2\theta{}},\qquad\lvert\partial{}_{i}\chi{}\rvert\leq C\theta{}^{-1}\overline{x}_{i}^{-1}\leq C\theta{}^{-1}x_{i}^{-1}\text{ on }\mathcal{R}_{2\theta{}}.

Step 1: Estimate for first order derivatives.
We first show that

(10.9)

\sum_{i=1}^{2}\lVert w_{ii}^{1/2}\partial{}_{i}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}\leq CA\theta{}^{-1}\delta{}^{-2}(\lVert\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{2\theta{}})}+\lVert L\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{2\theta{}})}).

Multiply
10.1
by
\chi^{2}\psi{}
and rewrite and rearrange (“integrate by parts”) to get

(10.10)

\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}\chi{}^{2}w_{ij}\partial{}_{i}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}=-\chi{}^{2}\psi{}L\psi{}+\sum_{i}\chi{}^{2}w_{i}\psi{}\partial{}_{i}\psi{}+\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}[\partial{}_{i}(\chi{}^{2}w_{ij}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{})-2\chi{}\partial{}_{i}\chi{}w_{ij}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}-\chi{}^{2}\partial{}_{i}w_{ij}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}.

The ellipticity condition
10.3
and Young’s inequality imply

(10.11)

\delta{}\sum_{i}\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}(\partial{}_{i}\psi{})^{2}\leq\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{abstract-elliptic-1}\text{{)}}.

Let
\epsilon{}>0
. Use Young’s inequality and then the conditions

10.4
and
10.5
to obtain

(10.12)

\begin{split}\text{{(RHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{abstract-elliptic-1}\text{{)}}&\leq\epsilon{}\sum_{i}\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}(\partial{}_{i}\psi{})^{2}+C\epsilon{}^{-1}(\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert L\psi{}\rvert^{2})+\sum_{i,j=1}^{2}[\partial{}_{i}(\chi{}^{2}w_{ij}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{})\\
&\qquad+C\epsilon{}^{-1}\psi{}^{2}\sum_{i,j}[\chi{}^{2}w_{jj}^{-1}w_{j}^{2}+w_{jj}^{-1}\lvert\partial{}_{i}\chi{}w_{ij}\rvert^{2}+w_{jj}^{-1}\lvert\partial{}_{i}w_{ij}\rvert]\\
&\leq\epsilon{}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}(\partial{}_{i}\psi{})^{2}+\sum_{i,j}\partial{}_{i}(\chi{}^{2}w_{ij}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{})+CA\theta{}^{-1}\epsilon{}^{-1}(\lvert\psi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert L\psi{}\rvert^{2})\end{split}

Take
\epsilon{}<\delta{}/4
to absorb the first term on the right of

10.12
to the left side of
10.11
. Integrate
over
\mathcal{R}_{2\theta{}}
, noting that the total derivative term has vanishing
integral, to conclude
10.9
.

Step 2: Estimate for second order derivatives.
We now show that

(10.13)

\sum_{i,j}\lVert w_{ij}\partial_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}+\sum_{i\neq{}j}\lVert w_{ii}^{1/2}w_{jj}^{1/2}\partial_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}\leq CA\theta^{-1}\delta^{-1}(\lVert\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{4\theta{}})}+\lVert L\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{4\theta{}})})

Begin by computing

(10.14)

\begin{split}2\sum_{i\neq{}j}w_{ij}^{2}(\partial_{ij}^{2}\psi{})^{2}+\sum_{i,j}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial_{jj}^{2}\psi{}&=(L\psi{})^{2}-\sum_{i\neq{}j}\underbrace{2w_{ii}w_{ij}\partial_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial_{ij}^{2}\psi{}}_{\text{(I)}}\\
&\qquad-\underbrace{2\sum_{i,j,k}w_{ij}w_{k}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{k}\psi{}}_{\text{(II)}}-\underbrace{\sum_{i,j}w_{i}w_{j}\partial{}_{i}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}}_{\text{(III)}}.\end{split}

Treat term
(I)
using
10.3
and Young’s
inequality. Terms
(II)
and
(III)
can be handled with
Young’s inequality:

(10.15)

\begin{split}\lvert\text{(I)}\rvert&\leq 2w_{ii}w_{ii}^{1/2}w_{jj}^{1/2}\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\leq(1-\delta{})w_{ii}^{2}(\partial{}_{ii}\psi{})^{2}+(1-\delta{})w_{ii}w_{jj}(\partial{}_{ij}\psi{})^{2}\\
\text{(II)}+\text{(III)}&\leq\epsilon{}\sum_{i,j}w_{ij}(\partial{}_{ij}\psi{})^{2}+C\epsilon{}^{-1}\sum_{i}w_{i}(\partial{}_{i}\psi{})^{2}.\end{split}

Substitute
10.15
into
10.14
, take
\epsilon{}=\delta{}/2
and absorb the
\epsilon{}
-term to the left to get

(10.16)

\begin{split}\mathchoice{\frac{\delta{}}{2}}{\frac{\delta{}}{2}}{\delta{}/2}{\delta{}/2}\sum_{i,j}w_{ij}^{2}(\partial_{ij}^{2}\psi{})^{2}+\sum_{i\neq{}j}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial_{jj}^{2}\psi{}&\leq(L\psi{})^{2}+C\delta{}^{-1}\sum_{i}w_{i}^{2}(\partial{}_{i}\psi{})^{2}\end{split}

We now multiply by
\chi^{2}
and integrate the second term on the left by parts
twice:

(10.17)

\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{jj}^{2}\psi{}=\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}w_{jj}(\partial{}_{ij}\psi{})^{2}-\partial{}_{i}(\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ji}^{2}\partial{}_{j}\psi{})+\partial{}_{j}(\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{})+\mathcal{E}_{ij},

where

(10.18)

\begin{split}\mathcal{E}_{ij}&\coloneqq{}2\chi{}\partial{}_{i}\chi{}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}+\chi{}^{2}\partial{}_{i}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}+\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}\partial{}_{i}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ij}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}\\
&\qquad-2\chi{}\partial{}_{j}\chi{}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}-\chi{}^{2}\partial{}_{j}w_{ii}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}-\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}\partial{}_{j}w_{jj}\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{}\partial{}_{j}\psi{}.\end{split}

Estimate the terms in
10.18
using Young’s inequality

10.4
and
10.6
:

(10.19)

\begin{split}\lvert\mathcal{E}_{ij}\rvert\leq C\epsilon{}\chi^{2}w_{ii}w_{jj}(\partial_{ij}\psi{})^{2}+C\epsilon{}\chi^{2}w_{ii}^{2}(\partial{}_{ii}^{2}\psi{})^{2}+CA^{2}\theta{}^{-2}\epsilon^{-1}w_{jj}(\partial_{j}\psi{})^{2}.\end{split}

Substitute
10.18
and
10.19
into

10.17
and move all terms but

\chi{}^{2}w_{ii}w_{jj}(\partial{}_{ij}\psi{})^{2}
to the right side of

10.16
. Take
\epsilon{}>0
small enough based on

\delta{}
to absorb the
\epsilon{}
-weighted terms to the left, integrate
over
\mathcal{R}_{2\theta{}}
, and use
10.9
(with

2\theta{}
in place of
\theta{}
) to conclude
10.13
.
∎

Now we construct elliptic operators involving
S
,
S^{2}
, and
\Box{}
to
which we apply
10.7
.

{lemma}

Fix
0<\theta{}<\theta{}^{\prime}<1
. Let
U_{0}
be sufficiently large
depending on
\mathfrak{D}_{1}
. Fix a point
(u_{0},r_{0})\in\{r\geq R_{\bullet}\}\cap\{u\geq U_{0}\}
. Write
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}(u_{0},r_{0})
. We have

(10.20)

\sum_{i+j\leq 2}\lVert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{i}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{j}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}})}\lesssim_{\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime}}\lVert S^{\leq 2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}+r_{0}\min(u_{0},r_{0})\lVert\Box{}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}.

Proof.

Step 1: Elliptic operator in the region
\{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq\epsilon{}u\}
.
We show that there
is
\epsilon{}>0
(depending on
\mathcal{G}_{0}
and
G_{\eta_{0}}
) such that

(10.21)

\sum_{i+j\leq 2}u^{i}r^{i}\lVert\overline{\partial}_{u}^{i}\overline{\partial}_{r}^{j}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}}(u,r))}\lesssim_{\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime}}\lVert S^{\leq 2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}(u,r))}+ur\lVert\Box{}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}(u,r))}\quad\text{in }\{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq\epsilon{}u\},

Write
\chi{}=\chi{}_{\lesssim R_{\bullet}}(r)
. Compute

(10.22)

\begin{split}S^{2}-S&=(u+\chi{}\cdot(v-u))^{2}\overline{\partial}_{u}^{2}+r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}^{2}+2r(u+\chi{}\cdot(v-u))\overline{\partial}_{u}\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
&\qquad+r(\chi{}^{\prime}(v-u)+\lambda{}^{-1}\chi{})\partial{}_{u}-u\chi{}(1-(-\gamma{})/\kappa{})\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
\end{split}

Let
B>0
be a constant to be chosen. Now
\eqref{S2-minus-S-near}-Br^{2}\cdot\eqref{box-I-gauge}
is

(10.23)

\begin{split}S^{2}-S-Br^{2}\Box{}&=(u+\chi{}\cdot(v-u))^{2}\overline{\partial}_{u}^{2}+(1+B(1-\mu{}))r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}+(2r(u+\chi{}\cdot(v-u))-Br^{2}(-\gamma{})^{-1})\overline{\partial}_{u}\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
&\qquad+r(\chi{}^{\prime}\cdot(v-u)+\lambda{}^{-1}\chi{}+B(2-2\varpi{}/r))\overline{\partial}_{r}-(u\chi{}(1-(-\gamma{})/\kappa{})+Br(-\gamma{})^{-1})\overline{\partial}_{u}.\end{split}

We now verify conditions

10.2
,
10.3
,
10.4
,
10.5
and
10.6

of
10.7
. In
\{r\leq\eta{}_{0}u\}
we have

(10.24)

w_{uu}=u^{2}+O(1)ur\quad w_{rr}=(1+B(1-\mu{}))r^{2}\quad w_{ur}=(2ur+(BO(1))+O(1))r^{2}),

where we write
O(1)
for a term controlled by
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
and
G_{\eta_{0}}
.
Now let
\epsilon{}<\eta_{0}
. It follows that in
\{r\leq\epsilon{}u\}
for
\epsilon{}
sufficiently
small depending on
\mathcal{G}_{0}
,
G_{1/2}
, and
B
, we have

(10.25)

\lvert w_{uu}\rvert\geq\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}u^{2}\quad w_{rr}=(1+B(1-\mu{}))r^{2}\quad\lvert w_{ur}\rvert\leq 3ur.

Since
1-\mu{}\geq 1/2
in
\{r\geq R_{0}\}
, it follows that in
\{R_{0}\leq r\leq\epsilon{}u\}
,

10.2
and
10.3
are satisfied for
B=36

(with
\delta{}>0
an explicit numerical constant).
Conditions
10.4
,
10.5
and
10.6
follow
from computations involving
\mathfrak{B}_{V}
and
G_{\eta_{0}}
.

Step 2: Elliptic operator in the region
\{r\geq\epsilon{}u\}
.
Let
\epsilon{}
be as in
Step 1. In fact, the value of
\epsilon{}
does not play a role in the elliptic
estimate for this region. We show that for
u
large depending on

C(\mathfrak{D}_{2})
, we have

(10.26)

\sum_{i+j\leq 2}u^{i}r^{i}\lVert\overline{\partial}_{u}^{i}\overline{\partial}_{r}^{j}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}}(u,r))}\lesssim_{\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime}}\lVert S^{\leq 2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}(u,r))}+ur\lVert\Box{}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}(u,r))}\quad\text{in }\{r\geq\epsilon{}u\}.

For
u\geq\epsilon^{-1}R_{\bullet}
, we have
r\geq R_{\bullet}
, so

(10.27)

S^{2}-S=u^{2}\overline{\partial}_{u}^{2}+r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}^{2}+2ur\overline{\partial}_{u}\overline{\partial}_{r}.

Now
\eqref{S2-minus-S-far}-2ur\cdot\eqref{box-I-gauge}
is

(10.28)

S^{2}-S-2ur\Box{}=u^{2}\overline{\partial}_{u}^{2}+(r^{2}+2ur)\overline{\partial}_{r}^{2}+2ur\Bigl(1-\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{1}{(-\gamma{})}}{1/(-\gamma{})}{1/(-\gamma{})}\Bigr)\overline{\partial}_{u}\overline{\partial}_{r}+4u(1-\varpi{}/r)\overline{\partial}_{u}-u\mathchoice{\frac{2}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{2}{(-\gamma{})}}{2/(-\gamma{})}{2/(-\gamma{})}\overline{\partial}_{r}.

To apply
10.7
and obtain
10.26
, we need to check that this operator
satisfies the conditions

10.2
,
10.4
,
10.5
and
10.6
.
Clearly
10.2
is satisfied. Since
\lvert 1-1/(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq r^{-1}u^{-1}C(\mathfrak{B}_{0})
,
10.3
is satisfied for
u

large enough depending on
\mathfrak{B}_{0}
. Since

\lvert\overline{\partial}(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq r^{-1}u^{-1}C(\mathfrak{B}_{V})
,

10.4
is satisfied for
u
large enough. Since
u\lesssim r
,
10.5
and
10.6
are satisfied.
∎

10.2.
Klainerman’s Sobolev-type inequality

In this section we upgrade the
L^{2}
estimates in

section

10.1
to
L^{2}
–
L^{\infty}
Sobolev inequalities.

{lemma}

Let
c>1
. Let
Q\coloneqq{}Q_{\ell{}_{1},\ell{}_{2}}\subset\mathbf{R}^{2}
be a rectangle of side lengths
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
. We
have

(10.29)

\lVert f\rVert_{L^{\infty}(Q)}\lesssim_{c}\ell{}_{1}^{-1/2}\ell{}_{2}^{-1/2}\sum_{i+j\leq 2}\lVert(\ell{}_{1}\partial{}_{x^{1}})^{i}(\ell{}_{2}\partial{}_{x^{2}})^{j}f\rVert_{L^{2}(cQ)}

Proof.

Apply the usual Sobolev embedding on a unit rectangle to the rescaled function

f(\ell_{1}x^{1},\ell_{2}x^{2})
.
∎

{lemma}

[Weighted
L^{2}
–
L^{\infty}
estimate]
Fix
0<\theta{}<\theta{}^{\prime}<1
. Fix a point
(u_{0},r_{0})\in\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}\cap\{u\geq U_{0}\}
for

U_{0}
as in
10.20
. Write
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}(u_{0},r_{0})
as in

10.7
. We have

(10.30)

u_{0}^{1/2}r_{0}^{1/2}\lVert\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}\lesssim_{\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime}}\lVert S^{\leq 2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}+\lVert r^{2}\Box{}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}.

Proof.

Combine
10.20
with
10.29
,
noting that
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}
is a rectangle with side lengths proportional
to
u_{0}
and
r_{0}
and that the coordinates
u
and
r
are comparable
to
u_{0}
and
r_{0}
in
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}(u_{0},r_{0})
.
∎

10.3.
Estimates for
(r\overline{\partial}_{r})
-derivatives of the scalar field and of geometric quantities

10.3.1.
Estimates for
(r\overline{\partial}_{r})
-derivatives of the scalar field

{lemma}

[Commutation formula for
r\overline{\partial}_{r}
and
S
]
For
n\geq 0
and
m\geq 0
and
n+m\geq 1
, we have

(10.31)

\begin{split}&[\Box{},(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b\leq m-1\\
i+j+k\leq n\end{subarray}}\{1,1+(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{i}\log(-\gamma{})\}^{\leq 1}\{1,(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{j}S^{1+a}\log(-\gamma{})\}^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{k}S^{b}\Box{}\\
&\,+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c\leq m\\
i+j+k\leq n\\
c\leq m-1\end{subarray}}\{O_{\infty}(1),r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{i}S^{a}\varpi{}\}((r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{j}S^{b}\log(-\gamma{}))^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq 1+k}S^{c}\\
&\,+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c\leq m\\
i+j+k+\ell{}\leq n+1\\
\ell{}\geq 1\end{subarray}}(1+(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{i}\log(-\gamma{}))^{\leq 1}\{O_{\infty}(1),r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{j}S^{a}\varpi{}\}((r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{k}S^{b}\log(-\gamma{}))^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\ell{}}S^{c},\\
\end{split}

where we write
O_{\infty}(1)
for a constant-coefficient polynomial in
r^{-1}
.

Proof.

The lemma follows from
10.33
and
10.35
, which we prove below.

Step 1: Commutation formula for
r\overline{\partial}_{r}
.
A computation reveals that

(10.32)

\begin{split}[\Box{},r\overline{\partial}_{r}]&=(1+r\overline{\partial}_{r}\log(-\gamma{}))\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\Bigl[-1+\mathchoice{\frac{4\varpi{}}{r}}{\frac{4\varpi{}}{r}}{4\varpi{}/r}{4\varpi{}/r}-\mathchoice{\frac{3\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{3\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{3\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}{3\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}\Bigr](r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}\\
