&\qquad+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\Bigl[-1+\mathchoice{\frac{3\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{3\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{3\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}{3\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}-\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r}}{\frac{2}{r}}{2/r}{2/r}r\overline{\partial}_{r}\varpi{}+\Bigl(2-\mathchoice{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{2\varpi{}/r}{2\varpi{}/r}\Bigr)r\overline{\partial}_{r}\log(-\gamma{})\Bigr]r\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}(1+r\overline{\partial}_{r}\log(-\gamma{}))\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\bigl[O_{\infty}(1)+r^{-1}\varpi{}\bigr](r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}\\
&\qquad+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\bigl[O_{\infty}(1)+r^{-1}r\overline{\partial}_{r}\varpi{}+\{1,r^{-1}\varpi{}\}r\overline{\partial}_{r}\log(-\gamma{})\bigr]r\overline{\partial}_{r}.\end{split}

It follows by induction that for
n\geq 1
, we have

(10.33)

\begin{split}[\Box{},(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}]&=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{i+j\leq n-1}(1+(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{1+i}\log(-\gamma{}))(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{j}\Box{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i+j+k\leq n+1\\
k\geq 1\end{subarray}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\{O_{\infty}(1),r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{i}\varpi{}\}((r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{j}\log(-\gamma{}))^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{k}\\
\end{split}

Step 2: Commutation formula for
S
.
In the region
\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}
of interest, where
S=r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}
, the computation
A.49
gives

(10.34)

\begin{split}[\Box{},S]&=_{\mathrm{s}}(2+S\log(-\gamma{}))\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\bigl[O_{\infty}(1)+\{O_{\infty}(1),r^{-1}\varpi{}\}S\log(-\gamma{})+r^{-1}S^{\leq 1}\varpi{}\bigr](r\overline{\partial}_{r})(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq 1}.\\
\end{split}

It follows by induction that for
n\geq 1
, we have

(10.35)

\begin{split}[\Box{},S^{n}]&=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{a+b\leq n-1}\{1,S^{1+a}\log(-\gamma{})\}S^{b}\Box{}\\
&\qquad+\sum_{a+b+c\leq n-1}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\bigl[O_{\infty}(1)+\{O_{\infty}(1),r^{-1}S^{a}\varpi{}\}S^{1+b}\log(-\gamma{})+r^{-1}S^{\leq 1+a}\varpi{}\bigr](r\overline{\partial}_{r})(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq 1}S^{c}.\\
\end{split}

∎

{lemma}

Fix
0<\theta{}<\theta{}^{\prime}<1/2
. Fix a point
(u_{0},r_{0})\in\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}\cap\{u\geq U_{0}\}
for

U_{0}
as in
10.20
. Write
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}(u_{0},r_{0})
as in

10.7
. For
n,m\geq 0
, we have

(10.36)

\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{})}\lesssim C(n,m,\theta{},\theta{}^{\prime},\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})\lVert S^{\leq n+m+3}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}})}.

{corollary}

Let
n,m\geq 0
and let
\epsilon{}>0
. In the region
\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}
, we have

(10.37)

(r^{1/2}\tau{}^{1-\epsilon{}}+r\tau{}^{1/2-\epsilon{}})\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(\epsilon{},n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+3}).

Proof.

In the region
\{u\geq U_{0}\}
, this is an immediate consequence of

10.36
, the definition of the pointwise norm (see

section

2.7.3
), and the results of
section

9
. In
the region
\{u\leq U_{0}\}
, we can write
r\overline{\partial}_{r}=S-u\overline{\partial}_{u}
to reduce to estimating expressions of the form

(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{n^{\prime}}S^{m^{\prime}}\varphi{}
for
n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=n+m
. From the calculation

(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{n}=_{\mathrm{s}}u^{\leq n}\overline{\partial}_{u}^{1\leq n}=_{\mathrm{s}}u^{\leq n}C(\mathfrak{b}_{nV})D^{1\leq n}
and the boundedness of
u
, we have

(10.38)

\lvert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{n^{\prime}}S^{m^{\prime}}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim_{U_{0}}C(\mathfrak{D}_{n})\lvert D^{\leq n^{\prime}}S^{\leq m^{\prime}}\varphi{}\rvert,

which we can estimate with the pointwise norm. Recalling that
U_{0}
depends
only on
\mathfrak{D_{1}}
completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
10.36
.

Let
c>1
be a parameter that will be chosen in the proof. We allow all
estimates to depend on
n
,
m
,
\theta{}
,
\theta{}^{\prime}
, and
c
. The
desired estimate follows from
10.52
, after choosing
c>1

so that
c^{n+m+3}\theta{}\leq\theta{}^{\prime}
. The restriction
\theta{}^{\prime}<1/2
ensures that
r>2R_{\bullet}
in the region
\mathcal{R}_{\theta{}^{\prime}}
(and
so
S=r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}
). In this proof, we
write
\mathcal{R}(\theta{})
in place of
\mathcal{R}_{\theta}{}
. The right
sides of our equations are to be understood schematically, as in

section

2.6.4
.

Step 1:
L^{2}
estimate for the commutator.
We claim that for
n\geq 0
and
m\geq 0
and
n+m\geq 1
, we have

(10.39)

\begin{split}&\lVert r^{2}\Box{}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\\
&\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))})\\
&\qquad[\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 1}\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+2}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}].\end{split}

The idea is to use the transport equation for
(-\gamma{})
and
\varpi{}
to express the
coefficients in
10.31
in terms of the scalar field, and then
put the top order terms in
L^{2}
and the lower order terms in
L^{\infty}
.

Step 1a: Estimate for derivatives of
(-\gamma{})
.
When
n=0
, we can estimate

(10.40)

\lvert S^{m}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m})

by the results of
section

9
. When
n\geq 1
,
differentiating the transport equation
(r\overline{\partial}_{r})\log(-\gamma{})=((r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}
gives

(10.41)

\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\lesssim C(\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert,\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert)\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert

Step 1b: Estimate for derivatives of
\varpi{}
.
When
n=0
, we can estimate

(10.42)

\lvert S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m})

by the results of
section

9
. When
n\geq 1
, we claim

(10.43)

r^{-1}\lvert(r\partial{}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{m},\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert,(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{})\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert.

The transport equation for
\varpi{}
is

(10.44)

r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varpi{}=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}(1-\mu{})((r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(1,r^{-1}\varpi{})((r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}.

Differentiate to obtain

(10.45)

\begin{split}r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varpi{}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(1,r^{-1}S^{\leq m}\varpi{},r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{1\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varpi{})((r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{})\\
&\qquad\{(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{},(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\}.\end{split}

Now
10.43
follows from an induction argument and the
n=0

case.

Step 1c: Completing the proof of
10.39
.
Substitute

10.43
,
10.42
,
10.40
and
10.41

into
10.31
to obtain

(10.46)

\begin{split}\lvert r^{2}\Box{}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert&\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m},\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert,\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert)\\
&\Bigl[\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c\leq m\\
i+j+k\leq n\\
c\leq m-1\end{subarray}}(1+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq i}S^{\leq a}\varphi{}\rvert)(1+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq j}S^{\leq b}\varphi{}\rvert)\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq k+2}S^{c}\varphi{}\rvert\\
&\mathbf{1}_{n\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a+b+c\leq m\\
i+j+k+\ell{}\leq n+1\\
\ell{}\geq 1\end{subarray}}(1+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq i}\varphi{}\rvert)(1+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq j}S^{\leq a}\varphi{}\rvert)(1+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq k}S^{\leq b}\varphi{}\rvert)\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\ell{}}S^{c}\varphi{}\rvert\Bigr].\\
\end{split}

To obtain
10.39
, put the top order terms in
L^{2}
and absorb the
lower order terms into the constant.

Step 2: Basic estimates for the scalar field.
It is an immediate consequence of

10.20
that for
n\geq 1
, we have

(10.47)

\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-\min(2,n)}S^{\leq m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\lVert r^{2}\Box{}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-\min(2,n)}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}.

It follows from
10.47
and
10.39
that, for
n\geq 3
,

(10.48)

\begin{split}&\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\\
&\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-3}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-2}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))})\\
&\qquad[\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-2}S^{\leq m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 1}\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}].\end{split}

Finally, it follows from
10.30
and
10.39
that, for
n,m\geq 0
,

(10.49)

\begin{split}&u_{0}^{1/2}r_{0}^{1/2}\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\\
&\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))})\\
&\qquad[\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{\leq m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\mathbf{1}_{m\geq 1}\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+2}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}].\end{split}

Step 3: Completing the proof.
For
n,m\geq 0
, we will show the
L^{2}
–
L^{2}
estimate

(10.50)

\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m})\lVert S^{\leq n+m+1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c^{n+m+1}\theta{}))}

and the
L^{2}
–
L^{\infty}
estimate

(10.51)

u_{0}^{1/2}r_{0}^{1/2}\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})\lVert S^{\leq n+m+3}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c^{n+m+3}\theta{}))}.

Note that
10.51
implies the desired
L^{\infty}
–
L^{\infty}
estimate

(10.52)

\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})\lVert S^{\leq n+m+3}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c^{n+m+3}\theta{}))}

by the Hölder’s inequality
\lVert\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim u_{0}^{1/2}r_{0}^{1/2}\lVert\psi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}
.

Step 3a: The base case
10.50
for
n\leq 2
and
m\geq 0
.

First,
10.50
is trivial when
n=0
for all
m\geq 0
. For
the case
1\leq n\leq 2
, we claim that, for
m\geq 0
,

(10.53)

\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq 2}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m})\lVert S^{\leq m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c^{m+1}\theta{}))}.

An induction argument using
10.20
and
10.35
shows that,
for
m\geq 0
,

(10.54)

\lVert r^{2}\Box{}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m})\lVert S^{\leq m+1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c^{m}\theta{}))}.

Conclude the desired
10.53
using
10.47
and
10.54
.

Step 3b: The base case
10.51
for
n=0
and
m\geq 0
.

Equations

10.30
and
10.54
imply that

(10.55)

u_{0}^{1/2}r_{0}^{1/2}\lVert S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim\lVert S^{\leq m+2}\psi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\lVert r^{2}\Box{}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m})\lVert S^{\leq m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c^{m+1}\theta{}))}.

Step 3c: The inductive step.
Let
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
. Suppose that

10.50
holds for pairs
(\leq n+1,k)
and

10.51
holds for pairs
(\leq n-1,k)
for all
k\geq 0

as well as for pairs
(n,\leq m-1)
(this latter condition is vacuous when
m=0
). We will show that
10.50
holds for the pair
(n+2,m)
and
10.51
holds for the pair
(n,m)
. In view of
the base cases established in Steps 3ab, this inductive step establishes

10.50
and
10.51
.

We first claim that

(10.56)

\begin{split}&\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n+2}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{m},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))},\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))})\\
&\qquad[\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{\leq m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}+\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c\theta{}))}].\end{split}

Indeed, this follows from
10.48
(since the last term in
square brackets in that estimate can be removed by induction). Next,
use
10.56
to control the last term in square brackets in the
estimate
10.49
and obtain

(10.57)

\begin{split}&u_{0}^{1/2}r_{0}^{1/2}\lVert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(\theta{}))}\lesssim\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{elliptic:rn2-prep}\text{{)}}.\end{split}

By the inductive hypotheses, we have

(10.58)

\begin{split}\text{{(LHS of }}\lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{elliptic:rn2-prep}\text{{)}}&\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+1},\lVert S^{n+m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c^{n+m+3}\theta{}))})\cdot C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})\lVert S^{\leq n+m+3}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{2}(\mathcal{R}(c^{n+m+3}\theta{}))}.\end{split}

To conclude the proof, control
\lVert S^{n+m+2}\varphi{}\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathcal{R}(c^{n+m+3}\theta{}))}\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})
and combine

10.58
,
10.57
and
10.56
.
∎

{lemma}

Let
n,m\geq 0
and let
0\leq k\leq 2
. We have

(10.59)

\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{k}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})\quad\text{in }\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}\cap\{r\geq u\}.

Proof.

The cases
k=0,1
follow from
10.37
, so we
focus on the case
k=2
.

Suppose for the sake of induction that we have already shown
10.59

for
k=2
and pairs
(n^{\prime},m^{\prime})
with
m^{\prime}<m
or
m=m^{\prime}
and
n^{\prime}<n
. Set
\psi{}_{0}=(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n+1}S^{m}\varphi{}
. By

sections

10.3.1
,
10.83
and
10.72
and the
induction hypothesis we have

(10.60)

\begin{split}\lvert r^{3}\Box{}\psi{}_{0}\rvert&\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+4})[r\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+3}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert+r\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+2}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert]\\
&\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})[r^{-1}\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-1}\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert]\\
&\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})[r^{-1}+r^{-1}\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert],\\
\end{split}

and by
10.37
, we have

(10.61)

\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})\psi{}_{0}\rvert\leq r^{-1}C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+4}).

A computation (starting from
7.12
) for general
\psi{}
shows that

(10.62)

\partial{}_{u}((r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})(r\psi{}))=r^{3}(-\gamma{})\Box{}\psi{}+2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)(-\gamma{})(r\overline{\partial}_{r})\psi{}-\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r}}{\frac{2}{r}}{2/r}{2/r}(-\nu{})(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})(r\psi{}).

Specialize this equation to
\psi{}=\psi_{0}
, integrate to
C^{\text{out}}
, where
the data for
\psi_{0}
vanishes, and use

10.60
and
10.61
and the inductive hypothesis to obtain

(10.63)

\begin{split}&\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}(u,v)\rvert=\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})(r(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n+1}S^{m}\varphi{})(u,v)\rvert\\
&\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})\Bigl[\int_{1}^{u}r^{-1}(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}+\int_{1}^{u}r^{-1}\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert(u^{\prime},v)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u^{\prime}\Bigr].\\
\end{split}

Grönwall’s inequality, the monotonicity
r^{-1}(u^{\prime},v)\leq r^{-1}(u,v)
for

u^{\prime}\in[1,u]
, and the estimate
ur^{-1}\leq 1
in the region of interest give

(10.64)

\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})^{2}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}(u,v)\rvert\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})ur^{-1}\exp(C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})ur^{-1})\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})ur^{-1},

This concludes the proof of
10.59
for
k=2
and the pair

(n,m)
.
∎

{lemma}

We have

(10.65)

\lvert U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})[\lvert US^{m}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-1}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-1}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert].

Proof.

Compute the wave equation (in the region
\{r\geq 2R_{\bullet}\}
)

(10.66)

U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}\varpi{},(-\gamma{}))[r\Box{}+U+r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})]

It follows that, for
n\geq 1
,

(10.67)

U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}=\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}\varpi{},(-\gamma{}))[r\Box{}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-1}S^{m}+U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-1}S^{m}+r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}].

It follows from
10.46
and
10.37
that
(in the region
\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}
)

(10.68)

\begin{split}\lvert r^{2}\Box{}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert&\lesssim C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+3})[\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+2}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert].\end{split}

Now
10.68
and
10.67
imply

(10.69)

\lvert U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})[r^{-1}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert+r^{-1}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-1}S^{m}\varphi{}\rvert]

We obtain
10.65
from
10.69
by induction on
n
.
∎

10.3.2.
Estimates for
(r\overline{\partial}_{r})
-derivatives of

\varpi{}

{lemma}

For
n\geq 1
and
m\geq 0
, we have

(10.70)

r\tau{}^{1-\epsilon{}}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert\leq C(\epsilon{},n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+3})\quad\text{in }\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}.

For
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, we have

(10.71)

\tau{}^{1-\epsilon{}}\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}\rvert\leq C(\epsilon{},\lvert\alpha{}\rvert,\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert})\quad\text{in }\{r\leq 5R_{\bullet}\}.

For
m\geq 1
, we have

(10.72)

\tau{}^{1-\epsilon{}}\lvert S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert\leq C(\epsilon{},n,)\quad\text{in }\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}.

Proof.

The transport equation for
\varpi{}
is

(10.73)

r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varpi{}=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}(1-\mu{})((r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}\varpi{})((r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}.

An induction argument implies that for
n\geq 1
, we have

(10.74)

r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\varpi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}S^{\leq m}\varpi{},r^{-1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{1\leq n-1}S^{\leq m}\varphi{})((r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{})^{2}.

Now
10.70
follows from

8.40
and
10.37
.

For
10.71
, specialize
8.47
to the region
\{r\leq 5R_{\bullet}\}
for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
,

(10.75)

\lvert\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}\varpi{}\rvert\leq\tau{}C(\mathfrak{B}_{<\alpha{}})\lvert\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\leq C(\epsilon{},\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert})\tau{}^{-1+\epsilon{}}.

It follows that, in the region
\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}
, for
m\geq 1
,

(10.76)

\begin{split}\lvert S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert(u,r)&\leq\lvert S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert(u,5R_{\bullet})+\int_{5R_{\bullet}}^{r}\lvert\overline{\partial}_{r}S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert(u,r^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(R_{\bullet})\lvert S^{m}\varpi{}\rvert(u,5R_{\bullet})+\int_{5R_{\bullet}}^{r}\mathcal{O}(r^{-1}S^{\leq m}\varpi{})\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u,r^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{m})\tau{}^{-1+\epsilon{}}+C(\mathfrak{D}_{m+3})\int_{5R_{\bullet}}^{r}r^{-2}\tau{}^{-1+\epsilon{}}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}\\
&\leq C(\mathfrak{D}_{m+3})\tau{}^{-1+\epsilon{}},\end{split}

which is
10.72
.
∎

{lemma}

[Asymptotic expansion of
\varpi
]
In
\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}\cap\{r\geq u\}
, we have

(10.77)

\varpi{}(u,r)=\varpi{}|_{\mathcal{I}}(u)+\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}(u,r),

where
\varpi_{\mathcal{I}}
satisfies

(10.78)

\lvert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\varpi{}|_{\mathcal{I}}(u)\rvert\lesssim C(\epsilon{},m,\mathfrak{D}_{m})\min(r^{\epsilon}{},u^{\epsilon}{}),

and the error
\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}
satisfies

(10.79)

\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}\rvert\lesssim C(\epsilon{},n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})r^{-1}\min(r^{\epsilon}{},u^{\epsilon}{}).

Proof.

Step 1: Proof of
10.79
.
Define

(10.80)

\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}(u,r)\coloneqq{}-\int_{r}^{\infty}\overline{\partial}_{r}\varpi{}(u,r^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-2}(r^{\prime 2}\overline{\partial}_{r})\varpi{}(u,r^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}=-\int_{r}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}r^{\prime-2}(1-\mu{})((r^{\prime 2}\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}(u,r^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime},

so that
10.77
holds with
\varpi_{\mathcal{I}}(u)\coloneqq{}\lim_{r\to\infty}\varpi{}(u,r)
. It follows from
10.72
and
10.59

(after replacing instances of
u\overline{\partial}_{u}
with
S-r\overline{\partial}_{r}
)
that, for
n\geq 1
,

(10.81)

\begin{split}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}\rvert&\lesssim r^{-2}\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{\leq m}(1-\mu{})\rvert\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n-1}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}\\
&\lesssim C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+4})r^{-2}.\end{split}

When
n=0
, we differentiate under the integral sign and use

10.72
,
8.40
and
10.59
(after replacing instances of

u\overline{\partial}_{u}
with
S-r\overline{\partial}_{r}
) to get

(10.82)

\begin{split}\lvert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}\rvert&\lesssim\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-2}\lvert(u\partial{}_{u})^{\leq m}(1-\mu{})\rvert\lvert(r^{\prime 2}\overline{\partial}_{r})(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}(u,r^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim C(\epsilon{},n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})\int_{r}^{\infty}r^{\prime-2}\min(r^{\epsilon}{},u^{\epsilon}{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}r^{\prime}\lesssim C(\epsilon{},n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})r^{-1}\min(r^{\epsilon}{},u^{\epsilon}{}).\end{split}

Use
10.81
and
10.82
to conclude

10.79
.

Step 2: Proof of
10.78
.
By similar arguments to those in

10.72
, one can use
10.59
to obtain

\lvert(r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r})(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\varpi{}\rvert\lesssim C(m,\mathfrak{D}_{m+5})
. This shows that

(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\varpi{}
is integrable in
r
towards

\mathcal{I}
, uniformly on compact subsets of
u
. It follows from a fact
in real analysis (as in the proof of
8.85
) that

(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\varpi{}|_{\mathcal{I}}(u)=\lim_{r\to\infty}((u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\varpi{})(u,r)
. Then
10.78

follows from
10.72
and
8.40
(after replacing
instances of
u\overline{\partial}_{u}
with
S-r\overline{\partial}_{r}
).
∎

10.3.3.
Estimates for
(r\overline{\partial}_{r})
-derivatives of
(-\gamma{})

{lemma}

For
n,m\geq 0
, we have

(10.83)

r\tau{}\lvert U^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+3})\quad\text{in }\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}.

Proof.

When
n=0
, this follows from
section

8.4
. When
n\geq 1
, the
transport equation for
\log(-\gamma{})
gives

(10.84)

\begin{split}\lvert U^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert&=U^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-1}S^{m}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})\log(-\gamma{})=\lvert U^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n-1}S^{m}((r\overline{\partial}_{r})\varphi{})^{2}\rvert\\
&\lesssim\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert U(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq n}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}.\end{split}

Use
10.65
and
10.37
, and the definition of the
pointwise norm
\mathcal{P}_{\alpha{},1}\leq C(\lvert\alpha{}\rvert,\mathfrak{D}_{\lvert\alpha{}\rvert})
to estimate

(10.85)

\begin{split}\lvert U^{\leq 1}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}S^{m}\log(-\gamma{})\rvert&\lesssim C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+2})[\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}S^{\leq m}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n+1}S^{\leq m-1}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}+\lvert US^{m}\varphi{}\rvert^{2}]\\
&\lesssim C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+3})r^{-1}\tau{}^{-1}.\end{split}

∎

{lemma}

[Asymptotic expansion of
(-\gamma{})
]
In
\{r\geq 5R_{\bullet}\}\cap\{r\geq u\}
, we have

(10.86)

(-\gamma{})(u,r)=1+r^{-2}\mathcal{F}_{(-\gamma{})}(u)+\mathcal{E}_{(-\gamma{})}(u,r),

where
\mathcal{F}_{(-\gamma{})}
and
\mathcal{E}_{(-\gamma{})}
satisfy

(10.87)

\lvert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\mathcal{F}_{(-\gamma{})}\rvert\leq C(m,\mathfrak{D}_{m+5})

and

(10.88)

\lvert(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\mathcal{E}_{(-\gamma{})}\rvert\leq C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})r^{-3}

Proof.

Step 1: Preliminary computations and the derivation of

10.86
.
Write
K=r^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}
. Compute

(10.89)

\displaystyle K(-\gamma{})

\displaystyle=r^{-1}(K\varphi{})^{2}(-\gamma{})

(10.90)

\displaystyle K^{2}(-\gamma{})

\displaystyle=-(K\varphi{})^{2}(-\gamma{})+r^{-1}K^{2}\varphi{}\cdot K\varphi{}(-\gamma{})+r^{-2}(K\varphi{})^{4}(-\gamma{})

\displaystyle K^{3}(-\gamma{})

\displaystyle=-3K^{2}\varphi{}\cdot K\varphi{}(-\gamma{})-3r^{-1}(K\varphi{})^{4}(-\gamma{})+r^{-1}(K^{2}\varphi{})^{2}(-\gamma{})+5r^{-2}K^{2}\varphi{}\cdot(K\varphi{})^{3}(-\gamma{})

(10.91)

\displaystyle\qquad+r^{-3}(K\varphi{})^{6}(-\gamma{})+(r\overline{\partial}_{r})K^{2}\varphi{}\cdot K\varphi{}(-\gamma{})

Next, define

(10.92)

\mathcal{E}_{(-\gamma{})}(u,r)\coloneqq{}\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho{}^{-2}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\rho{}^{\prime-2}\int_{\rho{}^{\prime}}^{\infty}\rho{}^{\prime\prime-2}K^{3}(-\gamma{})(u,\rho{}^{\prime\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{}^{\prime\prime}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{}^{\prime}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{},

so that

(10.93)

\begin{split}1-(-\gamma{})(u,r)&=\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho{}^{-2}K(-\gamma{})(u,\rho{})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{}=-r^{-1}\lim_{r\to\infty}K(-\gamma{})(u,r)-\int_{r}^{\infty}\rho{}^{-2}\int_{\rho}^{\infty}\rho{}^{\prime-2}K^{2}(-\gamma{})(u,\rho{}^{\prime})\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{}^{\prime}\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}\rho{}\\
&=-r^{-1}\lim_{r\to\infty}K(-\gamma{})(u,r)-2r^{-2}\lim_{r\to\infty}K^{2}(-\gamma{})(u,r)-\mathcal{E}_{(-\gamma{})}(u,r).\end{split}

From
10.89
and
10.90
, the boundedness statement of
10.59
, and the gauge condition
(-\gamma{})|_{\mathcal{I}}=1
, we see that

(10.94)

\lim_{r\to\infty}K(-\gamma{})(u,r)=0,\qquad\lim_{r\to\infty}K^{2}(-\gamma{})(u,r)=-\lim_{r\to\infty}(K\varphi{})^{2}(u,r)

It follows that
10.86
holds for
\mathcal{F}_{(-\gamma{})}(u)=-2\lim_{r\to\infty}(K\varphi{})^{2}(u,r)
.

Step 2: Proof of
10.87
.
This is proved with a similar method to

10.78
. The important points are that, by
10.59
,
K(K\varphi{})^{2}
is bounded
(which provides the integrability in
r
towards infinity, uniformly on
compact subsets of
u
), and
K(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{\leq m}\varphi{}
is bounded (since we
can replace instances of
u\overline{\partial}_{u}
by
S-r\overline{\partial}_{r}
).

Step 3: Proof of
10.88
.
All terms on the right side of

10.91
are bounded by
C(n,m,\mathfrak{D}_{n+m+5})
after applications
of
(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}
, by

10.59
(since we can replace instances of

u\overline{\partial}_{u}
by
S-r\overline{\partial}_{r}
). It follows that
all terms in

(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{m}\mathcal{E}_{(-\gamma{})}

are bounded by
r^{-3}
(with the constant just mentioned).
∎

10.4.
Retrieving the assumptions of the work of Luk–Oh on late-time tails

10.4.1.
Notation of the work of Luk–Oh

We define notation as in
[
29
, Sec. 2.1.1–2.1.3]
. Define the following parameters:

•

R_{\text{far}}=5R_{\bullet}
,

•

M_{c}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

•

\delta{}_{c}=1/2

•

J_{c}=3
,

•

K_{c}=0
,

•

\eta_{c}=1
,

•

M_{0}\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

•

A_{0}=C(\varpi_{i},c_{\mathcal{H}},r_{\text{min}},M_{0},\mathfrak{D}_{M_{0}+5})
,

•

\alpha_{0}=3/4
,

•

\nu_{0}=1/4
,

•

D=C(\mathfrak{D}_{M_{0}})
,

•

\alpha_{d}=4
,

•

\delta_{d}\in(0,1]
,

•

J_{d}=3
,

•

K_{d}=0
,

•

\eta_{d}=1
,

Let
\mathbf{g}=g
be the spacetime metric on
\mathcal{M}
. We now define
Cartesian coordinates on
\mathcal{M}
. Let
(\theta{},\phi{})
be the
standard local coordinate system on
S^{2}
. Set

(10.95)

\begin{split}x^{0}&=\chi{}(r)(2v-r)+(1-\chi{}(r))(2u+r)\end{split}

and
x^{1}=r\sin\theta{}\cos\phi{}
,
x^{2}=r\sin\theta{}\sin\phi{}
, and
x^{3}=r\cos\theta{}
. Set

(10.96)

\mathbf{T}\coloneqq{}\partial{}_{x^{0}},

where
\partial_{x^{0}}
is defined with respect to the coordinates

(x^{0},x^{1},x^{2},x^{3})
. Define

(10.97)

\bar{u}\coloneqq{}x^{0}-r+3R_{\text{far}}\qquad\overline{\tau{}}\coloneqq{}x^{0}-\chi{}_{>4R_{\text{far}}}(r)(r-3R_{\text{far}})

for a cutoff function
\chi_{>4R_{\text{far}}}(r)
that is
0
when
r\leq 2R_{\text{far}}
and
1
when
r\geq 4R_{\text{far}}
. In particular, observe
that

(10.98)

\bar{u}=2u+3{R_{\text{far}}}\quad\text{in }\{r\geq R_{\text{far}}\},

and

(10.99)

\mathbf{T}\overline{\tau{}}=1,\quad\text{and }\overline{\tau{}}=\begin{cases}x^{0},&\text{in }\{r\leq 2R_{\text{far}}\},\\
\bar{u},&\text{in }\{r\geq 4R_{\text{far}}\}.\end{cases}

Write
(\partial_{\tau}^{(\tau{})},\partial_{r}^{(\tau{})})
for the coordinate derivatives in the

(\tau{},r)
coordinate system and

(\underline{\partial{}}_{\bar{u}},\overline{\partial}_{r})
for the coordinate
derivatives in
(\bar{u},r)
-coordinates. We will write
\mathcal{M}=\mathcal{M}_{\text{near}}\cup\mathcal{M}_{\text{med}}\cup\mathcal{M}_{\text{wave}}
for

(10.100)

\begin{split}\mathcal{M}_{\text{near}}&\coloneqq{}\{(u,r)\in\mathcal{M}:r\leq 2R_{\text{far}},\overline{\tau{}}\geq 1\},\\
\mathcal{M}_{\text{med}}&\coloneqq{}\{(u,r)\in\mathcal{M}:R_{\text{far}}\leq r\leq 400\bar{u},\overline{\tau{}}\geq 1\},\\
\mathcal{M}_{\text{wave}}&\coloneqq{}\{(u,r)\in\mathcal{M}:r\geq 4\bar{u},\overline{\tau{}}\geq 1\}.\\
\end{split}

and write
\mathcal{M}_{\text{far}}\coloneqq{}\mathcal{M}_{\text{med}}\cup\mathcal{M}_{\text{wave}}
.

10.4.2.
Global assumptions on the spacetime

Assumption
(G1)
is clearly satisfied. Assumption
(G2)
is satisfied, since the only boundary component of

\mathcal{M}
is the null event horizon. Finally, it is easy to check the causality condition
(G3)
based on the
definition of
x^{0}
.

10.4.3.
Assumptions on the metric

Define the absolute value of a
(2,0)
-tensor as in

[
29
, Sec. 2.1.4]
. A tedious but straightforward computation
involving the expression for a Lie derivative in local coordinates, the chain
rule, the definitions of our schematic geometric quantities, and the results of

section

9
yields the following expression for these
absolute values in terms of components in
(u,r)
and
(r,v)
-coordinates.

{lemma}

Fix a function
m(\overline{\tau{}},r)
. Let
\mathbf{a}
be
(2,0)
-tensor

(10.101)

\mathbf{a}=\mathbf{a}^{ur}(\overline{\partial}_{u}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{u})+\mathbf{a}^{rr}\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+r^{-2}\not{\mathbf{a}}

for
\not{\mathbf{a}}^{AB}
a
(2,0)
-tensor on
S^{2}
. Then in

\mathcal{M}_{\text{far}}
we have

(10.102)

\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\tau{}\mathbf{T}}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{\langle{}r\rangle{}\partial{}_{r}^{(\tau{})}}^{m}\mathbf{a}\rvert\lesssim_{n,m}C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m})[\lvert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{\leq m}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}\mathbf{a}^{ur}\rvert+\lvert(u\overline{\partial}_{u})^{\leq m}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{\leq n}\mathbf{a}^{rr}\rvert+r^{-2}\lvert\not{\mathbf{a}}^{AB}\rvert].

Similarly, if
\mathbf{b}
takes the form

(10.103)

\mathbf{b}=\mathbf{b}^{vr}(\underline{\partial}_{v}\otimes\underline{\partial}_{r}+\underline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\underline{\partial}_{v})+\mathbf{b}^{rr}\underline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\underline{\partial}_{r}+r^{-2}\not{\mathbf{b}},

then in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{near}}
we have

(10.104)

\lvert\mathcal{L}_{\tau{}\mathbf{T}}^{n}\mathcal{L}_{\langle{}r\rangle{}\partial{}_{r}^{(\tau{})}}^{m}\mathbf{b}\rvert\lesssim_{n,m}C(\mathfrak{D}_{n+m})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n^{\prime},m^{\prime}\geq 0\\
n^{\prime}+m^{\prime}=n+m\end{subarray}}[\lvert S^{\leq m^{\prime}}D^{\leq n^{\prime}}\mathbf{b}^{vr}\rvert+\lvert S^{\leq m^{\prime}}D^{\leq n^{\prime}}\mathbf{b}^{rr}\rvert+r^{-2}\lvert\not{\mathbf{b}}^{AB}\rvert.

Finally, if
a
is a spherically symmetric smooth function, such that

(10.105)

\sum_{m+n\leq N}\lvert S^{m}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}a\rvert\lesssim m(\overline{\tau{}},r)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{far}},\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{m+n\leq N}\lvert S^{m}D^{n}a\rvert\lesssim m(\tau{},r)\quad\text{in }\mathcal{M}_{\mathrm{near}},

then

(10.106)

a=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{N}(m(\tau{},r)),

where the
O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}
notation is defined as in
[
29
, Sec. 2.1.4]
.

One computes

(10.107)

\mathbf{g}^{-1}=-\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{1}{(-\gamma{})}}{1/(-\gamma{})}{1/(-\gamma{})}(\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}})+(1-\mu{})\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+r^{-2}\mathring{\not{\gamma{}}}^{-1}

and

(10.108)

\mathbf{g}^{-1}=\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}(\underline{\partial{}}_{r}\otimes\underline{\partial{}}_{v}+\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\otimes\underline{\partial{}}_{r})+(1-\mu{})\underline{\partial{}}_{r}\otimes\underline{\partial{}}_{r}+r^{-2}\mathring{\not{\gamma{}}}^{-1}.

for
\mathring{\not{\gamma{}}}
the round metric on the unit sphere.

In
\mathcal{M}_{\text{near}}
, by

sections

8.3
,
10.108
and
10.4.3
, we have

(10.109)

\mathbf{g}^{-1}=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{c}}(1),

which satisfies assumption
(\mathbf{g}\mathbf{B}V1)
.

For
\mathbf{m}^{-1}
the inverse Minkowski metric, we have

(10.110)

\mathbf{g}^{-1}-\mathbf{m}^{-1}=(1-(-\gamma{})^{-1})(\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}})-\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{2\varpi{}/r}{2\varpi{}/r}-\mathchoice{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}\Bigr)\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}

By
10.88
,
10.79
,
10.107
and
10.4.3
, and a Taylor
expansion, we have

(10.111)

\mathbf{g}^{-1}-\mathbf{m}^{-1}=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{c}}(r^{-1+\epsilon{}})\quad\text{in }\mathcal{M}_{\text{med}},

where the implicit constant depends on
\epsilon{}
,
M_{c}
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{M_{c}+5}
. This satisfies assumption
(\mathbf{gB}V2)
(with
\delta_{c}=1/2
).

By
10.88
and
10.79
and Taylor expansion, in

\mathcal{M}_{\text{wave}}
we have the asymptotics

(10.112)

\begin{split}\mathbf{g}^{\overline{u}r}&=-1+r^{-2}\mathring{\mathbf{h}}^{\overline{u}r}_{2,0}(u)+\varrho{}_{3}[\mathbf{h}^{\overline{u}r}],\\
\mathbf{g}^{rr}&=r^{-1}\mathring{\mathbf{h}}^{rr}_{1,0}(u)+\varrho{}_{2}[\mathbf{h}^{rr}],\\
r^{2}\mathbf{g}^{AB}&=\mathring{\not{\gamma{}}}^{AB},\end{split}

where the unlisted components vanish, and where, in the notation of
[
29
, Sec. 2.1.4]
,

(10.113)

\begin{split}\mathring{\mathbf{h}}^{\overline{u}r}_{2,0}&=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}^{M_{c}}}(1),\quad\text{and}\quad\mathring{\mathbf{h}}^{rr}_{1,0}=2\varpi{}|_{\mathcal{I}}(u)=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}(u^{\epsilon}{}),\end{split}

and

(10.114)

\varrho_{3}[\mathbf{h}^{\overline{u}r}]=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{c}}(r^{-3}),\quad\text{and}\quad\varrho_{2}[\mathbf{h}^{rr}]=\mathcal{E}_{\varpi}{}+r^{-2}\mathbf{e}^{2}=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{c}}(r^{-2}u^{\epsilon}{}).

This satisfies assumption
(\mathbf{gB}V3)
(with
\eta_{c}=1
and
\delta_{c}=1/2
).

10.4.4.
Stationary estimate

The assumption
(SE1)
is trivially satisfied, since
\mathcal{M}

has no timelike boundary components.

Define the inverse metric

(10.115)

{}^{(\infty)}\mathbf{g}^{-1}\coloneqq{}-(\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}})+\Bigl(1-\mathchoice{\frac{\varpi{}_{f}}{r}}{\frac{\varpi{}_{f}}{r}}{\varpi{}_{f}/r}{\varpi{}_{f}/r}+\mathchoice{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}\Bigr)\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+r^{-2}\mathring{\not{\gamma{}}}^{-1}

in
(\bar{u},r)
-coordinates. Since
\mathbf{T}r=0
, we have the
stationarity property
\mathcal{L}_{\mathbf{T}}(^{(\infty)}\mathbf{g})=0
.
The stationary estimate for the Reissner–Nordström metric

{}^{(\infty)}\mathbf{g}
(with mass
\varpi_{f}
and charge
\mathbf{e}
)
follows from the methods in
[
2
, Sec. 7]
(see also the discussion in

[
29
, Ex. 3.6]
). As for the convergence of
\mathbf{g}^{-1}
to

{}^{(\infty)}\mathbf{g}^{-1}
, we have

(10.116)

\mathbf{g}-\,^{(\infty)}\mathbf{g}^{-1}\coloneqq{}(1-(-\gamma{})^{-1})(\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}+\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{\bar{u}})+\mathchoice{\frac{\varpi{}-\varpi{}_{f}}{r}}{\frac{\varpi{}-\varpi{}_{f}}{r}}{\varpi{}-\varpi{}_{f}/r}{\varpi{}-\varpi{}_{f}/r}\overline{\partial}_{r}\otimes\overline{\partial}_{r}.

On
\Sigma{}_{\bar{\tau{}}}\cap\{r\geq R_{\text{far}}\}
(where
\Sigma{}_{\bar{\tau{}}}
is defined as
in
[
29
, Sec. 2.1.3]
), the assumption
(SE2)
(with

\delta_{c}=1/2
) follows from

10.83
and
10.72
(and an easy decay estimate
for
\varpi{}-\varpi_{f}
that follows from the energy decay established in

section

6
). The verification of assumption
(SE2)
in
the region
\Sigma{}_{\tau{}}\cap\{r\leq R_{\text{far}}\}
is done in

(v,r)
-coordinates, and it is similar (but uses
section

8.3
). Thus
assumption
(SE2)
holds.

10.4.5.
Assumptions on the initial data

The assumption
\text{(D}_{\Sigma_{1}}\text{)}
on the initial data is satisfied for
our compactly supported data (with
D=C(\mathfrak{D}_{M_{0}})
), for any

\alpha_{d}\in\mathbf{R}
and
\delta{}_{d}\in(0,1]
. Note that, although the data hypersurface
\Sigma_{1}

leaves the region
\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}
, the scalar field vanishes in the region
\Sigma_{1}\cap\mathcal{R}_{\text{char}}^{c}
.

10.4.6.
Assumptions on the scalar field

By the results of
section

9
and
section

10.4.3
,
we have

(10.117)

\lvert S^{m}D^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim v^{-1+\epsilon{}}\implies\varphi{}=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{0}}(A_{0}\bar{\tau{}}^{-1+\epsilon{}})\quad\text{in }\mathcal{M}_{\text{near}}.

By
10.37
and
10.4.3
, we have

(10.118)

\lvert S^{m}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim u^{-1+\epsilon{}}\implies\varphi{}=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{0}}(A_{0}\bar{u}^{-1+\epsilon{}})\quad\text{in }\mathcal{M}_{\text{med}}.

By
10.37
and
10.4.3
, we have

(10.119)

\lvert S^{m}(r\overline{\partial}_{r})^{n}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim r^{-1/2-\epsilon{}}u^{-1+2\epsilon{}}\implies\varphi{}=O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{M_{0}}(A_{0}r^{-1/2-\epsilon{}}\bar{u}^{-1+2\epsilon{}})\quad\text{in }\mathcal{M}_{\text{wave}}.

Taking
\epsilon{}=1/4
, the above three implications show that assumption

(S)
is satisfied with
\alpha_{0}=3/4
and
\nu_{0}=1/4
.

10.5.
Obtaining a late-time tails result from the work of Luk–Oh

We begin by showing that the first higher radiation field vanishes (i.e. the
radiation field
\Phi{}
has no
r^{-1}
term in its expansion).

{lemma}

In the notation of
[
29
, Sec. 2.3]
, we have

(10.120)

\mathring{\Phi{}}_{1,0}(u)\equiv 0.

Proof.

Define
\Phi{}\coloneqq{}r\varphi{}
. From
7.12
, we have

(10.121)

\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}\Phi{}=-\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{3}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{3}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{3}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{3}}\kappa{}(-\nu{})\Phi{}\implies\partial{}_{u}(r^{3}\partial{}_{v}\Phi{})+\mathchoice{\frac{3}{r}}{\frac{3}{r}}{3/r}{3/r}(-\nu{})r^{3}\partial{}_{v}\Phi{}=-2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)\kappa{}(-\nu{})\Phi{}=O(u^{-1/2+\epsilon{}}).

By Grönwall’s inequality and the vanishing of the data on
C^{\text{out}}
, we have

(10.122)

\lvert r^{3}\partial{}_{v}\Phi{}\rvert\lesssim u^{1/2+\epsilon{}}e^{Cu/r}.

In particular, if we define
\mathring{\Phi{}}_{0}(u)\coloneqq{}\lim_{v\to\infty}\Phi{}(u,v)
, then
(for
r(u,v)\geq u
) we have

(10.123)

\lvert\Phi{}(u,v)-\mathring{\Phi{}}_{0}(u)\rvert\leq\int_{v}^{\infty}\lvert\partial{}_{v}\Phi{}(u,v^{\prime})\rvert\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}v^{\prime}\lesssim r^{-2}u^{1/2+\epsilon{}}.

In particular, it follows that

(10.124)

\mathring{\Phi{}}_{1,0}(u)\coloneqq{}\lim_{v\to\infty}r(u,v)(\Phi{}(u,v)-\mathring{\Phi{}}_{0}(u))\equiv 0,

as desired.
∎

10.2
Sharp Price’s law result.

There exists a universal large constant
N\geq 0
and small constants
\epsilon{},\delta{}>0

such that for every
k\geq 0
, we have the following asymptotic along the event
horizon

(10.125)

\lvert(v\underline{\partial{}}_{v})^{k}\varphi{}|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)-C_{k}\mathfrak{L}v^{-3}\rvert=O(v^{-3-\delta{}}),

where the implicit constant depends on
\varpi_{i}
,
c_{\mathcal{H}}
,

r_{\mathrm{min}}
,
k
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{\max(N,\epsilon^{-1}k)}
. Moreover,
for
0\leq k\leq 2
, we have

(10.126)

\lvert(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{k}\varphi{}|_{\mathcal{H}}(v)-C_{k}\mathfrak{L}\bar{v}^{-3}\rvert=O(\bar{v}^{-3-\delta{}}),

where
\bar{v}
and
\partial_{\bar{v}}
are the coordinate and associated
derivative in the Eddington–Finkelstein-type gauge (see

section

2.4
), and where the implied constant depends on the same
parameters as in
10.125
.

Proof.

Define
J_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq{}2
and
\alpha_{\mathfrak{f}}\coloneqq{}3
. Then
[
29
, Eq. (2.83)]
holds. Evidently, the asymptotic spatial profile
defined in
[
29
, Eq. (2.90)]
is identically equal to
1
. In
spherical symmetry and with
K_{1}=0
, we have

(10.127)

\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\bullet{}}}}{{\Phi{}}}_{(0)1,0}\!(u)=\,\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathclap{\bullet{}}}}{{\Phi{}}}_{1,0}\!(u)=\mathring{\Phi{}}_{1,0}(u),

so
10.120
implies
[
29
, Eq. (2.73)]
. In the
previous section we showed that all the main assumptions of
[
29
]

are satisfied. An appeal to
[
29
, Main Theorem 4]
shows that there
exists
\epsilon{}>0
small,
M\geq 0
large, and an explicit constant
C\neq{}0
such
that for
M_{0},M_{c}\geq M
and
\delta{}>0
sufficiently small, we have

(10.128)

\varphi{}(\overline{\tau{}},r)=C\mathfrak{L}\overline{\tau{}}^{-3}+O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{\lfloor\epsilon{}M\rfloor}((D+A_{0})\overline{\tau{}}^{-3-\delta{}})\quad\text{in }\{r\leq\overline{\tau{}}^{1-2\delta{}}\},

where
\mathfrak{L}=\int_{\mathcal{I}}2\varpi{}|_{\mathcal{I}}(u)\varphi{}(u)\mathop{}\!\mathrm{d}{}u
is non-zero
for solutions arising from generic Cauchy data, by
[
32
, Thm. 4.3]
(to
relate this expression for
\mathfrak{L}
to the notion of final asymptotic
charge in
[
29
, Sec. 2.r5]
, one integrates the recurrence equations
in
[
29
, Sec. 2.3]
). Specializing to a region
\{r\leq R_{0}\}
, where

\overline{\tau{}}=2v-r
, and performing a Taylor expansion, we get

(10.129)

\varphi{}(r,v)=C\mathfrak{L}v^{-3}+O_{\mathbf{\Gamma{}}}^{\lfloor\epsilon{}M\rfloor}(v^{-3-\delta{}}),

where the implicit constant depends on
\varpi_{i}
,
c_{\mathcal{H}}
,

r_{\text{min}}
, and
\mathfrak{D}_{M}
. Differentiating this asymptotic
yields
10.125
.

For the result in the Eddington–Finkelstein-type gauge, one notes that the
argument of
9.22
(see also

9.34
and
9.25
) provides, on the event horizon,

(10.130)

\begin{split}S-\bar{v}\partial_{\bar{v}}&=\rho{}S+(1-\rho{})v\lambda{}U\\
S^{2}-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{2}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a,b,c\leq 1\\
a+b+c\geq 1\end{subarray}}(S^{\leq 1}\rho{})^{a}(S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{}))^{b}(v\lambda{}U(v\lambda{}))^{c}\{U,S\}^{1\leq 2},\\
\end{split}

for some quantity
\rho{}
, together with the estimates

(10.131)

\lvert S^{\leq 1}\rho{}\rvert\lesssim v^{-1},\qquad\lvert S^{\leq 1}(v\lambda{})\rvert\lesssim v^{-1},\qquad\lvert v\lambda{}U(v\lambda{})\rvert\lesssim v^{-1}.

The upshot is that for
0\leq k\leq 2
,

(10.132)

\lvert S^{k}\varphi{}-(\bar{v}\partial{}_{\bar{v}})^{k}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim v^{-1}\lvert\{U,S\}^{1\leq 2}\varphi{}\rvert\lesssim v^{-4},

where the final estimate is due to
10.129
. Noting that
S=v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}
on the horizon,
10.126
now follows from
the above equation and
10.125
.
∎

Appendix A
Proofs of vector field commutator calculations

Proof of
section

2.8.1
.

From

(A.1)

V=(\chi{}+\lambda{}^{-1}(1-\chi{}))\partial{}_{v}+\chi{}\lambda{}U,

we obtain
2.46
and

(A.2)

V-\overline{\partial}_{r}=\chi{}(\lambda{}-1)\overline{\partial}_{r}+\chi{}\lambda{}U,

which gives
2.47
. From
A.1
, one obtains
2.48
:

(A.3)

\partial{}_{v}=F^{-1}V-F^{-1}\chi{}\lambda{}U\qquad\text{for }F=(\chi{}+(1-\chi{})/\lambda{})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}.

∎

Proof of
2.49
.

One obtains
2.49
by computing

(A.4)

S=\chi{}(v\partial{}_{v}+v\lambda{}U)+(1-\chi{})((r+u(-\nu{}))\overline{\partial}_{r}+u(-\nu{})U)

and using
2.48
.
∎

Proof of
section

2.8.2
.

Use the transport equations for
(-\nu{})
and
\lambda{}
to compute

(A.5)

[\overline{\partial}_{r},U]=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\{1,\varpi{},(1-\mu{})^{-1}\}[\overline{\partial}_{r}+U],

and then use
\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}(1-\mu{})^{-1}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}
and
\varpi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{g}_{0}
to get
2.51
.

We have

(A.6)

[\overline{\partial}_{r},\underline{\partial{}}_{v}]=[\overline{\partial}_{r},\partial_{v}]+[\overline{\partial}_{r},\lambda{}U]=\lambda^{-2}\partial{}_{v}\lambda{}\partial{}_{v}+\lambda{}^{-1}\partial{}_{v}\lambda{}U+\lambda{}[\partial{}_{r},U].

It follows that

(A.7)

[\overline{\partial}_{r},V]=\chi{}^{\prime}(\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-\overline{\partial}_{r})+\chi{}\lambda{}^{-2}\partial{}_{v}\lambda{}\partial{}_{v}+\chi{}\lambda{}^{-1}\partial{}_{v}\lambda{}U+\chi{}\lambda{}[\overline{\partial}_{r},U].

Now use
2.48
, write
\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=\lambda{}[\overline{\partial}_{r}+U]
, and use
2.51
to obtain
2.52
.

Now we compute
[\overline{\partial}_{r},S]
. First, compute

(A.8)

\begin{split}[\overline{\partial}_{r},S]&=\chi{}^{\prime}(v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-(r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}))+\chi{}[\overline{\partial}_{r},v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}]+(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
&=\underbrace{\chi{}^{\prime}(v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-(r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}))}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}}+\underbrace{\chi{}v\overline{\partial}_{r}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v}}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(II)}}+(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
\end{split}

We now treat term
(I)
. Observe that

(A.9)

\overline{\partial}_{r}=\lambda{}^{-1}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-U\qquad\overline{\partial}_{u}=\mathchoice{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}.

From this we claim that

(A.10)

\text{(I)}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}D

Indeed, we have

(A.11)

\begin{split}\text{(I)}&=\chi{}^{\prime}\Bigl(v-u\mathchoice{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}-\mathchoice{\frac{r}{\lambda{}}}{\frac{r}{\lambda{}}}{r/\lambda{}}{r/\lambda{}}\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v}+\chi{}^{\prime}rU\\
&=\chi{}^{\prime}(v-u-r)\underline{\partial{}}_{v}+\chi{}^{\prime}(\mathchoice{\frac{u}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{u}{\kappa{}}}{u/\kappa{}}{u/\kappa{}}(\kappa{}-(-\gamma{}))+r(1-\lambda{}^{-1}))+\chi{}^{\prime}rU\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}+\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}U,\end{split}

and using
\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=\partial_{v}+\lambda{}U
and
2.48
gives

A.10
. For term
(II)
, write

(A.12)

\text{(II)}=\chi{}v\overline{\partial}_{r}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}(\chi{}(-\gamma{})^{-1}vD\kappa{}+\chi{}\kappa{}(-\gamma{})^{-2}vD(-\gamma{}))D,

so that

(A.13)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\text{(II)}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{0}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{V}D.

Combine
A.10
and
A.13
with
A.8
to
conclude
2.53
.
∎

Proof of
section

2.8.2
.

First we compute
[U,V]
. Since
U
and
\underline{\partial{}}_{v}
commute, we have

(A.14)

[U,V]=\chi{}^{\prime}(\overline{\partial}_{r}-\underline{\partial{}}_{v})+(1-\chi{})[U,\overline{\partial}_{r}].

Use
2.51
and
2.48
to conclude.

Next, we compute
[U,S]
. Since
U
and
v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}
commute, we have

(A.15)

[U,S]=-\chi{}^{\prime}(v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-(r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}))+(1-\chi{})[U,r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}].

As shown in
A.10
, the first term is of the schematic form

\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}D
. To treat the second term, we
compute using
A.9
that

(A.16)

[U,\overline{\partial}_{r}]=-\mathchoice{\frac{U\lambda{}}{\lambda{}^{2}}}{\frac{U\lambda{}}{\lambda{}^{2}}}{U\lambda{}/\lambda{}^{2}}{U\lambda{}/\lambda{}^{2}}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\{1,\varpi{},(1-\mu{})^{-1}\}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\qquad[U,\overline{\partial}_{u}]=U\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v},

which implies

(A.17)

\begin{split}[U,r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}]&=-\overline{\partial}_{r}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})}}{\frac{1}{(-\nu{})}}{1/(-\nu{})}{1/(-\nu{})}\overline{\partial}_{u}+\Bigl(-r\mathchoice{\frac{U\lambda{}}{\lambda{}^{2}}}{\frac{U\lambda{}}{\lambda{}^{2}}}{U\lambda{}/\lambda{}^{2}}{U\lambda{}/\lambda{}^{2}}+uU\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}\Bigr)\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v}.\end{split}

Since
-\overline{\partial}_{r}+(-\nu{})^{-1}\overline{\partial}_{u}=U
and the coefficient of the
final term consists has the structure
r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{0}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{U}
, the desired
2.55
follows from
A.15
.

Now we compute
[V,S]
. Use
A.9
to obtain the
following identities:

(A.18)

\displaystyle[\underline{\partial{}}_{v},v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}]

\displaystyle=\underline{\partial{}}_{v},

(A.19)

\displaystyle[\underline{\partial{}}_{v},r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}]

\displaystyle=\Bigl(r\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\lambda{}^{-1}+u\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}\Bigr)+1\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v},

(A.20)

\displaystyle[\overline{\partial}_{r},v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}]

\displaystyle=v\overline{\partial}_{r}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}\Bigr)\overline{\partial}_{u},

(A.21)

\displaystyle[\overline{\partial}_{r},r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}]

\displaystyle=\overline{\partial}_{r}.

It follows that

(A.22)

\chi{}[\underline{\partial{}}_{v},S]-\chi{}^{2}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=\chi{}(1-\chi{})\Bigl(r\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\lambda{}^{-1}+u\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})/\kappa{}}\Bigr)+1\Bigr)\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{V}D.

and

(A.23)

(1-\chi{})[\overline{\partial}_{r},S]-(1-\chi{})^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}=\chi{}^{\prime}(1-\chi{})(v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-(r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}))+\chi{}(1-\chi{})v\overline{\partial}_{r}\Bigl(\mathchoice{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\kappa{}}{(-\gamma{})}}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}{\kappa{}/(-\gamma{})}\Bigr)\overline{\partial}_{u}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{V}D

To conclude, note that

(A.24)

[V,S]-\chi{}^{2}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-(1-\chi{})^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}D.

and

(A.25)

\chi{}^{2}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}+(1-\chi{})^{2}\overline{\partial}_{r}-V=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}D.

∎

Proof of
2.60
.

The strategy is to use an induction argument to simultaneously prove the
following two estimates

(A.26)

\displaystyle[U,L]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S+U})[U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}-V}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S+U}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}]

(A.27)

\displaystyle[V,L]

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S+U})r^{-2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}.

We then prove
2.60
assuming
A.26
and
A.27
. Observe that

2.57
,
2.58
and
2.59
follow immediately from

A.26
and
A.27
and the definition of
\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}{}
(see

2.43
). To streamline the notation, we schematically write
“
\text{good}^{U}_{\beta}{}
” (resp. “
\text{good}^{V}_{\beta}{}
” and
“
\text{good}^{V^{2}}_{\beta}{}
”) for terms appearing on the right side of

A.26
(resp.
A.27
and
2.60
) for the multi-index
\beta{}
. Observe
that
\text{good}^{U}_{\beta}{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}^{U}_{\alpha}{}
whenever
\beta{}\leq\alpha{}
(and the analogous statements hold for the schematic quantities
“
\text{good}^{V}_{\beta}{}
” and “
\text{good}^{V^{2}}_{\beta}{}
”).

Step 1: Proof of
A.26
and
A.27
.
Both sides of both equations vanish when

\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=0
. when
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
, both statements follow from

section

2.8.2
.

Now suppose inductively that
A.26
and
A.27
hold for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}
and
\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}
(with
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert,\lvert\alpha{}^{\prime}\rvert\geq 1
).
We will show that they hold for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}
.
Before proceeding, we note that
A.26
and
A.27
for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}
imply

(A.28)

[D,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S+U})D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}.

Step 1a: Proof of
A.26
.
The induction hypothesis for
A.26
with multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}
implies

(A.29)

\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+U})[U,\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}]\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}^{U}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}

We now use
A.26
for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}
to compute

(A.30)

\begin{split}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}[U,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}}]&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+U})[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-V}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-V}\\
&\qquad+r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+U}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+U})[[U,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-V}[D,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-V}\\
&\qquad+r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+U}[D,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}]+\text{good}^{U}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}^{U}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

In passing to the last line, we noted that the first term in square brackets on
the second line is good by
A.29
, and the other two terms are good by

A.28
. Summing
A.29
and
A.30
concludes the
proof of
A.26
for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}
.

Step 1b: Proof of
A.27
.
The induction hypothesis for
A.27
with
multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}
implies

(A.31)

[V,\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}]\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}^{V}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.

We now use
A.27
for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}
and

A.31
and
A.28
for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}
to compute

(A.32)

\begin{split}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}[V,\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}}]&=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+U})r^{-2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-U}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}^{V}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

In passing to the final equality we used

A.31
and
A.28
. Add
A.31
and
A.32
to
establish
A.27
for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}
.

Step 2: Proof of
2.60
given
A.26
and
A.27
.
We now show that

2.60
holds for multi-indices
\leq\alpha{}
if
A.26
and
A.27
do.

(A.33)

\begin{split}V[V,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]&=_{\mathrm{s}}V^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-S+V+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S+U+V})r^{-2}VD\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}}^{V^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-S+V+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S+U+V})r^{-2}[D,V]\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}}^{V^{2}}.\end{split}

To pass to the last line we used
2.54
and absorbed the

r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{0}
term into the
\mathcal{O}
term. Use

A.33
to establish

(A.34)

[V,\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}]V=_{\mathrm{s}}V\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S}V+\text{good}_{\alpha}^{V^{2}}=_{\mathrm{s}}V[V,\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S}]+\text{good}_{\alpha}^{V^{2}}

Sum
A.33
and
A.34
to conclude
2.60
for multi-indices

\leq\alpha{}
.
∎

Proof of
2.61
.

Write
L=L_{1}UL_{2}
for
L_{i}\in\Gamma^{\alpha_{i}}
and
\alpha{}=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+U
, where

\alpha_{i}\geq 0
. Set
L^{\prime}=L_{1}L_{2}
, and use
A.26
to compute
L-UL^{\prime}=-[U,L_{1}]L_{2}
, noting that
L_{1}\in\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}
:

(A.35)

\begin{split}L-UL^{\prime}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-U-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S})[U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}-U-V}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-S}]\\
&=\mathcal{O}(\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}-U-S+V},r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S})[U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}-U-V}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha{}-S}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U-S}]\end{split}

∎

Proof of
2.62
.

If
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\leq 1
, then this is immediate because
L=L^{\prime}
. The operators
L

and
L^{\prime}
are made up of the same vector fields, but in possibly different
orders. We can reorder
L
into
L^{\prime}
by performing finitely many swaps of
two adjacent vector fields. It is therefore enough to consider
L=L_{1}\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}L_{2}
and
L^{\prime}=L_{1}\Gamma_{2}\Gamma_{1}L_{2}
, where
L_{i}\in\Gamma^{\alpha_{i}}
and
\Gamma_{i}\in\Gamma^{\beta_{i}}
for
\lvert\beta_{i}\rvert=1
and
\alpha_{i}\geq 0
and
\alpha{}=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}+\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}
. Inspecting
section

2.8.2
shows that

(A.36)

[\Gamma{}_{1},\Gamma{}_{2}]=_{\mathrm{s}}[1+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\beta{}_{1}+\beta{}_{2}-U-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{\beta{}_{1}+\beta{}_{2}-S}+\mathfrak{B}_{\beta{}_{1}+\beta{}_{2}-S}]D=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{\leq\beta{}_{1}+\beta{}_{2}-S}D,

and so

(A.37)

\begin{split}L-L^{\prime}&=L_{1}[\Gamma{}_{1},\Gamma{}_{2}]L_{2}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{\leq\alpha{}_{1}+\beta{}_{1}+\beta{}_{2}-S}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}_{1}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}_{2}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{\leq\alpha{}-S}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}_{1}+\alpha{}_{2}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{\leq\alpha{}-S}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-\beta{}_{1}-\beta{}_{2}}.\end{split}

In the final equality we used the consequence
[D,\Gamma^{\beta}{}]=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{<\beta{}}D\Gamma^{\leq\beta{}-1}
of
2.60
. If
\beta_{1}=\beta_{2}
, then
\Gamma_{1}=\Gamma_{2}
, so
L-L^{\prime}=0
. We can therefore
suppose
\beta_{1}\neq{}\beta_{2}
, in which case
\beta_{1}+\beta_{2}\geq U+V
. This concludes the
proof.
∎

Proof of
2.64
.

We first prove
2.64
. Use
2.15
to compute

(A.38)

\partial_{v}U=\partial_{v}(-\nu{})^{-1}\partial{}_{u}+(-\nu{})^{-1}\partial{}_{u}\partial{}_{v}=\kappa{}\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\kappa{}U-r^{-1}(\lambda{}U-\partial{}_{v})

and use
2.48
to conclude.

Now we prove
2.63
. We first prove the formula with
VU
on the left
side. By
2.17
and
U=-\underline{\partial{}}_{r}
, we have

(A.39)

\underline{\partial{}}_{v}U=\kappa{}\Box{}+\kappa{}(1-\mu{})U^{2}-r^{-1}\kappa{}(2-2r^{-1}\varpi{})U+r^{-1}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\Box{}+r^{-1}D+U^{2}].

By
2.64
, we have

(A.40)

\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{0}}\overline{\partial}_{r}U=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\Box{}+r^{-1}D].

The claim with
UV
on the left now follows from

A.39
and
A.40
and the expression
V=\chi{}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}+(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}
. To complete
the proof, note that
[U,V]=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}r^{-1}D
by
A.14
.
∎

Proof of
2.68
.

A direct computation shows that

(A.41)

\begin{split}[\Box{},U]+\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}U^{2}&=\mathchoice{\frac{U\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{U\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{U\kappa{}/\kappa{}}{U\kappa{}/\kappa{}}\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{2/r^{2}}{2/r^{2}}\Bigl[-1+\mathchoice{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{2\varpi{}/r}{2\varpi{}/r}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\mathchoice{\frac{rU\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{rU\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{rU\kappa{}/\kappa{}}{rU\kappa{}/\kappa{}}\bigl(1-\mathchoice{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}\bigr)\Bigr]U+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}\underline{\partial}_{v}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{U}[\Box{}+r^{-2}V+r^{-2}U],\end{split}

which is
2.65
.

A similar computation shows that

(A.42)

\begin{split}\chi{}[\Box{},\underline{\partial{}}_{v}]&=\mathchoice{\frac{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{\chi{}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\lambda{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{\chi{}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\lambda{}}{\kappa{}}}{\chi{}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\lambda{}/\kappa{}}{\chi{}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\lambda{}/\kappa{}}U^{2}-\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r}}{\frac{2}{r}}{2/r}{2/r}\Bigl[-r^{-1}\chi{}\underline{\partial{}}_{v}\varpi{}+\mathchoice{\frac{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}{\chi{}\underline{\partial}_{v}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}(1-\varpi{}/r)\Bigr]U\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{V}[\Box{}+r^{-2}V+r^{-2}U+U^{2}]\end{split}

where we used the fact that
\mathfrak{b}_{V}
can depend on
R_{\bullet}
. Similarly,
one computes

(A.43)

\begin{split}(1-\chi{})[\Box{},\overline{\partial}_{r}]-(1-\chi{})\mathchoice{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)}{r^{2}}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}{2(\varpi{}-\mathbf{e}^{2}/r)/r^{2}}\overline{\partial}_{r}^{2}&=\mathchoice{\frac{(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}{(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{2/r^{2}}{2/r^{2}}\Bigl[-1+\mathchoice{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{\frac{2\varpi{}}{r}}{2\varpi{}/r}{2\varpi{}/r}+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{2}}{\frac{1}{2}}{1/2}{1/2}\mathchoice{\frac{r\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{r\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{r\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}{r\overline{\partial}_{r}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}\bigl(1-\mathchoice{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\frac{\mathbf{e}^{2}}{r^{2}}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}{\mathbf{e}^{2}/r^{2}}\bigr)\Bigr](1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{r}\\
&\qquad-\mathchoice{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{\frac{1}{r^{2}}}{1/r^{2}}{1/r^{2}}\mathchoice{\frac{1}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{1}{(-\gamma{})}}{1/(-\gamma{})}{1/(-\gamma{})}(1-\chi{})\overline{\partial}_{u}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{V}[\Box{}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{V}V+r^{-2}U].\end{split}

A direct computation shows that

(A.44)

G\overline{\partial}_{r}^{2}=V^{2}+\mathcal{E}

for
G=(1+2\chi{}(\lambda{}-1)+\chi^{2}(\lambda{}-1)^{2})
and
\mathcal{E}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{V}[D+U^{2}]
. Since
r(\lambda{}-1)=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}
, we find that
G
does not vanish and
(1-\chi{})G^{-1}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}
when
R_{\bullet}
is large enough
depending on
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}
. We next compute

(A.45)

[\Box{},V]=\chi{}[\Box{},\underline{\partial{}}_{v}]+(1-\chi{})[\Box{},\overline{\partial}_{r}]+[\Box{},\chi{}](\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-\overline{\partial}_{r})

and

(A.46)

[\Box{},\chi{}]=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}[1+D]

for
R_{\bullet}
large enough depending on
\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}
(due to the
occurrence of
F^{-1}
for
F=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}(1+\chi{}(1-\lambda{})^{-1})
). Since
\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}(\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-\overline{\partial}_{r})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}D
, we can use

2.63
to compute

(A.47)

[\Box{},\chi{}](\underline{\partial{}}_{v}-\overline{\partial}_{r})=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}[V^{2}+D+U^{2}]

Combine

A.42
,
A.43
,
A.44
,
A.45
,
A.46
and
A.47

to obtain
2.66
.

Write
\underline{S}\coloneqq{}v\underline{\partial{}}_{v}
and
\overline{S}\coloneqq{}r\overline{\partial}_{r}+u\overline{\partial}_{u}
. One computes explicitly

(A.48)

[\Box{},\underline{S}]=\Bigl(1+\mathchoice{\frac{\underline{S}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{\underline{S}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\underline{S}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}{\underline{S}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}\Bigr)\Box{}-\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{2/r^{2}}{2/r^{2}}\Bigl[-\underline{S}\varpi{}+\Bigl(1+\mathchoice{\frac{\underline{S}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{\underline{S}\kappa{}}{\kappa{}}}{\underline{S}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}{\underline{S}\kappa{}/\kappa{}}\Bigr)(r-\varpi{})\Bigr]U+\mathchoice{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{\frac{1}{\kappa{}}}{1/\kappa{}}{1/\kappa{}}(\underline{S}\lambda{}+\lambda{})U^{2}.

and

(A.49)

[\Box{},\overline{S}]=\Bigl(2+\mathchoice{\frac{\overline{S}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\overline{S}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\overline{S}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}{\overline{S}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}\Bigr)\Box{}+\mathchoice{\frac{\overline{S}(-\nu{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{\overline{S}(-\nu{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\overline{S}(-\nu{})/(-\gamma{})}{\overline{S}(-\nu{})/(-\gamma{})}\overline{\partial}_{r}^{2}+\mathchoice{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{\frac{2}{r^{2}}}{2/r^{2}}{2/r^{2}}\Bigl[\varpi{}-\overline{S}\varpi{}+\mathchoice{\frac{r\overline{S}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{\frac{r\overline{S}(-\gamma{})}{(-\gamma{})}}{r\overline{S}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}{r\overline{S}(-\gamma{})/(-\gamma{})}(1-r^{-1}\varpi{})\Bigr]\overline{\partial}_{r}.

Since

(A.50)

[\Box{},S]=\chi{}[\Box{},\underline{S}]+(1-\chi{})[\Box{},\overline{S}]+[\Box{},\chi{}](\underline{S}-\overline{S}),

and

(A.51)

\underline{S}-\overline{S}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathbf{1}_{R_{\bullet}\leq r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}\mathfrak{b}_{S}DD^{\leq 1},

we conclude
2.67
using similar arguments to those used in the computation of

[\Box{},V]
.
∎

Proof of
2.68
.

Schematically write “
\text{good}_{\alpha}{}
” for terms appearing on the right side of

2.69
when
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
. With this
notation,
2.69
reads

(A.52)

[\Box{},L]+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}UL+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VL=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha}{}.

Let
\alpha{}
and
\alpha{}^{\prime}
be multi-indices with
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert,\lvert\alpha{}^{\prime}\rvert\geq 1
. Suppose that

2.69
holds for all operators in
\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}
and in
\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}}
. We will show that if
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
and
L^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\alpha{}^{\prime}}
, then

2.69
holds for
LL^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}
.

Step 0: The base cases
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\leq 1
.
If
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=0
, then
L=1
, so both sides
of
2.69
vanish. If
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert=1
, then
L\in\{U,V,S\}
, and so
2.69
follows from inspection
of
2.68
. From now on we can suppose that
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert,\lvert\alpha{}^{\prime}\rvert\geq 1
.

Step 1: Differentiating the right side of
2.69
.
We
will use
2.60
to show that

(A.53)

\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.

To start, we have

(A.54)

\begin{split}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}^{\prime}}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}[\underbrace{\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}\Box{}}_{\text{(I)}}+\underbrace{r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}V^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}}_{\text{(II)}}\\
&\qquad+\underbrace{r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}}_{\text{(III)}}+\underbrace{r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}^{\prime}}}_{\text{(IV)}}].\end{split}

We handle the terms in the square brackets one by one. Clearly

(A.55)

\text{(I)}=_{\mathrm{s}}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}\Box{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.

We now develop term
(II)
using
2.60
:

(A.56)

\begin{split}\text{(II)}&=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-1}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}[V^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}+\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}+V}r^{-2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-U-S+V}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}r^{-2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\\
\end{split}

To pass to the second line we used the fact that term
(II)
vanishes
unless
\alpha{}^{\prime}_{S}>0
, so

(A.57)

\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}+V}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S+V}=\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.

From
2.60
, we handle term
(III)
:

(A.58)

\begin{split}\text{(III)}&=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}[V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}+\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}r^{-2}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-U-1}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-U-1}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}},\\
\end{split}

where in passing to the second line we recalled that
\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}
(see
2.39
and
2.43
). We handle term

(IV)
similarly using
2.60
:

(A.59)

\begin{split}\text{(IV)}&=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}[U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}+\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}[U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-V}+r^{-1}\mathfrak{G}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-S}]]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}[U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}+V\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}]=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

Substitute
A.55
,
A.56
,
A.58
and
A.59
into
A.54
to
obtain
A.53
.

Step 2: Differentiating
2.69
.
In
this step we show that

(A.60)

L[\Box{},L^{\prime}]+\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.

Let
X\in\{U,V\}
. Since
f_{U},f_{V}\in r^{-2}\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ}\mathfrak{g}_{0}
, we have
\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}}f_{X}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{B}_{\alpha{}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}}=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}
. Use
this to compute

(A.61)

\begin{split}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}},f_{X}X]&=_{\mathrm{s}}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}},f_{X}]X+f_{X}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}},X]=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha{}_{1}+\alpha{}_{2}\leq\alpha{}\\
\lvert\alpha{}_{1}\rvert\geq 1\end{subarray}}(\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}_{1}}f_{X})\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}_{2}}X+f_{X}[\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}},X]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha{}_{1}+\alpha{}_{2}\leq\alpha{}\end{subarray}}(\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}_{1}}f_{X})[X,\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}_{2}}]+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\alpha{}_{1}+\alpha{}_{2}\leq\alpha{}\\
\lvert\alpha{}_{1}\rvert\geq 1\end{subarray}}(\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}_{1}}f_{X})X\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}_{2}}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}(\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}f_{X})[X\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}+[X,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]]=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}[X\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}+[X,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]].\end{split}

Using
A.61
and
2.60
and
\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}

(since
\lvert\alpha{}^{\prime}\rvert>1
implies
\alpha{}<\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}
), we compute

(A.62)

\begin{split}&L(\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}UL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VL^{\prime})-\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}-\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}[L,f_{U}U]L^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}[L,f_{V}V]L^{\prime}=_{s}[L,f_{D}D]L^{\prime}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{G}_{\alpha}{}[X\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}+[X,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]L^{\prime}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}[X\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-1}+[X,\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}]L^{\prime}]\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}+r^{-2}\mathfrak{g}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}_{<\alpha{}}D\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}-U}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\\
\end{split}

To complete the proof of
A.60
, combine

2.69
for the multi-index
\alpha{}
with

A.62
, and then apply
A.53
, recalling that
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
:

(A.63)

\begin{split}L[\Box{},L^{\prime}]+\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}&=L([\Box{},L^{\prime}]+\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}UL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VL^{\prime})\\
&\qquad-\bigl(L(\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}UL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VL^{\prime})-\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}-\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}\bigr)\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}L(\text{good}_{\alpha{}^{\prime}})+\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

Step 3: Acting on
2.69
from the right.
We now show
that

(A.64)

[\Box{},L]L^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.

Act on both sides of
2.69
for
L\in\Gamma{}^{\alpha}{}
with
L^{\prime}\in\Gamma{}^{\alpha{}^{\prime}}
on the right to get

(A.65)

\begin{split}[\Box{},L]L^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}&=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}\Box{}L^{\prime}+\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}[\Box{},L^{\prime}]+\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

By
A.60
and
2.60
and the inductive hypothesis, we have
\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-1}[\Box{},L^{\prime}]=_{s}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}
, and substituting this into
A.65

proves
A.64
.

Step 4: Completing the induction.
Add
A.60
and
A.64
to get

(A.66)

\begin{split}[\Box{},LL^{\prime}]+(\alpha{}_{U}+\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime})f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+(\alpha{}_{V}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime})f_{V}VLL^{\prime}&=L[\Box{},L^{\prime}]+\alpha{}_{U}^{\prime}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}^{\prime}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}\\
&\qquad+[\Box{},L]L^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}ULL^{\prime}+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VLL^{\prime}\\
&=_{\mathrm{s}}\text{good}_{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}.\end{split}

We have now shown that
2.69
for
L\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
and
L^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\alpha}{}
implies

2.69
for
LL^{\prime}\in\Gamma^{\alpha{}+\alpha{}^{\prime}}
.
Since we established
2.69
for
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\leq 1

in Step 0, an induction argument concludes
2.69
for
all
\alpha{}
.
∎

Proof of
2.72
.

We can assume
\lvert\alpha{}\rvert\geq 1
, since the case
\alpha{}=0
is trivial.

Step 1: Proof of
2.70
.
Observe that
2.70
is a
consequence of
sections

2.8.3
and
2.8.1
and the following
two identities:

(A.67)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\partial{}_{v}+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}U],

(A.68)

\displaystyle rV^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}

\displaystyle=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}[\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}\partial{}_{v}(r\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{})+\partial{}_{v}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}+\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}U\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}].

First, recall that
A.67
was proved in

section

2.8.1
. To prove
A.68
, it
is enough to consider the quantity
\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}rV^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-S}
, since
\mathbf{1}_{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}}rV^{2}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}V\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-S+V}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{B}_{0}V\Gamma^{<\alpha{}}
is handled by

A.67
. Use

A.67
to rewrite the first
V
in terms of

\partial_{v}
and
U
, then use
\partial{}_{v}r=\lambda{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}
to commute
r
past
\partial_{v}
, and note that the term
beginning with
U
is supported in
\{r\leq 2R_{\bullet}\}
.

Step 2: Proof of
2.71
.
By the good sign
f_{U}\geq 0
and the
triangle inequality, we have

(A.69)

\begin{split}r^{2-s}UL\varphi{}\Box{}L\varphi{}&=-\alpha_{U}r^{2-s}f_{U}(UL\varphi{})^{2}-\alpha_{V}r^{2-s}f_{V}UL\varphi{}VL\varphi{}\\
&\qquad+r^{2-s}(UL\varphi{})(\Box{}L\varphi{}+\alpha{}_{u}f_{U}UL\varphi{}+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VL\varphi{})\\
&\leq\underbrace{\alpha{}_{V}\lvert r^{2-s}f_{V}\rvert\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert\lvert VL\varphi{}\rvert}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}}+\underbrace{r^{2-s}\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}UL\varphi{}+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VL\varphi{}\rvert}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(II)}}.\end{split}

Term
(I)
vanishes unless
\alpha_{V}>0
, namely unless
L
contains a

V
. In this case, use
2.62
and the wave equation

2.63
to rewrite

(A.70)

\mathbf{1}_{\alpha{}_{V}>0}UL\varphi{}=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{\alpha}{}[\Box{}\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}\varphi{}+D\Gamma{}^{<\alpha{}}].

Control term
(I)
using

sections

2.8.3
,
A.67
and
A.70

and
\lvert r^{2-s}f_{V}\rvert\leq C(\mathcal{G}_{\alpha,s})
. For term

(II)
, use

sections

2.8.3
,
2.70
and
A.70
.

Step 3: Proof of
2.72
.
By the triangle inequality, we have

(A.71)

\begin{split}r^{2-s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}\rvert&\leq\underbrace{\alpha{}_{U}\lvert r^{2-s}f_{U}\rvert\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(I)}}+\underbrace{r^{2-s}\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert\Box{}L\varphi{}+\alpha{}_{U}f_{U}UL\varphi{}+\alpha{}_{V}f_{V}VL\varphi{}\rvert}_{\coloneqq{}\text{(II)}}\\
&\qquad+\underbrace{\alpha{}_{V}\lvert r^{2-s}f_{V}\rvert\lvert\partial{}_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert\lvert VL\varphi{}\rvert}_{\coloneqq{}{\text{(III)}}}.\\
\end{split}

Handle terms
(I)
and
(II)
as in Step 2. In particular, for
term
(I)
we bring the
U
that
L
contains to the front using

2.62
, and then use the wave equation
\partial_{v}U=_{\mathrm{s}}\mathfrak{b}_{0}[\Box{}+r^{-1}D]
from
2.64
. It is important here that

\partial_{v}U
produces a wave equation with no
U^{2}
-term; such a term would
produce
U^{2}\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}-U}=_{\mathrm{s}}U\Gamma{}^{\leq\alpha{}}
, and this
top-order term is not admissible. The analogous term in Step 2 was

U^{2}\Gamma^{\leq\alpha{}-V}=_{\mathrm{s}}U\Gamma^{<\alpha{}}
, which is a lower order
term. For term
(III)
, use
A.67
and

\lvert r^{2-s}f_{V}\rvert\leq C(\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0})
to get

(A.72)

\text{(III)}\leq\mathbf{1}_{r\geq R_{\bullet}}C((\mathfrak{B}_{0}^{\circ},\mathfrak{g}_{0},\alpha{})\lvert\partial_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert(\lvert UL\varphi{}\rvert+\lvert\partial_{v}L\varphi{}\rvert).

∎
